Extensions of locally analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations for GL n \mathrm{GL}_{n}
Yiqin He

Abstract
Let L L be a finite extension of 𝐐 p {\mathbf{Q}}_{p} . In this paper, we study the locally 𝐐 p {\mathbf{Q}}_{p} -analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations of GL n ​ ( L ) {\rm GL}_{n}(L) which are associated to a Zelevinsky-segment. We then compute the Ext {\rm Ext} -groups of them. They are related to the (parabolic) Breuil’s simple ℒ \mathcal{L} -invariants, which arise in the automorphic side of a conjectured p p -adic local Langlands correspondence.



1
Introduction

Let
L
(resp.
E
) be a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Suppose that
E
is sufficiently large containing all the embeddings
\Sigma_{L}:=\{\sigma:L\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\}
of
L
in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
.

In this paper, we study some locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized
parabolic
Steinberg representations of
{\rm GL}_{n}(L)
(constructed by locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction), which are given by a Zelevinsky-segment. The heart of this paper is a computation of the
{\rm Ext}^{1}
-groups of them. They arise from the automorphic side of the
p
-adic local Langlands program, the so-called parabolic Breuil’s simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants in
[
24
]
. These results are previously only known when they are induced from a
Borel subgroup
.

In Borel case, Yiwen Ding
[
15
, Section 2]
computes the extension groups of certain locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized (Borel) Steinberg representations of
{\rm GL}_{n}(L)
over
E
. In general, let
{\mathbf{G}}
be a connected reductive algebraic group over
L
. In
[
28
]
, Sascha Orlik computes the extensions of smooth generalized (Borel) Steinberg representations of
{\mathbf{G}}(L)
, in the category of smooth
G
-representations with coefficients in a certain self-injective ring
R
. Moreover, if
{\mathbf{G}}
is split, then Lennart Gehramnn
[
21
]
also computes the extensions of locally analytic generalized (Borel) Steinberg representations of
{\mathbf{G}}(L)
, which gives a slight generalization of the above result of Ding.

Our paper will provides a “parabolic” version of Ding’s results. We sketch the main results of this paper as follows.

Let
\Delta_{n}
be the set of simple roots of
{\rm GL}_{n}
(with respect to the Borel subgroup
{\mathbf{B}}
of upper triangular matrices), and we identify the set
\Delta_{n}
with
\{1,2,\cdots,n-1\}
. Let
{\mathbf{T}}
be the torus of diagonal matrices. Fix two integers
k
and
r
such that
n=kr
. We put
\Delta_{n}(k):=\{r,2r,\cdots,(k-1)r\}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
and
\Delta_{n}^{k}:=\Delta_{n}\backslash\Delta_{n}(k)
. For a subset
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)
, we denote by
{\mathbf{P}}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
the parabolic subgroup of
{\rm GL}_{n}
containing the Borel subgroup
{\mathbf{B}}
such that
\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I
are precisely the simple roots of the unipotent radical of
\mathbf{P}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
. Let
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
be the Levi subgroup of
{\mathbf{P}}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
containing the group
{\mathbf{T}}
such that
I\cup\Delta_{n}^{k}
is equal to the set of simple roots of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
. Let
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
be the parabolic subgroup opposite to
{\mathbf{P}}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
. In particular, we have

{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}:={\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\emptyset}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\rm GL}_{r}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&{\rm GL}_{r}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&0\\
0&0&0&{\rm GL}_{r}\\
\end{array}\right)\subset{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}:={\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\emptyset}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\rm GL}_{r}&\ast&\cdots&\ast\\
0&{\rm GL}_{r}&\cdots&\ast\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\ast\\
0&0&0&{\rm GL}_{r}\\
\end{array}\right).

For simplicity, if
I=\{ir\}
for some
1\leq i\leq k-1
, we put
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}:={\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\{ir\}}
and
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}:={\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\{ir\}}
.

Let
\pi
be a fixed irreducible cuspidal representation of
{\rm GL}_{r}(L)
over
E
. Following
[
1
]
, we consider the Zelevinsky-segment (see also Section
6.1
, Definition
6.1
and Definition
6.2
)

\Delta_{[k-1,0]}(\pi):=\pi|\det|_{L}^{r-1}\otimes_{E}\cdots\otimes_{E}\pi|\det|_{L}\otimes_{E}\pi,

(1.1)

which forms an irreducible cuspidal smooth representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
over
E
.

In the sequel, we put
G:={\rm GL}_{n}(L)
. Let
\underline{\lambda}
be a dominant weight of
(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{\rm GL}_{n})\times_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E
with respect to
(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{B}})\times_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E
. Let
L(\underline{\lambda})
be the unique irreducible
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation of
G
with the highest weight
\underline{\lambda}
. Consider the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation

i^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{1/2}\Delta_{[r-1,0]}(\pi)\Big)^{\infty}\otimes L(\underline{\lambda}).

(1.2)

(by tensoring a normalized smooth parabolic induction with a
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation of
G
), where
\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}
is the modulus character of
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
.

In this paper, we first define (and study) the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic generalized (parabolic) Steinberg representations
\{v^{\infty}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}
associated to the Zelevinsky-segment
\Delta_{[k-1,0]}(\pi)
and weight
\underline{\lambda}
, and show that they are precisely the Jordan-Hölder factors of
i^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
(
1.2
). In particular,
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=v^{\infty}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\emptyset}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
(resp.,
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi):={\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{0})
) is the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic (resp., smooth) parabolic Steinberg representation. We add the terminology ”parabolic” to distinguish from the usual (Borel) Steinberg representation induced from the Borel subgroup
{\mathbf{B}}(L)
. Recall that the Jordan-Hölder factors of
i^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi,\underline{0})
are classified by the orientations of some graph in

[
1
, Section 2.2, Theorem]
, we also compare our approach (or description) to that of the earlier work.

We then define the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic analogy
\{v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}
(the so-called locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations associated to the Zelevinsky-segment
\Delta_{[k-1,0]}(\pi)
and weight
\underline{\lambda}
) of
\{v^{\infty}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}
. Denote by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic Steinberg representation. The Orlik-Strauch constructions (see
[
30
, Theorem]
) on locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations give a way to determine the composition factors of
v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. By generalizing the results in
[
29
]
, we establish the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) Tits complexes (see Section
4.3
), and compute the multiplicities of certain composition factors of
v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by following the route of
[
29
]
. We refer to Theorem
4.6
for more detailed and more precise statements.

The main result of this paper is to compute the
{\rm Ext}
-groups of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations. We prove

Theorem 1.1
.

(Theorem
5.19
)
For
ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, there exists an isomorphism of
E
-vector spaces

{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(1.3)

In particular, we have
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=d_{L}+1
.

See Section
2.3
for a precise definition of the
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}
-group on locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations. Moreover, for
\psi\in{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
, we will give an explicit construction of the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation

\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
, which is an extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
attached to
\psi
via (
1.3
).

For
ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, we next find a subrepresentation
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
of
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
in Section
5.5
, such that we have a natural isomorphism (see Proposition
5.34
) of
E
-vector spaces

{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(1.4)

The subrepresentation
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
has a simpler and more clear structure than
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.
We show that the socle of
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
(the author does not know if the socle of
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, which is actually true for
(r,k)=(2,2)
). The proof needs the very strongly admissibility of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic parabolic generalized Steinberg representation, which cannot be deduced directly from the strategy of the proof of
[
15
, Proposition 2.23]
. We prove it by using global methods (more precisely, the Emerton’s completed cohomology for the Shimura variety associated to certain division algebra). For any
\psi\in{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
, we denote by
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
the extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
attached to
\psi
via (
1.4
), which is a subrepresentation of
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
. Moreover, we can show that
\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
actually comes from
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
by pushing forward along the injection
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. In Remark
1.3
, We describe how this theorem is related with the
parabolic simple

\mathcal{L}
-invariants
in
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence.

Sketch of the proof of Theorem
1.1

The proof follows along the route of
[
15
, Section 2.2]
. To complete the computations, we need some technical calculations. We briefly explain the basic strategy and point out the difficulties.

For any subset
I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, it follows from
[
1
, Proposition 2.10]
that the smooth parabolic induction

\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{1/2}\Delta_{[r-1,0]}(\pi)\Big)^{\infty}

admits a unique irreducible subrepresentation
\pi_{I}
, which is a smooth representation of the Levi subgroup
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
over
E
. In particular, when
r=1
(i.e., the Borel case), we see that
\pi_{I}
is just the trivial representation of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}^{\langle 1\rangle}(L)
over
E
.

As a preliminary, we compute smooth extension groups between

\{v^{\infty}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}(\pi)\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}
. See Appendix
6.4
for a collection of such results. The locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) Tits complexes (see Section
4.3
) show that locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic Steinberg representation is derived equivalent to a complex of (direct sum of) parabolically induced locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) principal series. We reduce our problem (i.e., the computation of the extension group (
1.3
)) to the computation of some extension groups (see Lemma
5.9
). Then by using some spectral sequences, Bruhat filtration (see Section
5.3
), and dévissage arguments, the computations of extension groups (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) are reduced to calculations of

{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
and
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
, where
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
consists of extensions on which the center
Z_{n}
of
G
acts via certain character
\eta
.

In the Borel case, we have explicit expressions

{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}^{\langle 1\rangle}(L)}(1,1)\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}^{\langle 1\rangle}(L),E)(\text{resp.\;}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}^{\langle 1\rangle}(L),Z_{n}=1}(1,1)\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}^{\langle 1\rangle}(L)/Z_{n},E)).

The reason that
r>1
causes technical issues is that we cannot compute the (or determine the dimensions of) extension groups
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
and
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z=\eta}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
explicitly, since the cuspidal smooth representation
\pi
appears in a (parabolic) Zelevinsky-segment. Thus we cannot evaluate the right dimensions of
{\rm Ext}^{1}
-groups directly as in the discussion of
[
15
, Corollary 2.16,Theorem 2.19]
. But in Borel case,
\pi
is the trivial representation, all these become trivial. We still are able to overcome this obstacle.

Firstly, the theory of types of Bushnell and Kutzko (more precisely, the semisimple Bushnell-Kutzko types for
{\rm GL}_{n}
, which classify the cuspidal smooth representations of
{\rm GL}_{n}
over
\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
or complex field
{\mathbb{C}}
) will give a way to compute smooth extension groups
{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
,
{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z=\eta}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
explicitly (see Appendix, Lemma
6.6
). Secondly, we point out that in general the locally analytic extension groups
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
and
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z=\eta}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
are much harder to calculate than the smooth case. But we still have canonical morphisms

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}^{\langle 1\rangle}(L)}(1,1)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I}),{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}^{\langle 1\rangle}(L),Z_{n}=1}(1,1)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z=\eta}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I}).\;

The author does not know whether they are isomorphisms in general. In this paper, we compute explicitly the extension groups
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
and
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})
for
i\in\{0,1\}
and
I={\Delta_{n}(k)},\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash\{jr\}
(see Lemma
6.6
and Lemma
5.7
). In these special cases, some spectral sequences degenerate (see Lemme
2.3
and the proof of Lemma
5.7
, these spectral sequences link locally analytic extension groups to smooth extension groups. ), so that the above morphisms are isomorphisms.

Based on these results, we define and show the injectivity of the following morphism

\varrho_{\{ir\},\emptyset}^{\sharp}:{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)/{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}(L),E\big)\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).\;

(1.5)

This is the heart of Section
5.4
. We next show that
\varrho_{\{ir\},\emptyset}^{\sharp}
is actually an isomorphism
by comparing dimensions (this strategy is similar to the discussion of
[
15
, Corollary 2.16,Theorem 2.19]
).  For general
J\subset I
with
|I|=|J|+1
, the author does not know whether the above discussion is true for
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)
. Along the construction of the map
\varrho_{\{ir\},\emptyset}^{\sharp}
, we will obtain an explicit description of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
(i.e., the extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
attached to
\psi
via (
1.3
)).

Remark 1.2
.

Applying our results to
r=1
and
\pi=|\det|_{L}^{\frac{1-n}{2}}
, our locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic) generalized parabolic Steinberg representations
\{v^{\infty}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}(\pi)\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}
(resp.,
\{v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}(\pi)\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}
) become the “classcial” locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic generalized (Borel) Steinberg representations (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized (Borel) Steinberg representations). In this case, our results are collapsed to Ding’s results
[
15
, Section 2]
. For general reductive group, it may happen that no Borel subgroup exists (
{\mathbf{G}}
may be not quasi-spilt). Motivated from the results of Lennart Gehramnn
[
21
]
, we expect our (parabolic version) results can be generalized to connected reductive group
{\mathbf{G}}(L)
(no longer split or quasi-split), this is the content of our future work.

Remark 1.3
.

(Parabolic simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants)

It would be more natural to put our locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations
in the framework of
p
-adic Langlands program, which appear naturally in the automorphic side of
p
-adic Langlands program. In
[
24
]
, the results in this paper are called
(parabolic) Breuil’s simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants
. In the work of author
[
24
]
, for certain potentially semistable Galois representation
\rho_{L}
of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
which admits a non-critical special parabolization, we will define
parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants

\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})
of
\rho_{L}
by using cohomology of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over Robba ring, which encode some Galois information of an admissible Hodge filtration on some linear algebra data via Fontaine’s theory. Then the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},V)
(see the argument after (
1.4
)) will carry the Galois information
\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})
. Moreover, we use Bernstein eigenvarieties (developed by Breuil-Ding
[
13
]
) to establish some local-global compatibility results,i.e., the correspondence between these two parabolic simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants can be realized in the
p
-adic completed cohomology of some Shimura varieties (especially, in the space of
p
-adic automorphic forms on certain definite unitary group). The above parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants are extension parameters at simple roots. The next goal is to explore generalizations of Breuil’s
\mathcal{L}
-invariants that conjecturally correspond to higher Fontaine-Mazur
\mathcal{L}
-invariants (or parabolic Fontaine-Mazur
\mathcal{L}
-invariants) at non-simple roots. See
[
8
]
(the locally analytic
{\rm Ext}^{1}
-conjecture) for general picture. See
[
40
]
,
[
9
]
and
[
31
]
) for partial development.

Remark 1.4
.

A modification of the recent work of Zicheng Qian
[
32
]
may give a way to compute the higher
{\rm Ext}
-groups
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)
for general
J\subseteq I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. In this paper, the author prove certain decomposition for the space of locally analytic distributions to compute the
N
-homology and gives a locally analytic version of Bernstein-Zelevinsky geometric lemma. Inspired by these technical ingredients, we expect that this
{\rm Ext}
-group only depends on
|J|-|I|
and plays a crucial role in finding the counterpart of the parabolic Higher Fontaine-Mazur
\mathcal{L}
-invariants in the locally analytic representations of
G
. Since we only concern parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants, Theorem
1.1
(or Theorem
5.19
) is enough for our application.

2
Preliminaries

2.1
General notation

Let
L
(resp.
E
) be a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with
\mathcal{O}_{L}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{E}
) as its ring of integers and
\varpi_{L}
(resp.
\varpi_{E}
) a uniformizer. Suppose
E
is sufficiently large, containing all the embeddings of
L
in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
. Put
\Sigma_{L}:=\{\sigma:L\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\}=\{\sigma:L\hookrightarrow E\}
, the set of all the embeddings of
L
in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
(equivalently, in
E
). Let
k_{E}
be the residue field of
E
. Let
\mathrm{v}al_{L}(\cdot)
(resp.
\mathrm{v}al_{p}
) be the
p
-adic valuation on
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
normalized by sending uniformizers of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
(resp. of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
) to
1
. Let
d_{L}:=[L:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]=|\Sigma_{L}|
, let
e_{L}:=\mathrm{v}al_{L}(\varpi_{L})
and let
f_{L}:=d_{L}/e_{L}
. We have
q_{L}:=p^{f_{L}}=|\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi_{L}|
.

General setting on Reductive groups and Lie algebras

For a Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{h}}
over
L
, and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, let
{\mathfrak{h}}_{\sigma}:={\mathfrak{h}}\otimes_{L,\sigma}E
(which is a Lie algebra over
E
). For
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, let
{\mathfrak{h}}_{J}:=\prod_{\sigma\in J}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}
. In particular, we have
{\mathfrak{h}}_{\Sigma_{L}}\cong{\mathfrak{h}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E
. The notation
U({\mathfrak{g}})
refers to the universal enveloping algebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}
over
L
.
Let
\mathbf{H}
be an algebraic group over
L
, and
{\mathfrak{h}}
be its Lie algebra over
L
. Let
\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\mathbf{H}
be the scalar restriction of
\mathbf{H}
from
L
to
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. The Lie algebra of
\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\mathbf{H}
over
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
can also be identified with
{\mathfrak{h}}
, where
{\mathfrak{h}}
is regarded as a Lie algebra over
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. We write
\mathbf{H}_{/E}=(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\mathbf{H})\times_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E
, which is an algebraic group over
E
. The Lie algebras of
\mathbf{H}_{/E}
over
E
is
{\mathfrak{h}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
. Let
\mathbf{G}
be a split connected reductive group over
L
, and
{\mathfrak{g}}
be its Lie algebra over
L
. We fix a maximal split torus
{\mathbf{T}}
and write
{\mathbf{B}}
for a choice of Borel subgroup containing
{\mathbf{T}}
. We use
{\mathbf{P}}
for the parabolic subgroup of
\mathbf{G}
containing
{\mathbf{B}}
, and let
{\mathbf{L}}_{{\mathbf{P}}}
be the Levi subgroup of
{\mathbf{P}}
containing
{\mathbf{T}}
. Let
{\mathbf{N}}_{{\mathbf{P}}}
be the unipotent radical of
{\mathbf{P}}
. Then
{\mathbf{P}}
admits a Levi decomposition
{\mathbf{P}}={\mathbf{L}}_{{\mathbf{P}}}{\mathbf{N}}_{{\mathbf{P}}}
. The Lie algebras (over
L
) of subgroups
{\mathbf{T}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{P}},{\mathbf{L}}_{{\mathbf{P}}},{\mathbf{N}}_{{\mathbf{P}}}
are denoted by
{\mathfrak{t}},{\mathfrak{b}},{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{l}}_{{\mathbf{P}}},{\mathfrak{n}}_{{\mathbf{P}}}
respectively.

Note that the group
\mathbf{G}_{/E}
is also a split-connected reductive group over
E
with maximal split torus
{\mathbf{T}}_{/E}
, Borel subgroup
{\mathbf{B}}_{/E}
, parabolic subgroup
{\mathbf{P}}_{/E}
, and
{{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathbf{P}}}_{/E}
is also the Levi subgroup of
{{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathbf{P}}}_{/E}
containing
{\mathbf{T}}_{/E}
. The Lie algebras (over
E
) of reductive groups
\mathbf{G}_{/E}
,
{\mathbf{P}}_{/E}
, and
{{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathbf{P}}}_{/E}
are given by
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
,
{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
and
{\mathfrak{l}}_{{\mathbf{P}},\Sigma_{L}}
.

General linear group
{\rm GL}_{n}
-I

Let
{\rm GL}_{n}
be the general linear group over
L
. Let
\Delta_{n}
be the set of simple roots of
{\rm GL}_{n}
(with respect to the Borel subgroup
{\mathbf{B}}
of upper triangular matrices). We identify the set
\Delta_{n}
with
\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
such that
i\in\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
corresponds to the simple root
\alpha_{i}:(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in{\mathfrak{t}}\mapsto x_{i}-x_{i+1}
, where
{\mathfrak{t}}
denotes the
L
-Lie algebra of the torus
{\mathbf{T}}
of diagonal matrices.

For a subset
I\subset\Delta_{n}
, we define an ordered partition
\underline{n}_{I}=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{|\Delta_{n}\backslash I|+1})
of
n
with
\Delta_{n}\backslash I=\{n_{1},n_{1}+n_{2},\cdots,n_{1}+\cdots+n_{|\Delta_{n}\backslash I|+1}\}
. Let
\mathbf{P}_{I}
be the parabolic subgroup of

{\rm GL}_{n}
containing
{\mathbf{B}}
such that
\Delta_{n}\backslash I
are precisely the simple roots of the unipotent radical
\mathbf{N}_{I}
of
\mathbf{P}_{I}
. Denote by
\mathbf{L}_{I}
the unique Levi subgroup of
\mathbf{P}_{I}
containing
{\mathbf{T}}
, then
\mathbf{L}_{I}
is isomorphic to

{\rm GL}_{n_{1}}\times\cdots{\rm GL}_{n_{|\Delta_{n}\backslash I|+1}}
, and
I
is equal to the set of simple roots of
\mathbf{L}_{I}
. Let
\Phi_{I}^{+}\subset\Phi_{I}
be the set of positive roots and roots of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}
with respect to
{\mathbf{T}}\subset{\mathbf{L}}_{I}\cap{\mathbf{B}}
. In particular, we have
\mathbf{P}_{\Delta_{n}}={\rm GL}_{n}
,
\mathbf{P}_{\emptyset}=\mathbf{B}
. Let
\overline{\mathbf{P}}_{I}
be the parabolic subgroup opposite to
\mathbf{P}_{I}
. Let
\mathbf{N}_{I}
(resp.
\overline{\mathbf{N}}_{I}
) be the nilpotent radical of
\mathbf{P}_{I}
(resp.
\overline{\mathbf{P}}_{I}
), let
\mathbf{Z}_{I}
be the center of
\mathbf{L}_{I}
and let
\mathbf{D}_{I}
be the derived subgroup of
\mathbf{L}_{I}
. Then we have a Levi decomposition
\mathbf{P}_{I}=\mathbf{L}_{I}\mathbf{N}_{I}
(resp.
\overline{\mathbf{P}}_{I}=\mathbf{L}_{I}\overline{\mathbf{N}}_{I}
). Let
{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}
be the center of
{\rm GL}_{n}
. Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
,
{\mathfrak{p}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{l}}_{I}
,
\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}}_{I}
,
\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I}
,
\overline{{\mathfrak{n}}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{d}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{z}}_{I}
,
\overline{{\mathfrak{z}}}_{I}
and
{\mathfrak{t}}
be the
L
-Lie algebras of
{\rm GL}_{n}
,
\mathbf{P}_{I}
,
\mathbf{N}_{I}
,
\mathbf{L}_{I}
,
\mathbf{L}_{I}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}
,
\overline{\mathbf{P}}_{I}
,
\overline{\mathbf{N}}_{I}
,
{\mathbf{D}}_{I}
,
{\mathbf{Z}}_{I}
,
{\mathbf{Z}}_{I}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}
and
{\mathbf{T}}
respectively.

Let
\underline{\lambda}:=(\lambda_{1,\sigma},\cdots,\lambda_{n,\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
be a weight of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
. For
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}=\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
, we call that
\underline{\lambda}
is
I
-dominant with respect to
{\mathbf{B}}_{/E}
(resp. with respect to
\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}_{/E}
) if
\lambda_{i,\sigma}\geq\lambda_{i+1,\sigma}

(
resp.
\lambda_{i,\sigma}\leq\lambda_{i+1,\sigma}
)
for all
i\in I
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. We denote by
X_{I}^{+}
(resp.
X_{I}^{-}
) the set of
I
-dominant integral weights of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
with respect to
{\mathbf{B}}_{/E}
(resp. with respect to
\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}_{/E}
). Note that
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{+}
if and only if
-\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{-}
. For
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{+}
, there exists a unique irreducible algebraic representation, denoted by
L(\underline{\lambda})_{I}
, of
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{/E}
with the highest weight
\underline{\lambda}
with respect to
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{/E}\cap{\mathbf{B}}_{/E}
. We put
\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})_{I}:=(L(\underline{\lambda})_{I})^{\vee}
, it is the irreducible algebraic representationof
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{/E}
with highest weight
-\underline{\lambda}
with respect to
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{/E}\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{/E}
. Denote
\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}:=L(\underline{\lambda})_{\emptyset}
. If
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, let
L(\underline{\lambda}):=L(\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta_{n}}
. Note that if
I\subseteq J\subseteq\Delta_{n}
, and
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{J}^{+}
, then we have
L(\underline{\lambda})_{J}^{{\mathbf{N}}_{I}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)}\cong L(\underline{\lambda})_{I}
(see
[
15
, Page 7997]
).

We view the
L
-points of the above groups as locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic groups. Throughout this paper, we put
G:={\rm GL}_{n}(L)
. A
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation of
G
over
E
is the induced action of
G\subset{\rm GL}_{n/E}(E)
on an algebraic representation of
{\rm GL}_{n/E}
. By abuse of notatio, we will use the same notation to denote
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representations induced from an algebraic representation of
{\rm GL}_{n/E}
.

Let
\underline{\lambda}
be an integral weight, denoted by
M(\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{\text{U}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\Sigma_{L}})}\underline{\lambda}
(resp.,
\overline{M}(\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{\text{U}(\overline{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{\Sigma_{L}})}\underline{\lambda}
), the corresponding Verma module with respect to
{\mathfrak{b}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
(resp.,
\overline{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
). Let
L(\underline{\lambda})
(resp.,
\overline{L}(\underline{\lambda})
) be the unique simple quotient of
M(\underline{\lambda})
(resp., of
\overline{M}(\underline{\lambda})
). Actually, when
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
(i.e.,
-\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{-}
),
L(\underline{\lambda})
is finite-dimensional and isomorphic to the algebraic representation
L(\underline{\lambda})
introduced above (hence there is no conflict of notation). We have
\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})\cong L(\underline{\lambda})^{\vee}
. In general, for any subset
I
of
\Delta_{n}
, and
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{+}
, we define the generalized parabolic Verma module
M_{I}(\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{\text{U}({\mathfrak{p}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}})}L(\underline{\lambda})_{I}
(resp.
\overline{M}_{I}(-\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{\text{U}(\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}})}\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})_{I})
with respect to
{\mathfrak{p}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}}
(resp.
\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}}
). Let
\rho
be the half of the sum of positive roots of
{\rm GL}_{n}
. We have a dot action
\cdot
of
\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}
on the weight
\underline{\lambda}
given by
w\cdot\underline{\lambda}=w(\underline{\lambda}+\rho)-\rho
. Denote by
\mathscr{W}_{n}
(
\cong S_{n}
) the Weyl group of
{\rm GL}_{n}
, and denote by
s_{i}
the simple reflection corresponding to
i\in\Delta_{n}
. For any
I\subset\Delta_{n}
, define
\mathscr{W}_{I}
as the subgroup of
\mathscr{W}_{n}
generated by simple reflections
s_{i}
with
i\in I
. The Weyl group of
\mathbf{G}_{/E}
is
\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}:=\Pi_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}\cong S_{n}^{d_{L}}
, where
\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}\cong\mathscr{W}_{n}
is the
\sigma
-th factor of
\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}
. For
i\in\Delta_{n}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, let
s_{i,\sigma}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}
be the simple reflection corresponding to
i\in\Delta_{n}
.

For any
I\subset\Delta_{n}
, define
\mathscr{W}_{I,\sigma}
to be the
\sigma
-copy of
\mathscr{W}_{I}
, i.e., to be the subgroup of
\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}
generated by simple reflections
s_{i,\sigma}
with
i\in I
. For
S\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
and
I\subset{\Delta_{n}}
, we put
\mathscr{W}_{I,S}:=\prod_{\sigma\in S}\mathscr{W}_{I,\sigma}
. If
\underline{i}_{S}:=(i_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in S}\in{\Delta_{n}^{|S|}}
, we put
s_{\underline{i}_{S}}:=\prod_{\sigma\in S}s_{i_{\sigma},\sigma}
. Let
I,J
be subsets of
\Delta_{n}
. Recall that
\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}
has a canonical set of representatives, which we will denote by
[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}]:=[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}]^{\min}
, given by taking in each double coset the elements of minimal length. When
J=\emptyset
, we put
{}^{I}\mathscr{W}_{n}=[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{\emptyset}]^{\min}=[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}]^{\min}
. For
S\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
and
I\subset{\Delta_{n}}
, we put
{}^{I}\mathscr{W}_{n,S}:=\prod_{\sigma\in S}{{}^{I}\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}}
. Moreover, we have relative Bruhat decompositions

G=\coprod_{w\in[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}]}{\mathbf{P}}_{{I}}(L)w{\mathbf{P}}_{{J}}(L)=\coprod_{w\in[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}]}{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{I}}(L)w{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{J}}(L).

(2.1)

General linear group
{\rm GL}_{n}
-II

In this paper, we will study some locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic and locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations given by a Zelevinsky-segment. We list the basic notation in the theory of the Zelevinsky-segment.

Let
k,r
be two integers such that
n=kr
. We put
{\Delta_{n}(k)}:=\{r,2r,\cdots,(k-1)r\}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
and
\Delta_{n}^{k}:=\Delta_{n}\backslash{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. For a subset
I\subset{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, we put

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}:={\mathbf{L}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}:={\mathbf{P}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}:={\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},

(2.2)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}:={\mathbf{N}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}:={\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}:={\mathbf{Z}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},{\mathbf{D}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}:={\mathbf{D}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}.

Let
{\mathfrak{l}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{p}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
\overline{{\mathfrak{n}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{d}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
\overline{{\mathfrak{z}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
be the
L
-Lie algebras of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}
,
{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
{\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
{\mathbf{D}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
and
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}
,
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}
respectively. Furthermore, for
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}^{+}
, we put

\displaystyle L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{I}:=L(\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},\overline{L}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda})_{I}:=\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},\hskip 210.0pt

(2.3)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle M}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda}):={M}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}(\underline{\lambda}),\overline{M}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda}):=\overline{{M}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}(-\underline{\lambda}).\;

Similarly, for a subset
S\subset\Sigma_{L}
, we put

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}:=\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I,S}:=\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I,S},^{I}\mathscr{W}_{n,S}^{{\langle r\rangle}}:=^{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}\mathscr{W}_{n,S}\hskip 110.0pt.\;

(2.4)

When
I=\emptyset
, we omit the subscripts
I
in (
2.2
), (
2.3
) and (
2.4
). For example, we have

{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\rm GL}_{r}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&{\rm GL}_{r}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&0\\
0&0&0&{\rm GL}_{r}\\
\end{array}\right)\subseteq{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\rm GL}_{r}&0&\cdots&0\\
\ast&{\rm GL}_{r}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&0\\
\ast&\ast&\cdots&{\rm GL}_{r}\\
\end{array}\right)

The parabolic subgroups of
{\rm GL}_{n}
containing the parabolic subgroup
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
are
\{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\}_{I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)}
.

Characters

For a topological commutative group
M
, we use
{\rm Hom}(M,E)
(resp.
{\rm Hom}_{\infty}(M,E)
) to denote the
E
-space of continuous (resp. locally constant) additive
E
-valued characters on
M
. If
M
is a totally disconnected group, then the terminology ”locally constant” is often replaced by smooth. If
M
is a locally
L
-analytic group, denote by
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(M,E)
the
E
-vector space of locally
\sigma
-analytic characters on
M
(i.e., the continuous characters which are locally
\sigma
-analytic
E
-valued functions on
M
). The local class field theory implies a bijection
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\cong{\rm Hom}({\rm Gal}_{L},E)
. Let
\alpha\in E^{\times}
, denote by
{\rm unr}(\alpha)
the unramified character of
L^{\times}
sending uniformizers to
\alpha
.

Representation theory

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a split connected reductive group over
L
, and
{\mathbf{P}}
a parabolic subgroup of
{\mathbf{G}}
.
Let
G,P,N_{P},L_{P}
be the
L
-points of
{\mathbf{G}},{\mathbf{P}},{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{P}},{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathbf{P}}
, respectively. Let
\overline{P}
be the parabolic subgroup opposite to
P
.

If
V
is a continuous representation of
G
over
E
, we denote by
V^{{\rm an}}
its locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic vectors. If
V
is
locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
G
, we denote by
V^{\mathrm{sm}}
(resp.
V^{\mathrm{lalg}}
) the smooth (resp, locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) subrepresentation of
V
consisting of its smooth (locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) vectors (see
[
18
]
,
[
19
]
and
[
34
]
and for details).

For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, we identify
w
with the corresponding permutation matrix. If
H
is a closed subgroup of
G
and
(\rho,V)
is an abstract representation of
H
on an
E
-vector space
V
, we put
H^{w}:=w^{-1}Hw
(resp.,
{}^{w}H:=wHw^{-1}
), and
\rho^{w}(h):=\rho(whw^{-1})
. Then
(\rho^{w},V)
forms an abstract representation of
H^{w}
on the same
E
-vector space
V
.

Let
\pi_{P}
be a continuous Banach representation of
P
over
E
. We denote by

(\mathrm{Ind}_{P}^{G}\pi_{P})^{\mathcal{C}^{0}}:=\{f:G\rightarrow\pi_{P}\text{ continuous}:f(pg)=pf(g),\forall p\in P\}

the continuous parabolic induction of
G
. It becomes a continuous representation of
G
over
E
by endowing the left action of
G
with the right translation on functions:
(gf)(g^{\prime})=f(g^{\prime}g)
. Likewise, let
\pi_{P}
be a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation of
P
on a locally convex
E
-vector space of compact type, denoted by

(\mathrm{Ind}_{P}^{G}\pi_{P})^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}:=\{f:G\rightarrow\pi_{P}\text{ locally ${\mathbb{Q}}_{p}$-analytic}:f(pg)=pf(g),\forall p\in P\}

the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction of
G
. It becomes a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation of
G
on a locally convex
E
-vector space of compact type (see e.g.,
[
25
, Rem. 5.4]
), by endowing the same left action of
G
.

Let
\pi_{P}
be a smooth representation of
P
over
E
. We denote by

i^{G}_{P}\pi_{P}:=(\mathrm{Ind}_{P}^{G}\pi_{P})^{\infty}=\{f:G\rightarrow\pi_{P}\text{ smooth}:f(pg)=pf(g),\forall p\in P\}

the (un-normalized) smooth parabolic induction. It becomes a smooth representation of
G
over
E
by endowing the left action of
G
with the right translation on functions. Let
(\pi,V)
be a smooth representation of
G
over
E
. We denote by

r^{G}_{P}V=V/\langle\pi(n)v-v:n\in N_{P},v\in V\rangle
the Jacquet module of the smooth representation
(\pi,V)
with respect to
P
. Then
r^{G}_{P}V
becomes a smooth
L_{P}
-representations over
E
. Denote by
\delta_{P}
the modulus character of
P
. Let
V
(resp.
W
) be a smooth
G
-representation (resp.
L_{P}
-representation) over
E
, then we have

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{G}(V,i^{G}_{P}W)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{L_{P}}(r_{P}^{G}V,W).

(2.5)

Moreover, if
V
and
W
are admissible, we have

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{G}(i^{G}_{\overline{P}}W,V)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{L_{P}}(W\otimes_{E}\delta_{P},r^{G}_{P}V).

(2.6)

Smooth parabolic induction is a special case of smooth compact induction. Let
H
be a closed subgroup of
G
, and
\pi_{H}
be a smooth representation of
H
over
E
. Let

\displaystyle c\text{-}i^{G}_{H}\pi_{H}:=(\mathrm{c}\text{-}\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}\pi_{H})^{\infty}

(2.7)

\displaystyle=\{f:G\rightarrow H\text{ smooth}:\text{the support of $f$ is compact modulo $H$},f(pg)=pf(g),\forall p\in P\}

be the smooth compact induction (or smooth induction with compact support). It becomes a smooth representation of
G
over
E
by endowing the left action of
G
with the right translation on functions.

2.2
Preliminaries on locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations

In this section, let
H
be a strictly paracompact locally
L
-analytic group (where strictly paracompact means that any open covering of
H
can be refined into a covering by pairwise disjoint open subsets).

Let
V
be a Hausdorff convex
E
-vector space. Let
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,V)
be the locally convex
E
-vector spaces of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic
V
-valued functions on
H
(
[
35
, Section 2, Page 447]
, see also
[
19
, Definintion 2.1.25]
). Let
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
be the strongly dual space of
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
, which is called the locally convex
E
-algebra of
E
-valued distributions on
G
(see
[
35
, Proposition 2.3]
). We have an embedding
E[G]\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
by sending
h\in H
to the delta function
\delta_{h}:=[f\mapsto f(h)]
, with
f\in\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,V)
. By
[
35
, Lemma 3.1]
,
E[G]
generates a dense subspace in
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
. Let
{\mathfrak{h}}
be the
L
-Lie algebra of
H
, and let
U_{E}({\mathfrak{h}})=U({\mathfrak{h}})\otimes_{L}E
be the universal enveloping algebra of
{\mathfrak{h}}
over
E
. For any
X\in{\mathfrak{h}}
,
f\in\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
, and
h\in H
, we define

(X\cdot f)(h)=\frac{d}{dt}f(\exp(-tX)h)|_{t=0}.

Such an
{\mathfrak{h}}
-action extends
E
-linearly to an action of
U_{E}({\mathfrak{h}})
on
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
, which induces an injection
U_{E}({\mathfrak{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(H,E)
denote the
E
-vector spaces of locally constant
E
-valued functions on
H
, and let
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}(H,E)
denote its dual. The map
h\mapsto\delta_{h}
induces in fact an injection
E[H]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\infty}(H,E)
. Let
I({\mathfrak{h}})
be the closed two-sided ideal of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
generated by
{\mathfrak{h}}
. One has
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}(H,E)=\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)/I(\mathfrak{h})
(see
[
38
, Section 2]
for more detailed and more precise statements).

We refer without comment to
[
19
, Section 3.6]
or
[
35
, Section 3]
for the definitions of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
H
over locally convex
E
-vector spaces. Let
Z^{\prime}
be a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic closed subgroup of the center
Z_{H}
of
H
. Let
\chi^{\infty}
(resp.,
\chi
) be a fixed smooth character (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic character) of
Z^{\prime}
. We first list the following categories. Let
{\textbf{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)}
(resp.
{\textbf{Rep}_{E}^{\infty,\mathrm{ad}}(H)}
) be category of smooth (resp. admissible smooth) representations of
H
over
E
with morphisms being all
H
-equivalent linear maps. Let

{\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z^{\prime}=\chi^{\infty}}^{\infty}(H)}
(resp.
{\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z^{\prime}=\chi^{\infty}}^{\infty,\mathrm{ad}}(H)}
) be the category of smooth (resp. admissible smooth) representations of
H
over
E
on which
Z^{\prime}
acts via the character
\chi^{\infty}
. The morphisms are all
H
-equivariant linear maps. Denote by

{\textbf{Rep}_{E}(H)}
(resp.
{\textbf{Rep}_{E}^{\mathrm{ad}}(H)}
) the category of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
H
over barrelled locally convex Hausdorff
E
-vector spaces with morphisms being all continuous
H
-equivalent linear maps. Let
{\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z^{\prime}=\chi}(H})
(resp.
{\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z^{\prime}=\chi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(H)}
) be category of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
H
over barrelled locally convex Hausdorff
E
-vector space on which
Z^{\prime}
acts via the character
\chi
. The morphisms are all continuous
H
-equivalent linear maps.

Let
\mathcal{M}(H)
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}^{\infty}(H)
) be the abelian category of (abstract)
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
-modules (resp.,
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}(H,E)
-modules), and let
\mathcal{M}_{Z^{\prime},\chi}(H)
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{Z^{\prime},\chi}(H)
) be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{M}(H)
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}^{\infty}(H)
) of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
-modules (resp.,
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}(H,E)
) on which
Z^{\prime}
acts by
\chi^{-1}
.

Let
V
be a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation of
H
, then the continuous dual
V^{\vee}
is naturally equipped with a canonical
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
-module structure given by the action
\delta_{g}\cdot w(v)=w(g^{-1}v)
(this action is well-defined since
g\mapsto w(g^{-1}v)
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
, and gives a
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
-module structure on
V^{\vee}
by the density of
\{\delta_{h}\}_{h\in H}
in
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
). Suppose
V
is a smooth representation of
H
over
E
. In that case, the abstract dual
V^{\vee}
of
V
is a
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}(H,E)
-module (note that
V^{\vee}
is also the continuous dual of
V
if
V
is equipped with the finest locally convex topology, and the
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}(H,E)
-action is given in the same way as locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic case). It follows from
[
36
, Theorem 6.3]
that the functor
V\mapsto V^{\vee}
induces a fully faithful contravariant functor form
{\textbf{Rep}_{E}(H)}
to the abelian category
\mathcal{M}(H)
. Moreover, the functor
V\mapsto V^{\vee}
also induces a fully faithful contravariant functor from
{\textbf{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)}
to the abelian category
\mathcal{M}^{\infty}(H)
.

Recall that a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
V_{H}
of
H
over
E
is called very strongly admissible (in the sense of
[
17
, Definition 0.12]
) if there exists a continuous admissible representation
B_{H}
of
H
on an
E
-Banach space and a continuous
E
-linear and
H
-equivalent injection
V_{H}\hookrightarrow B_{H}
.

2.3
Smooth and locally analytic extension groups

Recall that
{\textbf{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)}
contains enough injectives and projectives
, see
[
43
]
or
[
28
, Section 3]
. An explicit injective resolution of
V\in{\textbf{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)}
is given by the Bruhat-Tits building
Y_{H}
of
H
(refer
[
5
, Chapter X, 1.11. Lemma, 2.3 and 2.4. Theorem]
, but in this paper, we do not need the concrete form of
Y_{H}
). The Bruhat-Tits building
Y_{H}
is a finite polysimplicial complex, with the decomposition

Y_{H}=\coprod_{q}Y_{q},

where
Y_{q}
is the set of
q
-dimensional cells. Let
C^{q}_{E}(Y_{H},V)^{\infty}
be the space of finite smooth
q
-chains with
V
-coefficients. Then

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow V\longrightarrow C^{1}_{E}(Y_{H},V)^{\infty}\longrightarrow C^{2}_{E}(Y_{H},V)^{\infty}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow C^{q}_{E}(Y_{H},V)^{\infty}\longrightarrow\cdots

is an injective resolution of
V
by the complex of
V
-valued cochains on the Bruhat-Tits building
Y_{H}
. In particular, this injective resolution lies in the category
{\textbf{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)}
(see
[
5
, Chapter X, 2.4. Theorem]
). Therefore, we can compute the
{\rm Ext}
-groups
{\rm Ext}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)}(V,W)
for a given pair of smooth
H
-representations
V,W
by choosing an injective resolution of
W
. By
[
15
, Proposition 2.3]
, if
V
,
W\in\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)
are admissible smooth representations of
H
. Then the functor
V\mapsto V^{\vee}
induces natural bijections
{\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)}(V,W)\cong{\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}(H)}(W^{\vee},V^{\vee}),\ \forall r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
. For
r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, we put

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i,\infty}_{H}(V,W):={\rm Ext}^{i}_{\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)}(V,W),\;{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{\infty}(H,V):={\rm Ext}^{i}_{\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)}(1,V).\;

for simplicity. Let
Z^{\prime}\subseteq Z_{H}
be a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic closed subgroup of
Z_{H}
, and let
\chi^{\infty}
be a smooth character of
Z^{\prime}
. If
V,W\in\mathrm{Rep}^{\chi^{\infty}}_{E,Z=\chi}(H)
, we put

{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{H,Z^{\prime}=\chi^{\infty}}(V,W):={\rm Ext}^{i}_{\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E,Z^{\prime}=\chi^{\infty}}(H)}(V,W)

for the
i
-th smooth extension group of
V,W
on which
Z^{\prime}
acts via
\chi^{\infty}
.

In this paper, we define extension groups of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations in the category
\mathcal{M}(H)
of all abstract
D^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)
-modules. Let
V
,
W
be locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
H
over
E
. As in
[
40
, Définition 3.1]
, we put

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{r}_{H}(V,W):={\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathcal{M}(H)}(W^{\vee},V^{\vee}),\;{\mathrm{H}}^{r}_{{\rm an}}(H,V)={\rm Ext}^{r}_{H}(1,V),

(2.8)

where
{\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathcal{M}(H)}
denotes the
r
-th extension group in the abelian category
\mathcal{M}(H)
. If
H^{\prime}
is a closed locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic subgroup of
H
, we have a natural map

{\rm Ext}^{r}_{H}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r}_{H^{\prime}}(V|_{H^{\prime}},W|_{H^{\prime}})

by restricting
V
and
W
on
H^{\prime}
. If furthermore
H^{\prime}
is a normal subgroup of
H
, then
H/H^{\prime}
is also a

{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic subgroup. Since

D^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H/H^{\prime},E)\cong D^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H,E)\otimes_{D^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H^{\prime},E)}E

(see
[
8
, Section 5.1]
), we deduce that
{\mathrm{H}}^{r}_{{\rm an}}(H^{\prime},V)
admits a
D^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}(H/H^{\prime},E)
-modules structure. Similarly, let
\chi
be a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic character of
Z^{\prime}
. If
Z^{\prime}
acts on both
V
and
W
via the character
\chi
, we put

{\rm Ext}^{r}_{H,Z^{\prime}=\chi}(V,W):={\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathcal{M}_{Z^{\prime},\chi}(H)}(W^{\vee},V^{\vee}),

where the latter denotes the
r
-th extension group in
\mathcal{M}_{Z^{\prime},\chi}(H)
. When
Z^{\prime}=Z
, we denote
{\rm Ext}^{r}_{H,\chi}(V,W):={\rm Ext}^{r}_{H,Z=\chi}(V,W)
. If moreover
V
and
W
are admissible locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
H
over
E
(resp., admissible locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
H
on which
Z^{\prime}
acts via
\chi
) , then
[
15
, Lemma 2.2]
implies that

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{H}(V,W)
(resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{r}_{H,Z^{\prime}=\chi}(V,W)
) consists of admissible locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations which are extensions of
V
by
W
(resp., which are extensions of
V
by
W
on which
Z^{\prime}
acts via
\chi
).

Remark 2.1
.

Suppose that
Z^{\prime}
is a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic closed subgroup of
Z
such that
H\cong H/Z^{\prime}\times Z^{\prime}
as
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic manifolds. Let
\chi^{\infty}
be a smooth character of
Z^{\prime}
over
E
. If
\chi^{\infty}
can be lifted to a smooth character of
H
, we have an equivalence of categories
\mathrm{Rep}_{E,Z^{\prime}=\chi^{\infty}}^{\infty}(H)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H/Z^{\prime})
,
V\mapsto V\otimes_{E}(\chi^{\infty})^{-1}
. We also have an equivalence of categories
\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{Z^{\prime},\chi^{\infty}}(H)\cong\mathcal{M}^{\infty}(H/Z^{\prime})
. Let
V
,
W\in\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E,Z^{\prime}=\chi}(H)
be admissible smooth representations of
H
, we have
{\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{Z^{\prime}=\chi^{\infty}}(H)}(V,W)\cong{\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{Z^{\prime},\chi^{\infty}}(H)}(W^{\vee},V^{\vee}),\ \forall r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
. For locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic case, if furthermore
H/Z^{\prime}
is also strictly paracompact, we have a natural equivalence of categories between
\mathcal{M}_{Z^{\prime},\chi}(H)\cong\mathcal{M}(H/Z^{\prime})
. Therefore, we have
{\rm Ext}^{r}_{H,Z^{\prime}=\chi}(V,W)\cong{\rm Ext}^{r}_{H/Z^{\prime}}(V,W)
.

We still need two lemmas. By
[
42
, Section 6]
, we have

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
V,W\in\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(H)
. Suppose there is an element
z\in Z_{H}
such that
z
acts on
V
and
W
by multiplication by two different scalars
z_{V}\neq z_{W}
. Then we have

{\rm Ext}^{\bullet,\infty}_{H}(V,W)=0
.

We end this section by recalling some spectral sequences which give relations between smooth extension groups and locally analytic extension groups. By
[
37
, Page 306-307 (+)]
and
[
25
, (46)]
, we have

Lemma 2.3
.

For any
V\in\mathbf{{Rep}}_{E}(H)
and
W\in\mathbf{{Rep}}_{E}^{\infty}(H)
, we have

{\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}(H)}(W^{\vee},{\mathrm{H}}^{s}({\mathfrak{h}}_{\Sigma_{L}},V^{\vee}))\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s}_{H}(V,W),

(2.9)

Furthermore, if
V\in\mathbf{{Rep}}_{E,Z^{\prime}=1}(H)
and
W\in\mathbf{{Rep}}_{E,Z^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}(H)
, then we have

{\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{Z^{\prime},1}(H)}(W^{\vee},{\mathrm{H}}^{s}(\overline{{{\mathfrak{h}}}}_{\Sigma_{L}},V^{\vee}))\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s}_{H,Z^{\prime}=1}(V,W).\;

(2.10)

In particular, if
V\in\mathbf{{Rep}}_{E}^{\infty}(H)
(resp.
\mathbf{{Rep}}_{E,Z^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}(H)
), we get an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{H}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{H}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{H}(V,W\otimes_{E}{\mathrm{H}}_{1}({\mathfrak{h}}_{\Sigma_{L}},E))

(2.11)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2,\infty}_{H}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}_{H}(V,W)

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{resp.\;}0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{H,Z^{\prime}=1}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{H,Z^{\prime}=1}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{H,Z^{\prime}=1}(V,W\otimes_{E}{\mathrm{H}}_{1}(\overline{{{\mathfrak{h}}}}_{\Sigma_{L}},E))

(2.12)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2,\infty}_{H,Z^{\prime}=1}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}_{H,Z^{\prime}=1}(V,W)\big).

Note that
{\mathrm{H}}^{s}({\mathfrak{h}}_{\Sigma_{L}},-)
(resp.,
{\mathrm{H}}_{s}({\mathfrak{h}}_{\Sigma_{L}},-)
) is
r
-th Lie algebraic cohomology (resp., homology) for
{\mathfrak{h}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
.

2.4
Künneth formula for smooth extension groups

Let
{\mathbf{H}}
and
{\mathbf{S}}
be two connected reductive groups over
L
. We put
H:={\mathbf{H}}(L)
(resp.,
S:={\mathbf{S}}(L)
) for its
L
-points, respectively. The following statement is well known. The proof follows along the line of
[
5
, Chapter X, 6.1. Theorem]
.

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
V,W\in\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(G)
and
V^{\prime},W^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(S)
. Then

\bigoplus_{p+q=m}{\rm Ext}^{p,\infty}_{H}(V,W)\otimes_{E}{\rm Ext}^{q,\infty}_{S}(V^{\prime},W^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{m,\infty}_{H\times S}(V{{\otimes}_{E}}W,V^{\prime}{{\otimes}_{E}}W^{\prime}).

Proof.

The proof of
[
5
, Chapter X, 6.1. Theorem]
can be modified to our setting.
∎

Remark 2.5
.

Let’s remark that the Künneth formulas for locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations are more subtle.

3
Generalized parabolic (smooth) Steinberg representations

Before studying the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations, we need some preparations on smooth generalized parabolic Steinberg representations of
G
. We study the generalized parabolic Steinberg representation attached to the Zelevinsky-segments (we recall the basic concepts of Zelevinsky-segments in Appendix Section
6.1
). We add the terminology
”parabolic” to distinguish from the usual (Borel) Steinberg representation induced from Borel subgroup
{\mathbf{B}}(L)
. To study parabolically induced representations (Section
3.2
), and define (generalized) Bruhat filtration (see (
5.7
)), we review in Section
3.1
some properties of the representatives of the minimal length of certain double cosets in
\mathscr{W}_{n}
. In Section
3.2
, we talk about (smooth) generalized parabolic Steinberg representations. We also describe
the Jordan-Hölder factors of such parabolically induced representations. The Proof is contained in Appendix Section
6.3
.

3.1
Double coset representatives

3.1.1
Double coset representatives-I

Let
I,J
be subsets of
\Delta_{n}
, and let
\underline{n}_{I}=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{l})
(resp.,
\underline{n}_{J}=(n_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,n_{e}^{\prime})
) be the ordered partition of
n
with respect to
I
(resp.,
J
) defined as in Section
2.1
“General linear group
{\rm GL}_{n}
-I”, where
l=|\Delta_{n}\backslash I|+1
(resp.,
e=|\Delta_{n}\backslash J|+1
).

We recall that the element
w
in
[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}]
is characterized by the properties
wJ\subseteq\Phi^{+}_{\Delta_{n}}
and
w^{-1}I\subseteq\Phi^{+}_{\Delta_{n}}
. More precisely, let
I_{1},\cdots,I_{l}
and
J_{1},\cdots,J_{e}
be the blocks of
\underline{n}_{I}
and
\underline{n}^{\prime}_{J}
respectively. Then by
[
1
, (1.6)]
, the correspondence
w\mapsto B(w):=(|I_{i}\cap w(J_{j})|)_{i,j}
is the bijection of
[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}]
with the set
M_{I,J}
of matrices
B=(b_{ij})_{i,j}
such that
b_{i,j}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
\sum_{j=1}^{e}b_{ij}=n_{i}
(resp.
\sum_{i=1}^{l}b_{ij}=n_{j}^{\prime}
) for any
1\leq i\leq l
(resp.
1\leq j\leq e
). Equivalently, by
[
4
, (1.2)]
, we can identify
[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}]
with the set

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}|w(x)<w(y)\text{\;if\;}x<y\text{\;and both $x$ and $y$ belong to the same block of $\underline{n}_{J}$},

(3.1)

\displaystyle w^{-1}(x)<w^{-1}(y)\text{\;if\;}x<y\text{\;and both $x$ and $y$ belong to the same block of $\underline{n}_{I}$}\}.

As in
[
11
, Theorem 2.7.4]
, for
w\in[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}]
, we define
J\cap w^{-1}I:=\{j\in J:w(j)=i\text{ for some }i\in I\}
(resp.,
wJ\cap I:=\{i\in I:w(j)=i\text{ for some }j\in J\}=w(J\cap w^{-1}I)
). Then
[
11
, Theorem 2.7.4]
asserts that
\mathscr{W}_{wJ\cap I}=\mathscr{W}_{I}\cup w\mathscr{W}_{J}w^{-1}
. Furthermore, by
[
11
, Corollary 2.8.9]
, the Levi decomposition of
{\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I}(L)^{w}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)\cap{}^{w}{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{J}(L)
) is given by (recall the notation
(-)^{w}
and
{}^{w}(-)
of Section
2.1
“Representation theory”)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I}(L)^{w}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)^{w}\cdot({\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}_{I}(L)^{w})={\mathbf{L}}_{J\cap w^{-1}I}(L)\cdot({\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}_{I}(L)^{w}),

(3.2)

\displaystyle\text{resp., }{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)\cap{}^{w}{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{J}(L)=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)\cap{}^{w}{\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)\cdot({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)\cap{}^{w}{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}_{J}(L))={\mathbf{L}}_{I\cap wJ}(L)\cdot({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)\cap{}^{w}{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}_{J}(L)).

3.1.2
Double coset representatives-II

We use the notation of Section
2.1
“General linear group
{\rm GL}_{n}
-II”. This section aims to review some properties of the representatives of the minimal length of certain double cosets in
\mathscr{W}_{n}
, which are related to the smooth parabolic induction associated with a Zelevinsky-segment (the contents of Definition
6.2
applied to
(m,s)=(r,k)
).

Let
I
be a subset of
\Delta_{n}(k)
. We define an ordered partition
\underline{k}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}:=(k_{1},\cdots,k_{l_{I}})
of
k
with
\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I=\{k_{1}r,(k_{1}+k_{2})r,\cdots,(k_{1}+\cdots+k_{l_{I}})r\}
and
l_{I}:=k-|I|
. We call
\underline{k}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
the ordered partition of integer
k
associated with the
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
. Then

{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)={\rm GL}_{k_{1}r}(L)\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{k_{l_{I}}r}(L).

Let
J
be another subset of
\Delta_{n}(k)
. We put

\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I},{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}):=\{w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}:({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I})^{w}={\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}\}\cong\{w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}:w(\Phi_{J})=\Phi_{I}\},

(3.3)

We have a bijection
\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\cong\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cap[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]
. Thus
we see that the set
\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
has a canonical set of representatives, given by taking in each double coset the elements of minimal length. It is easy to see that

\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\cong\mathscr{W}_{k}(\cong S_{k})
. More precisely, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=\{w^{\circ}:w^{\circ}(ir+l)=w(i)r+l,\forall 0\leq i\leq k-1,1\leq l\leq r,\text{for some }w\in\mathscr{W}_{k}\}.

(3.4)

In the sequel, we shall denote

\mathscr{W}_{I,J}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}):=\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cap[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}]

for
I,J\subset\Delta_{n}(k)
. If
I=J=\emptyset
, we omit the subscripts
I,J
. In particular,
\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cong\mathscr{W}_{k}
.

The following lemma will be used frequently when we meet the irreducible cuspidal representation.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
\pi^{\prime}
be an irreducible cuspidal representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
over
E
. For
w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]
, one has
r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\pi^{\prime}=0
unless
w\in\mathscr{W}_{I,\emptyset}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})=\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cap[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]
.

Proof.

Since
\pi^{\prime}
is an irreducible supercuspidal representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
, we get that the smooth representation
r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)^{w}\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\pi^{\prime}
is zero unless
{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)^{w}\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)=1
, this is equivalent to
w(\Delta_{n}^{k})\subseteq\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup J
by
[
11
, Corollary 2.8.8]
(or see
[
12
, Corollary 6.3.4]
and its proof). We claim that
w(\Delta_{n}^{k})\subseteq\Delta_{n}^{k}
(so
w(\Delta_{n}^{k})=\Delta_{n}^{k}
), i.e.,
w(\Delta_{n}^{k})\cap J=\emptyset
. Let
\{j\in J:w(\Delta_{n}^{k})\cap\{j\}\neq\emptyset\}=\{j_{1}<j_{2}<\cdots<j_{a}\}
. Then the condition
w(\Delta_{n}^{k})\subseteq\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup J
shows that for each
1\leq i\leq a
, there exists an integer
1\leq l_{i}\leq r-1
such that
J_{i}:=\{(j_{i}-1)r+l_{i},(j_{i}-1)r+l_{i}+1,\cdots,(j_{i}-1)r+(r-1),j_{i}r,j_{i}r+1,\cdots,j_{i}r+l_{i}-1\}\subset w(\Delta_{n}^{k})
, and
J_{i}^{\prime}:=\{(j_{i}+1)r+1,\cdots,(j_{i}+1)r+r-1,(j_{i}+2)r+1,\cdots,(j_{1+1}-2)r+1,\cdots,(j_{1+1}-2)r+r-1\}\subset w(\Delta_{n}^{k})
. Put
j_{0}=0
and
j_{a+1}=k+1
. Then

\displaystyle|w(\Delta_{n}^{k})|

\displaystyle=|J^{\prime}_{0}|+\sum_{i=1}^{a}(|J_{i}|+|J_{i}^{\prime}|)

(3.5)

\displaystyle=\min\{0,j_{1}-2\}\cdot(r-1)+\sum_{i=1}^{a}\Big(r-1+\min\{0,j_{i+1}-j_{i}-2\}\cdot(r-1)\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\min\{0,j_{1}-2\}+\sum_{i=1}^{a}\min\{1,j_{i+1}-j_{i}+1\}\Big)\cdot(r-1)<|\Delta_{n}^{k}|=k(r-1)

if
a>0
. This is impossible. By the characterization of elements in
[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]
, we see that
w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cap[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]
.
∎

Remark 3.2
.

This lemma is vacuous if
r=1
. Moreover, by
[
11
, Corollary 2.8.8]
(or see
[
12
, Corollary 6.3.4]
and its proof), we see that the following statements are equivalent:

\displaystyle{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}={\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}=1\Longleftrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}\subseteq{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)

(3.6)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow w^{-1}(\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I)\subseteq\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup J,

by (
3.2
). If
w\in\mathscr{W}_{I,J}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})=\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cap[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}]
, we further have Levi decompositions:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}={\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J\cap w^{-1}I}(L)\cdot({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}),

(3.7)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\cap{}^{w}{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)={\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cap wJ}(L)\cdot({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\cap{}^{w}{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)).

We use
\gamma_{I,J}^{w}
to denote the modulus character of
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}
acting on
\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)/\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}
.

We end this section by describing the modulus characters explicitly. Recall that
\underline{k}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
is the ordered partition of integer
k
associated with the
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
. For
0\leq i\leq l_{I}
, we put
s_{0}:=0
and
s_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{i}k_{j}r
. Explicitly, the modulus character
\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}
of
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)
is given by

\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}A_{1}&0&\cdots&0\\
\ast&A_{2}&\cdots&0\\
\ast&\ast&\ddots&0\\
\ast&\ast&\ast&A_{l}\\
\end{array}\right)\right)=\prod_{i=1}^{l}v_{k_{i}r}^{a_{i,I}}(A_{i}).

where
a_{i,I}=r\left(-\sum_{j=i+1}^{l}k_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}k_{j}\right)=r(2s_{i-1}+k_{i}-k)
(see
[
22
, Defintion 14.3.6]
). In particular, if
I=\emptyset
, then
a_{i,{\emptyset}}=r(2i-1-k)
.

3.2
Definitions of the generalized parabolic Steinberg representations

Fix an irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi
of
{\rm GL}_{r}(L)
over
E
. Let
\Delta_{[k-1,0]}(\pi)
be a Zelevinsky-segment over
E
(we use the notation of Definition
6.2
applied to
(m,s)=(r,k)
). Put

\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}:=(\otimes_{i=1}^{k}\pi(k-i))\otimes_{E}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{1/2}=\otimes_{i=1}^{k}\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k-2i+1)+k-i},\pi(k-i):=\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{k-i}.\;

(3.8)

This is an irreducible cuspidal smooth representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
over
E
.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
I
be a subset of
{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. We define
\pi_{I}
to be the unique irreducible subrepresentation of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}
. The existence of
\pi_{I}
is a direct consequence of
[
1
, 2.10. Proposition]
. In particular,
\pi_{\emptyset}=\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}
, and
\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}}=\langle\Delta_{[k-1,0]}(\pi)\rangle
(see the discussion after Appendix, Definition
6.1
).

For
w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
, we put

\displaystyle w(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}):=(\otimes_{i=1}^{k}\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{w(k-i)})\otimes_{E}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{1/2}.

(3.9)

The first goal is to compute
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}
.

Lemma 3.4
.

We have isomorphism of smooth
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
-representations:

{r}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\cong\bigoplus_{w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})}w(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}).

Remark 3.5
.

Indeed, this result is a direct consequence of
[
12
, Proposition 6.4.1]
, but we include a proof for the reader’s convenience.

Proof.

The arguments in Section
3.1
and the Bernstein-Zelevinsky geometric lemma
[
4
, 2.12.
Geometrical Lemma
]
shows that only elements in the subset
\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
appear in
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}
instead of the whole double coset
[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]
. In precise, by the Bernstein-Zelevinsky geometric lemma
[
4
, 2.12.
Geometrical Lemma
]
and
[
12
, Proposition 6.3.3]
, the semi-simplification of
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}
is isomorphic to

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]}{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\gamma^{w}\otimes_{E}\bigg({r}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}}\big({\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big)^{w}\bigg).

(3.10)

Recall that
\gamma^{w}
is the modulus character of
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}
acting on
\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)/\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}
. Since
{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
is an irreducible cuspidal representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
, we deduce by Lemma
3.1
that
w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
is a necessary condition for the non-vanishing of the representation
{r}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}}\big({\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big)^{w}
. In this case, by
[
12
, Corollary 6.3.4 (b), Theorem 6.3.5]
,
we have

\displaystyle({r}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}})^{\mathrm{ss}}\cong

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})}\bigg({i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\gamma^{w}\otimes_{E}(\otimes_{i=1}^{k}\pi\cdot v_{r}^{w(k-i)})\bigg)\otimes\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{1/2}

(3.11)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})}w(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}).

Note that all the central characters of
\{w(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}})\}_{w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})}
are pairwise different (since the central characters of
\{v_{r}^{k-i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
are pairwise different). Therefore, we deduce from Lemma
2.2
that

{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{G}\big(w(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}),w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}})\big)=0

for any pair of
w\neq w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
. It follows from (
3.11
) that
{r}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
is actually semi-simple and hence is isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})}w(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}})
.
∎

The following proposition plays a key role in proving the exactness of locally algebraic and locally analytic Tits complexes (see Section
4.3
). The proof of Proposition
3.6
is elementary but tedious (for example, if
r=1
, this proposition is obvious). We prove it in Appendix
6.4
. We suggest skipping the proof on the first reading.

Proposition 3.6
.

The family
\{\pi_{I}\}_{I\subset{\Delta_{n}(k)}}
satisfying the following conditions
\mathbf{[A1],[A2]}
.

[A1]

For
J\subseteq I
, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{J}={r}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{I},\hskip 213.0pt

(3.12)

\displaystyle\pi_{I}\hookrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{J}\text{ and therefore }P_{I,J}:{i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{G}\pi_{I}\hookrightarrow{i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}^{G}\pi_{J}.\hskip 20.0pt

(3.13)

[A2]

For any
I,J,I_{1},\cdots,I_{m}\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, we have

\displaystyle i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\cap i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{J}}=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}\cup J}}^{G}\pi_{{I}\cup{J}},\hskip 163.0pt

(3.14)

\displaystyle i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\cap\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}_{j}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}_{j}}\bigg)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\bigg(\pi_{{I}}\cap i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}_{j}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}_{j}}\bigg),\hskip 45.0pt

(3.15)

where the intersections are taken in
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
, by (
3.13
).

Definition 3.7
.

(Locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations)

Let
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, and let
I,J\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. We put

\displaystyle i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=\left(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{I}\right)\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda}),\hskip 150.0pt

\displaystyle u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=\sum_{J\supsetneq I}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

\displaystyle v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big/u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).

We put
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi):=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{0})
,
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi):=v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{0})
. We call
\{v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}(\text{resp., }\{v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}})
the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic (resp., smooth) generalized parabolic Steinberg representations of
G
, associated to the Zelevinsky-segment
\Delta_{[k-1,0]}(\pi)
and weight
\underline{\lambda}
. In particular, we have

v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}}^{\infty}(\pi)=\pi_{\Delta_{n}(k)}
.

Furthermore, for
J\subset I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
and
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}^{+}
. We put

\displaystyle v_{J,I}^{\infty}(\pi)=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{I}\Big/\sum_{J\subsetneq K\subseteq I}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}},

\displaystyle\Big(\text{resp., }v_{J,I}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=v_{J,I}^{\infty}(\pi)\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{I}\Big).\;

They are smooth (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) generalized parabolic Steinberg representations of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
. We put
{\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=v_{\emptyset,I}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi):=v_{\emptyset,I}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{0})
.

Proposition 3.8
.

\{v^{\infty}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}(\pi)\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}
are precisely the Jordan-Hölder factors of the smooth representation
