i^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi)
and
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\cong{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi)
, where
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi)
is the smooth parabolic Steinberg representation of
G
(see Definition
6.2
). Therefore, the Jordan-Hölder factors of the
locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
are
\{v^{\infty}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}
. In particular, we have
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\cong{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):={\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi)\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda})
.

We prove this Proposition in Appendix
6.3
, Lemma
6.4
.

4
Locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations

The main goal of this section is to study locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations. In Section
4.1
, we recall the theory of Orlik-Strauch. In Section
4.2
, we define locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations. In order to study the Jordan-Hölder factors of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representation, we first establish the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic and locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic Tits complexes in Section
4.3
. In Section
4.4
, we compute the multiplicities of certain composition factors of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic Steinberg representations by following the route of
[
29
]
.

4.1
Orlik-Strauch theory

In order to determine the composition factors of the above locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations, we apply the machinery constructing locally analytic representation
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{P}(-,-)
(the Orlik-Strauch functor).

Let
\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I},\Sigma_{L}}
be the Bernstein-Gelfand-Gelfand (BGG) category (see
[
7
, Section 2]
). Recall that if
\underline{{\lambda}}\in X^{+}_{I}
, then the generalized parabolic Verma module
\overline{M}_{I}(-\underline{{\lambda}})
belongs to the BGG category
\mathcal{O}^{\overline{{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{I},\Sigma_{L}}_{{\rm alg}}
. Furthermore, by
[
2
, Theorem 3.37]
, any irreducible constituents of
\overline{M}_{I}(-\underline{{\lambda}})\in\mathcal{O}^{\overline{{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{I},\Sigma_{L}}_{{\rm alg}}
in
\mathcal{O}^{\overline{{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{I},\Sigma_{L}}_{{\rm alg}}
have the form
\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda}^{\prime})
such that
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}
is strongly linked to
\underline{\lambda}
and
\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}\in X^{+}_{I}
(see
[
2
, Definition 3.31]
for the notion of strongly linked). Let
I^{\prime}
be a subset of
\Delta_{n}
containing
I
, then
\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I^{\prime}},\Sigma_{L}}
is a full subcategory of
\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I},\Sigma_{L}}
. Therefore, for any object
M\in\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I},\Sigma_{L}}
, there is a maximal subset
I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
such that
M\in\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I^{\prime}},\Sigma_{L}}
. We call
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I^{\prime}}
the maximal parabolic subgroup associated to
M
(or say that
I^{\prime}
is maximal for
M
).

We now recall the precise description of the Orlik-Strauch functor (see
[
30
, Theorem]
), see also
[
7
, Section 2]
. It has the form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{I}}}(-,-):\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{{{I}}},\Sigma_{L}}\times\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty,{\rm ad}}_{E}({\mathbf{L}}_{{I}}(L))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbf{{Rep}}_{E}(G),

\displaystyle(M,\pi_{I})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{I}}}(M,\pi_{I}).\;

By
[
7
, Theorem 2.3]
, or
[
30
, The main theorem]
, this functor satisfies the following properties.
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{I}}}(-,-)
is an exact bi-functor, which is contravariant (resp. covariant) in the first (resp. second) variable (see
[
30
, Propositions 4.7 and 4.9]
). If
M\in\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{I},\Sigma_{L}}
is simple, and
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I}
is the maximal parabolic subgroup associated to
M
, then the representation
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{I}}}(M,\pi_{I})
is topologically irreducible for any admissible irreducible smooth representation
\pi_{I}
of
{\mathbf{L}}_{{I}}(L)
. Let
I\subseteq J
and let
M\in\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{J},\Sigma_{L}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}}_{I},\Sigma_{L}}
. Then for all
\pi_{I}\in\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty,{\rm ad}}_{E}({\mathbf{L}}_{{I}}(L))
, we have an isomorphism of
G
-representations
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{I}}}(M,\pi_{I})\cong\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{J}}}(M,i^{{\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{I})
.

4.2
Locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations

Let
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, and let
I,J\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. We put
\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda}):=\pi_{I}\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{I}
and put

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda})\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}}.

If
J\supsetneq I
, then by
[
30
, The main theorem]
, we see that Proposition
3.6
(
3.13
) induces an injection

\displaystyle p^{{\rm an}}_{J,I}:{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{G(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}\Big(U(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{U(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{J,\Sigma_{L}})}\overline{L}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda})_{{J}},\pi_{J}\Big)

(4.1)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathcal{F}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G(L)}(q_{I,J},\mathrm{incl.})}\mathcal{F}^{G(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}\Big(U(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{U(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}})}\overline{L}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda})_{I},i^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\pi_{I}\Big)

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{F}^{G(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}\Big(U(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{U(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}})}\overline{L}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda})_{I},\pi_{I}\Big)={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

where the map
q_{I,J}:\overline{M}_{I}(-\underline{{\lambda}})=U(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{U(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}})}\overline{L}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda})_{{I}}\rightarrow\overline{M}_{J}(-\underline{{\lambda}})=U(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{U(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{J,\Sigma_{L}})}\overline{L}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda})_{J}
is defined in the next section. Therefore, we put

\displaystyle v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big/\sum_{J\supsetneq I}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).

We also put
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi):={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{{0}})
,
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi):=v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{0})
.

Definition 4.1
.

(Locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations)

For
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, we call
\{v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\}_{I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}
the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations associated to the Zelevinsky-segment
\Delta_{[k-1,0]}(\pi)
and weight
\underline{\lambda}
. Put
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,{\lambda})
and
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi):=v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{0})
, they are locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic Steinberg representations. Furthermore, for
J\subset I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
and
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}^{+}
. We put

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda})\right)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}.

We put

\displaystyle v_{J,I}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big/\sum_{J\subsetneq K\subseteq I}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

They are locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
. We put
{\rm St}_{I}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=v_{\emptyset,I}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

Example 4.2
.

(Borel case) When
r=1
, and
\pi=v_{1}^{\frac{1-n}{2}}
, we recover the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized (Borel) Steinberg representations defined in
[
15
, Section 2.1.2]
. More precisely, we have
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{{\rm an}}(v_{1}^{\frac{1-n}{2}},\underline{\lambda})=v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I}}^{{\rm an}}(\underline{\lambda})
,
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(v_{1}^{\frac{1-n}{2}},\underline{\lambda})=v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I}}^{\infty}(\underline{\lambda})
. In particular, we have
{\rm St}_{(1,n)}^{{\rm an}}(v_{1}^{\frac{1-n}{2}},\underline{\lambda})={\rm St}_{n}^{{\rm an}}(\underline{\lambda})
,

{\rm St}_{(1,n)}^{\infty}(v_{1}^{\frac{1-n}{2}},\underline{\lambda})={\rm St}_{n}^{\infty}(\underline{\lambda})
, etc.

4.3
The locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic and locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic Tits complexes

In this section, we will prove the exactness of the locally algebraic and locally analytic Tits complexes. They are used to compute the multiplicity of an irreducible constituent in the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representation
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{S}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
(see Section 4.4). In Section
4.4
and Section
5
, we will use these complexes to compute some extension groups between certain locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations.

Keep the notation in Section 2.1 “General linear group
{\rm GL}_{n}
-I”. For
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
and
w_{\sigma}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}
, we let
I(w_{\sigma})=\{{i}\in\Delta_{n}|l(s_{i,\sigma}w_{\sigma})>l(w_{\sigma})\}
, where
l(\bullet)
is the length function. Let
\mathrm{supp}(w_{\sigma})
be the set of simple reflections appearing in one (and so in any) reduced expression of
w_{\sigma}
, Let
w=(w_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}\cong S_{n}^{d_{L}}
. We put

I(w)=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}I(w_{\sigma}),\mathrm{supp}(w)=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\mathrm{supp}(w_{\sigma}).

By definition, we see that
w\in\mathscr{W}_{I,\Sigma_{L}}
if and only if
\mathrm{supp}(w_{\sigma})\subset I
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
.

Let
I
be a subset of
\Delta_{n}
, and let
\underline{{\lambda}}=(\underline{{\lambda}}_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\in X^{+}_{I}
. We put

\overline{M}_{I,\sigma}(-\underline{{\lambda}}_{\sigma}):=U(\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma})\otimes_{U(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{I,\sigma})}\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma})_{I},\overline{M}_{\sigma}(-\underline{{\lambda}}_{\sigma}):=\overline{M}_{\emptyset,\sigma}(-\underline{{\lambda}}_{\sigma}).

Then we have

\overline{M}_{I}(-\underline{{\lambda}})=U(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{U(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}})}\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})_{I}\cong\bigotimes_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\overline{M}_{I,\sigma}(-\underline{{\lambda}}_{\sigma}).

For
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, we write

\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma}:=(\lambda_{1,\sigma},\cdots,\lambda_{n,\sigma}),\underline{\lambda}^{\sigma}:=(\lambda_{1,\sigma^{\prime}},\cdots,\lambda_{n,\sigma^{\prime}})_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}}
. Then for each
w_{\sigma}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, fix once
and for all an embedding
i_{w_{\sigma}}:\overline{M}_{\sigma}(-w_{\sigma}\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma})\hookrightarrow\overline{M}_{\sigma}(-\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma})
. If
w_{\sigma}^{\prime}\geq w_{\sigma}
(for the Bruhat order on
\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}
), we have a unique map
i_{w^{\prime}_{\sigma},w_{\sigma}}:\overline{M}_{\sigma}(-w^{\prime}_{\sigma}\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma})\rightarrow\overline{M}_{\sigma}(-w_{\sigma}\cdot\underline{\lambda})
such that
i_{w^{\prime}_{\sigma}}=i_{w_{\sigma^{\prime}},w_{\sigma}}\circ i_{w_{\sigma}}
(
[
29
, Page 653]
). By the argument in
[
27
, Proposition 2.1]
or
[
29
, Section 2, Page 653]
, we have a surjective map
q_{I}:\overline{M}(-\underline{{\lambda}})\longrightarrow\overline{M}_{I}(-\underline{{\lambda}})
and an exact sequence,

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathscr{W}_{I,\Sigma_{L}}\\
l(w)=1\end{subarray}}\overline{M}(-w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\xrightarrow{\bigoplus_{w}\otimes_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}i_{w_{\sigma}}}\overline{M}(-\underline{{\lambda}})\longrightarrow\overline{M}_{I}(-\underline{{\lambda}})\longrightarrow 0.

(4.2)

If
I^{\prime}\subset I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
, then we obtain a transition map
q_{I^{\prime},I}:\overline{M}_{I^{\prime}}(-\underline{{\lambda}})\longrightarrow\overline{M}_{I}(-\underline{{\lambda}})
such that
q_{I}=q_{I^{\prime},I}\circ q_{I^{\prime}}
.

Let
\underline{{\lambda}}\in X^{+}_{\Delta_{n}}
be a dominant weight, recall that the
I
-parabolic BGG-resolution of
\overline{L}(-\underline{{\lambda}})

[
23
, Section 9.16, Theorem]
is given by the exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\overline{M}_{I}(-{}^{I}w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{}^{I}\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}\\
l(w)=l(^{I}w)-1\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\overline{M}_{I}(-w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\rightarrow\cdots

(4.3)

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{}^{I}\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}\\
l(w)=1\end{subarray}}\overline{M}_{I}(-w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\rightarrow\overline{M}_{I}(-\underline{{\lambda}})\rightarrow\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow 0,

where
{}^{I}w
is the longest element in
{}^{I}\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}
. The differentials in (
4.3
) are specified in the end of
[
29
, Section 2]
in terms of some alternate sums of
\{i_{w_{\sigma}^{\prime},w_{\sigma}}\}_{w_{\sigma}^{\prime},w_{\sigma}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma},\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
.

We use the notation of Section
2.1
”General linear group
{\rm GL}_{n}
-II”. Let
I
be a subset of
\Delta_{n}(k)
. In our setting, we apply (
4.3
) to get the following
I\cup\Delta_{n}^{k}
-parabolic BGG-resolution of
\overline{L}(-\underline{{\lambda}})
:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(-{}^{I\cup\Delta_{n}^{k}}w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{}^{I}\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}\\
l(w)=l(^{I}w)-1\end{subarray}}\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(-w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\rightarrow\cdots

(4.4)

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{}^{I}\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}\\
l(w)=1\end{subarray}}\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(-w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\rightarrow\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(-\underline{{\lambda}})\rightarrow\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow 0,

Applying the exact functor
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}(-,\pi_{I})
, we get the following exact sequence,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{I}:0\leftarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,{}^{I}w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})

\displaystyle\leftarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{}^{I}\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}\\
l(w)=l(^{I}w)-1\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\leftarrow\cdots

(4.5)

\displaystyle\cdots\leftarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{}^{I}\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}\\
l(w)=1\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\leftarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{{\lambda}})\leftarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{{\lambda}})\leftarrow 0.

We are going to prove the acyclicity of the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic) Tits complexes. The locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) Tits complexes show that locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) generalized parabolic Steinberg representation is derived equivalent to a complex of (direct sum of) parabolically induced locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) principal series.

For
0\leq j\leq k-1-|I|
and
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, we define

\displaystyle C^{\infty}_{I,j}:=C^{\infty}_{I,j}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|K\backslash I|=r\end{subarray}}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

(4.6)

\displaystyle C^{{\rm an}}_{I,j}:=C^{{\rm an}}_{I,j}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|K\backslash I|=r\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).

The following proposition first gives the acyclicity of the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic Tits complexes. It follows from (
3.14
) and (
3.15
), together with the same combinatorial technique in
[
28
, Propostion 11]
(see also
[
33
, Section 2, Proposition 6]
).

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
and
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, we have a natural exact sequence of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representations of
G
:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{I,k-1-|I|}\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{I,k-2-|I|}\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{I,k-3-|I|}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{I,1}

(4.7)

\displaystyle\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{I,0}=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow 0.

Here the differentials
d_{K^{\prime},K}:i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
are defined as follows. If
K\nsubseteq K^{\prime}
,
d_{K^{\prime},K}=0
. If
K^{\prime}=\{k_{1}r<\cdots<k_{l}r\}
and
K^{\prime}=K\cup\{k_{i}r\}
, we put
d_{K^{\prime},K}=(-1)^{i}p_{K^{\prime},K}
, where
p_{K^{\prime},K}:i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is the injection defined in (
3.13
).

We also have a locally analytic analogy of the above situations. This is a generalization of
[
29
, Theorem 4.2]
and
[
15
, Theorem 2.6]
. We modify the proof of
[
29
, Theorem 4.2]
to our content.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
and
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, we have a natural exact sequence of locally analytic representations of
G
:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{I,k-1-|I|}\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{I,k-2-|I|}\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{I,k-3-|I|}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{I,1}

(4.8)

\displaystyle\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{I,0}={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow 0.

Here the differentials
d_{K^{\prime},K}:{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
are defined as follows. If
K\nsubseteq K^{\prime}
,
d_{K^{\prime},K}=0
. If
K^{\prime}=\{k_{1}r<\cdots<k_{l}r\}
and
K^{\prime}=K\cup\{k_{i}r\}
, we put
d_{K^{\prime},K}=(-1)^{i}p^{{\rm an}}_{K^{\prime},K}
, where
p^{{\rm an}}_{K^{\prime},K}:{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is the injection defined in (
4.1
).

Proof.

We prove (
4.8
) for
\underline{\lambda}=\underline{0}
at first. By the same combinatorial technique in
[
28
, Proposition 11]
(see also
[
33
, Section 2, Proposition 6]
), it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)\cap{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi)={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J}}^{G}(\pi),

(4.9)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)\cap\bigg({\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I_{1}}}^{G}(\pi)+\cdots+{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I_{m}}}^{G}(\pi)\bigg)={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)\cap{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I_{1}}}^{G}(\pi)+\cdots+{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)\cap{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I_{m}}}^{G}(\pi).

Proof of the first identity of (
4.9
).

By the exactness of the induction functor
\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup J}}(L)}^{G}
, it suffices to establish the identity
\Big({\mathbb{I}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)}\pi_{I}\Big)\cap\Big({\mathbb{I}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)}\pi_{J}\Big)=\pi_{I\cup J}
. It is clear that the right-hand side is contained in the left-hand. Let
\mathcal{X}
be a nonzero irreducible constituent of its cokernel. Then by
[
30
, Theorem]
, we see that
\mathcal{X}
has the form
\mathcal{F}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J}(L)}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}(L)}(\overline{L}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-s\cdot\underline{0})_{I\cup J},M)
, where
s\in S_{n}^{|\Sigma_{L}|}
satisfying
s\cdot\underline{0}\in X^{+}_{\Delta^{k}_{n}\cup K}
for some maximal
I\subseteq K\subseteq I\cup J
, and
M
is an irreducible constituent of
i^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)}\pi_{I}
. On the other hand,
\mathcal{X}
also has the form

\mathcal{F}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J}(L)}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}(L)}(\overline{L}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-s^{\prime}\cdot\underline{0})_{I\cup J},M^{\prime})
, where
s^{\prime}\in S_{n}^{|\Sigma_{L}|}
satisfying
s\cdot\underline{0}\in X^{+}_{\Delta^{k}_{n}\cup K^{\prime}}
for some maximal
J\subseteq K^{\prime}\subseteq I\cup J
, and
M^{\prime}
is an irreducible constituent of
i^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)}\pi_{J}
. By

[
7
, Corollary 2.7]
, we have
K=K^{\prime}
,
s=s^{\prime}
and
M^{\prime}\cong M
. Therefore, we deduce that
s=s^{\prime}\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K,\Sigma_{L}}\backslash\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J,\Sigma_{L}}]\cap[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K^{\prime},\Sigma_{L}}\backslash\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J,\Sigma_{L}}]=1
since
K^{\prime}\cup K=I\cup J
(since we have
I\subseteq K\subseteq I\cup J
and
J\subseteq K^{\prime}\subseteq I\cup J
). In this case, we see that
M=M^{\prime}
is an irreducible constituent of
\Big(i^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)}\pi_{I}\Big)
and
\Big(i^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)}\pi_{J}\Big)
. Then, by (
3.14
) and
[
4
, Proposition 1.5 (b)]
, we deduce
M\cong M^{\prime}=\pi_{I\cup J}
. This proves the first identity.

Proof of the second identity of (
4.9
).

We prove the second identity by using induction on
m
. We deduce from the first identity that the right-hand side of the second identity is equal to
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup I_{1}}}}^{G}(\pi)+\cdots+{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup I_{m}}}}^{G}(\pi)
. Put
\mathcal{V}={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I_{1}}}}^{G}(\pi)+\cdots+{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I_{m-1}}}}^{G}(\pi)
and
\mathcal{W}={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup I_{1}}}}^{G}(\pi)+\cdots+{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup I_{m-1}}}}^{G}(\pi)
. Then by induction, we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup I_{j}\cup I_{m}}}}^{G}(\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup I_{m}}}}^{G}(\pi)\bigoplus\mathcal{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup I_{m}}}}^{G}(\pi)+\mathcal{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)\cap\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I_{j}\cup I_{m}}}}^{G}(\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup I_{m}}}}^{G}(\pi)\bigoplus\big({\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)\cap\mathcal{V}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)\cap\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I_{j}}}}^{G}(\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

(4.10)

The result follows from the snake lemma.

For general
\underline{\lambda}
, we use the same route of the proof of
[
29
, Theorem, 4.2]
. The proof is by induction on the
|\Delta_{n}(k)|
. When
|\Delta_{n}(k)|=1
, the acyclicity of (
4.8
) is trivial. We assume that
|\Delta_{n}(k)|>1
. We denote

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}[k]

\displaystyle:=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{}^{I}\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}\\
l(w)=k\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}}),\text{\;and\;}\mathcal{I}_{I,d,l}:=\bigoplus\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|K\backslash I|=d\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})[l],

(4.11)

for short (recall that
\mathcal{I}_{I,d,0}=C^{{\rm an}}_{I,d}
). The complexes in (
4.5
), for various

, induce the following double complex (note that the vertical sequences are given by (

), for various

):

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{I,0,l(^{I}w)},}

where
t=l(^{I}w)-1
and
i_{0}=|\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I|
. To prove the commutativity of this diagram, the heart is to establish the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{w^{\prime},w}}
\scriptstyle{q_{\Delta^{k}_{n}\cup K^{\prime},\Delta^{k}_{n}\cup K}}
\textstyle{\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{w^{\prime},w}}
\textstyle{\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}(-w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{\Delta^{k}_{n}\cup K^{\prime},\Delta^{k}_{n}\cup K}}
\textstyle{\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}(-w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}}),}

(4.12)

for any
K^{\prime}\subset K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
with
|K^{\prime}|=|K|-1
. Note that
w^{\prime}\geq w
and
l(w^{\prime})=l(w)+1
, then there exists a

\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, such that
w^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}}=w_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for
{\sigma^{\prime}}\neq\sigma
and
w^{\prime}_{\sigma}\geq w_{\sigma}
with
l(w^{\prime}_{\sigma})=l(w_{\sigma})+1
.
We can get the commutativity of (
4.12
) by applying the proof of a similar diagram in
[
29
, Theorem 4.2, Page 662]
to such
\sigma
-component of (
4.12
). Applying the functor
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}(L)}
to (
4.12
), we have by
[
30
, Theorem (iii)]
a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}(L)}^{G}(q_{K^{\prime},K},\mathrm{incl.})}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}(L)}^{G}(i_{w^{\prime},w})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}}^{G}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}(L)}^{G}(i_{w^{\prime},w})}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}(L)}^{G(L)}(q_{K^{\prime},K},\mathrm{incl.})}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K^{\prime}}}}^{G}(\pi,w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\lambda})}

These commutative diagrams, for various
K^{\prime}\subset K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, imply the commutativity of the above double complex.

Now we establish (
4.8
). As in the proof of
[
29
, Theorem 4.2, Page 663]
, it suffices to prove that each row of this double complex is exact except the second row and the rightmost column. The first row is exact by Proposition
4.3
. It remains to show the exactness of the following sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I(w)}}^{G}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq K\subseteq{I(w)}\\
|K\backslash I|=1\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow 0

(4.13)

for each
w\in{}^{I}\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}
. Note that

{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\cong\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I(w)}}(L)}^{G}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I(w)}}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I(w)}}(L)}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}}.

Therefore, the exactness of induction functor
\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I(w)}}(L)}^{G}
implies that it suffices to establish the exactness of the sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I(w)}}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I(w)}}(L)}(\pi,

\displaystyle w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq K\subseteq{I(w)}\\
|K\backslash I|=1\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I(w)}}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I(w)}}(L)}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})

(4.14)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I(w)}}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I(w)}}(L)}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow v_{I,I(w)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow 0.

This can be done by induction on
I(w)\subsetneq\Delta_{n}(k)
(since
|I(w)\backslash\Delta_{n}^{k}|<|\Delta_{n}(k)|
, we can apply the induction hypothesis). This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

When
r=1
and
\pi=v_{1}^{\frac{1-n}{2}}
, we recover the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic and locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic Tits complexes given in
[
15
, Section 2.1.2]
.

4.4
The Jordan-Hölder series of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representation

Let
m(w^{\prime}:w):=[\overline{M}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{0}):\overline{L}(-w\cdot\underline{0})]

be the multiplicity of the simple highest weight module
\overline{L}(-w\cdot\underline{0})
in the Verma module
\overline{M}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{0})
. By
[
23
, Section 5.2, Corollary]
, we see that
m(w^{\prime},w)\neq 0
if and only if
w\geq w^{\prime}
. Let
\underline{{\lambda}}\in X^{+}_{\Delta_{n}}
be a dominant weight. We have
m(w^{\prime},w)=[\overline{M}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\lambda}):\overline{L}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda})]
(one can use the fact that the translation functor
T^{w_{0}\cdot\underline{\lambda}}_{w_{0}\cdot\underline{0}}
(see
[
23
, section 7]
) is exact and
T^{w_{0}\cdot\underline{\lambda}}_{w_{0}\cdot\underline{0}}\overline{M}(-ww_{0}\cdot\underline{0})=\overline{M}(-ww_{0}\cdot\underline{\lambda})
, and
T^{w_{0}\cdot\underline{\lambda}}_{w_{0}\cdot\underline{0}}\overline{L}(-ww_{0}\cdot\underline{0})=\overline{L}(-ww_{0}\cdot\underline{\lambda})
). In particular,
[\overline{M}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\lambda}):\overline{L}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda})]\neq 0
if and only if
w\geq w^{\prime}
. The multiplicity
m(w^{\prime},w)
can be computed by Kazhdan-Lustzig polynomials.

Let
S
be a fixed subset of
\Delta_{n}(k)
. Recall that
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{S}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representation associated to
S
. By
[
30
, The main theorem]
, we see that the representations
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I(w)}}(\overline{L}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda}),v_{J,I(w)\backslash\Delta_{n}^{k}}^{\infty}(\pi))
where
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}
is an element such that
w\cdot\underline{\lambda}\in X^{+}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup{S}}
(hence
\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup S\subseteq I(w)
) and
J
is a subset of
I(w)\backslash\Delta_{n}^{k}
are topologically irreducible. All the Jordan Hölder factors of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{S}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
are obtained by this way. Denote by
m(w,J,S)
the
multiplicity of
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I(w)}}(\overline{L}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda}),v_{J,I(w)\backslash\Delta_{n}^{k}}^{\infty}(\pi))
in the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representation
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{S}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

The following proposition is a slight generalization of
[
29
, Theorem 4.6]
.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
w
be an element in the Weyl group
\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}
such that
I(w)=\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I
for some subset
S\subset I\subset{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. Then for any subset
J\subset I
, the multiplicity
m(w,J,S)
is given by

m(w,J,S)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}\\
\mathrm{supp}(w^{\prime})\backslash\Delta_{n}^{k}=J\backslash S\end{subarray}}(-1)^{l(w^{\prime})+|J\backslash S|}\big[{\overline{M}}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\lambda}):{\overline{L}}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\big].

(4.15)

Proof.

By the exact sequence (
4.8
) in the Proposition
4.4
, we get the following formula for the multiplicity

m(w,J,S)=\sum_{S\subseteq K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}}(-1)^{|K|}\big[{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\widetilde{I}}}}(\overline{L}(w\cdot\underline{\lambda}),v_{J,I}^{\infty}(\pi))\big].

By definition, we have

{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\cong\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}\big(\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(-\underline{\lambda}),{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big).

The non-vanishing of the expression
[{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}(\overline{L}(w\cdot\underline{\lambda}),v_{J,I}^{\infty}(\pi))]
implies that
K\subset I
is a necessary condition. On the other hand,
\mathcal{Y}=v_{J,I}^{\infty}(\pi)
is an irreducible constituent of
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
if and only if
K\subset J
. Therefore, by properties of the Orlik-Strauch functor (see Section
4.1
) and
[
7
, Corollary 2.7]
, we get that
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}\big(\overline{L}(w\cdot\underline{\lambda}),v_{J,I}^{\infty}(\pi)\big)
appears with multiplicity one in a Jordan-Hölder series of
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}\Big(\overline{L}(w\cdot\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)
. This shows that

\big[{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}(\overline{L}(w\cdot\underline{\lambda}),v_{J,I}^{\infty}(\pi))\big]=\big[\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(-\underline{\lambda}):{\overline{L}}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\big].\;

We now apply the character formula (see
[
23
, Section 9.6, Proposition]
) to
\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{K}(-\underline{\lambda})
, i.e.,

\mathrm{ch}\;{\overline{M}}_{{K}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda})=\sum_{w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup K,\Sigma_{L}}}(-1)^{l(w^{\prime})}\mathrm{ch}\;{\overline{M}}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\lambda}).

We obtain

\displaystyle\big[v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{S}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}({\overline{L}}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda}),v_{J,I}^{\infty}(\pi))\big]

(4.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{S\subseteq K\subseteq J}(-1)^{|K|}\big[{\overline{M}}_{K}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda}):{\overline{L}}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{S\subseteq K\subseteq J}(-1)^{|K|}\sum_{w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup K,\Sigma_{L}}}(-1)^{l(w^{\prime})}\big[{\overline{M}}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\lambda}):{\overline{L}}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}}(-1)^{l(w^{\prime})}\big[{\overline{M}}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\lambda}):{\overline{L}}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\big]\sum_{\mathrm{supp}(w^{\prime})\cup S\subseteq\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup{K}\subseteq\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup{J}}(-1)^{|K|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}\\
\mathrm{supp}(w^{\prime})\backslash\Delta_{n}^{k}=J\backslash S\end{subarray}}(-1)^{l(w^{\prime})+|J\backslash S|}\big[{\overline{M}}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\lambda}):{\overline{L}}(-w\cdot\underline{\lambda})\big].

This completes the proof.
∎

Example 4.7
.

Suppose
n=4
and
(r,k)=(2,2)
and
L={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. By
[
20
, Theorem 8.4 (iii)]
, the irreducible constituents of the generalized Verma module
\overline{M}^{\langle 2\rangle}(0)
are
\{\overline{L}(0),\overline{L}(-s_{2}\cdot 0),\overline{L}(-s_{2}s_{3}s_{1}s_{2}\cdot 0)\}
. On the other hand, we have

\{w\in\mathscr{W}_{4}:I(w)=\Delta_{4}^{2}=\{1,3\}\}=\{1,s_{2},s_{3}s_{2},s_{1}s_{2},s_{1}s_{3}s_{2},s_{2}s_{3}s_{1}s_{2}\}.

(4.17)

Then Proposition
4.6
implies
m(w,\emptyset,\emptyset)=1
for
w\in\{1,s_{2},s_{2}s_{3}s_{1}s_{2}\}
and
m(w,\emptyset,\emptyset)=0
for
w\in\{s_{3}s_{2},s_{1}s_{2},s_{1}s_{3}s_{2}\}
.

Remark 4.8
.

Let us remark that it is hard to determine if the multiplicity (
4.15
) is non-zero, due to the more complicated structure of the generalized (parabolic) Verma modules. Some questions arise in the parabolic Verma modules, which remain open except in some special cases. For a survey of this topic one can refer to
[
23
]
. Therefore, the description of composition factors of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations are more
subtle.

5
Extensions between locally analytic representations

This section gives the main result of this paper. We study the extension groups of certain locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations. In particular, we see that, for any
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
and
\underline{\lambda}\in X^{+}_{\Delta_{n}}
, the extensions of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
can be parameterized by
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
(see Theorem
5.19
).

Section
5.1
and Section
5.2
list some basic facts about smooth and locally analytic extension groups. As a Preliminary, we first compute smooth extensions between smooth generalized parabolic Steinberg representations in Appendix, Section
6.4
. These results are not new, and we include proofs only for the reader’s convenience. In Section
5.4
, we prove the first main Theorem
5.19
. The key technical ingredient is to show that the morphism (
5.41
) is an isomorphism. In Section
5.5
, we study certain subrepresentations
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
and
\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
(see (
5.54
)) of
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. We further prove that

{\rm soc}_{G}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\cong{\rm soc}_{G}\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\cong{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
(it might be true that
{\rm soc}_{G}{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\cong{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, but the author does not know how to prove it). We show that the extensions of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\underline{\lambda})
actually come by push-forward from the extensions of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
and
\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

5.1
Computation of
{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{\infty}(-,E)
and
{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}(-,E)

For a uniformizer
\varpi_{L}
one gets a character
\varepsilon_{L}:L^{\times}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}
which is identity on
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}
and sends
\varpi_{L}
to
1
.  Let
\psi_{\sigma,L}:=\sigma\circ\log\circ\varepsilon_{L}:L^{\times}\rightarrow E
for
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, and
\psi_{{\rm ur}}:L^{\times}\rightarrow E
be the unramified character sending
p
to
1
(thus sending
\varpi
to
e^{-1}
). Then by
[
14
, Lemma 1.14]
,
\{\psi_{\sigma,L}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
and
\psi_{{\rm ur}}
form a basis of
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
. In particular, the
E
-dimension of
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
is
d_{L}+1
. In particular,
{\rm Hom}_{\infty}(L^{\times},E)
is the
1
-dimensional subspace of
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
generated by
\psi_{{\rm ur}}
.

Let
X_{L}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}_{I})
denote the group of
L
-algebraic characters of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}
, and let
X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}):=X_{L}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}_{I})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}E
. Recall that
{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}
is the center of
{\rm GL}_{n}
. We put
Z_{n}={\mathbf{Z}}_{n}(L)
. By
[
28
, Proposition 9]
, we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L),E)\cong\bigwedge^{i}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}),\hskip 20.0pt{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)/Z_{n},E)\cong\bigwedge^{i}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}).

Let
I
be a subset of
\Delta_{n}
. Recall that
{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L),E\big)

(
resp.,
{\rm Hom}_{\infty}\big({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L),E\big)
)
denote the set consisting of
E
-valued continuous additive characters (resp., smooth additive characters) of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)
. By applying Lemma
2.3
to the case
V=W=1
and using
[
28
, Proposition 9]
, we get an injection
j_{I}:X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}_{I})\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L),E\big)
with image
{\rm Hom}_{\infty}\big({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L),E\big)
. This injection is given as follows. For any
\alpha\in X_{L}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}_{I})
, it induces a homomorphism on the
L
-points
\alpha(L):{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)\rightarrow L^{\times}
. Then
j_{I}
sends
\alpha
to
|\alpha(L)|_{L}
.

By
[
40
, (3.28), Corollary 3.14]
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L),E)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{0\leq t\leq i}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{t}{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{I}(L),E)\big)\otimes_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{i-t}\big({\mathfrak{d}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}},E\big)\Big),

(5.1)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)/Z_{n},E)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{0\leq t\leq i}\Big(\big(\bigwedge^{t}{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{I}(L)/Z_{n},E\big)\big)\otimes_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{i-t}\big(\overline{{\mathfrak{d}}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}},E\big)\Big).

Since
{\mathfrak{d}}_{I}
is semi-simple, we have
{\mathrm{H}}^{r}\big({\mathfrak{d}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}},E\big)=0
for
r=1,2
. In particular, we deduce natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}\big({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L),E\big)\cong\wedge^{i}{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{I}(L),E\big),{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)/Z_{n},E)\cong\wedge^{i}{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{I}(L)/Z_{n},E)

for
i=1,2
. On the other hand, by
[
25
, Corollary 8.10]
(since
{\mathbf{Z}}_{I}
is a torus, we apply it to the group
{\rm Res}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{Z}}_{I}
), one has

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{I}(L),E)\cong\bigoplus_{t+s=i}\Big(\bigwedge^{t}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}}_{I})\Big)\otimes_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{s}({{\mathfrak{z}}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}},E)

(5.2)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{I}(L)/Z_{n},E)\cong\bigoplus_{t+s=i}\Big(\bigwedge^{t}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}}_{I}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})\Big)\otimes_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{s}(\overline{{\mathfrak{z}}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}},E),

for all
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
. Combining (
5.1
), (
5.2
) with the Künneth formula for Lie algebras (see
[
40
, (3.24)]
), we deduce other expressions of
{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{I}(L),E)
and
{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{I}(L)/Z_{n},E)
.

Lemma 5.1
.

For
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L),E)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{0\leq t\leq i}\Big(\bigwedge^{t}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}}_{I})\Big)\otimes_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{i-t}({{\mathfrak{l}}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}},E),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)/Z_{n},E)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{0\leq t\leq i}\Big(\bigwedge^{t}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}}_{I}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})\Big)\otimes_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{i-t}(\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}},E).

5.2
More on smooth and locally analytic extension groups

The following lemma holds for any connected reductive algebraic group
{\mathbf{G}}
over
L
.

Lemma 5.2
.

(Frobenius reciprocity, see
[
28
, Lemma 8]
). Let
{\mathbf{P}}
be a parabolic subgroup
of
{\mathbf{G}}
with Levi factorization
{\mathbf{P}}={\mathbf{L}}_{{\mathbf{P}}}{\mathbf{N}}_{{\mathbf{P}}}
. Let
\pi
be a smooth representation
of
{\mathbf{G}}(L)
over
E
, and let
\sigma
be a smooth representation of
{\mathbf{L}}_{{\mathbf{P}}}(L)
over
E
. Then

{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{G}}(L)}(\pi,i^{{\mathbf{G}}(L)}_{{{\mathbf{P}}}(L)}\sigma)\cong{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{{\mathbf{P}}}(L)}(r^{{\mathbf{G}}(L)}_{{{\mathbf{P}}}(L)}\pi,\sigma)\text{ for all }i\geq 0.

In particular, the Frobenius reciprocity for
i=0
recovers adjunction property (
2.5
).

The next lemma lists some exact sequences arising from spectral sequences.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
E
be a biregular spectral sequence.

(a)

Suppose
E_{2}^{p,q}=0
when
p<0
, or
q<0
, or
0<q<m
. Then
E_{2}^{i,2}\cong H^{i}
for any
i<m
and we have an exact sequence

0\rightarrow E_{2}^{m,0}\rightarrow H^{m}\rightarrow E_{2}^{m-1,1}\rightarrow E_{2}^{m+1,0}\rightarrow H^{m+1}.

(b)

Suppose
E_{2}^{p,q}=0
for any
p\neq p_{1},p_{2}
and
p_{2}-p_{1}=1
, then we have a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow E_{2}^{p_{2},m-p_{2}}\rightarrow H^{m}\rightarrow E_{2}^{p_{1},m-p_{1}}\rightarrow 0.

(c)

Suppose
E_{2}^{p,q}=0
for any
q\neq q_{1},q_{2}
(
q_{1}<q_{2}
) , then we have a long exact sequence

\cdots\rightarrow E_{2}^{m-q_{1},q_{1}}\rightarrow H^{m}\rightarrow E_{2}^{m-q_{2},q_{2}}\rightarrow E_{2}^{m+1-q_{1},q_{1}}\rightarrow H^{m+1}\rightarrow\cdots.

Remark 5.4
.

(Yoneda extensions)

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an abelian category with enough injectives, then we can define the extension groups
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{\mathcal{C}}(A,B)
for all
r\geq 0
and
A,B\in\mathcal{C}
, by choosing an injective resolution of
B
. On the other hand, a Yoneda
i
-extension of
A
by
B
is an exact sequence starting at
B
and ending at
A
,

X:0\rightarrow B\rightarrow X_{i}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow X_{1}\rightarrow A\rightarrow 0.

Two Yoneda
i
-extensions
X,Y
are equivalent if there exists a commutative diagram:
