148.00632pt\raise-31.44446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 191.04105pt\raise-31.44446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 191.04105pt\raise-31.44446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 228.54105pt\raise-31.44446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 228.54105pt\raise-31.44446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The Yoneda
i
-extension group
\mathrm{Yext}^{i}_{\mathcal{C}}(A,B)
is given by the Yoneda
i
-extensions
A
by
B
modulo the above equivalence relation
\equiv
. By the arguments before
[
39
, Corollary 0.2]
, there is a natural equivalence between the two bi-functors
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{\mathcal{C}}(A,B)
and
\mathrm{Yext}^{i}_{\mathcal{C}}(A,B)
.

Let
\eta^{\infty}
be a smooth character of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
over
E
. Recall that the categories
\textbf{Rep}_{E}^{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
and
\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z_{n}=\eta^{\infty}}^{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
have enough injectives. For
V,W\in\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z_{n}=\eta^{\infty}}^{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
, there are then natural morphism

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta^{\infty}}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(V,W),

induced by Yoneda
i
-extensions. In general, it is not clear whether these homomorphisms are injective or surjective. When
i=1
, this homomorphism is injective since a short exact sequence splits in the category
\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z_{n}=\eta^{\infty}}^{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
if and only if it splits in
\textbf{Rep}_{E}^{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
.

Let
V,W,U,U^{\prime}\in\textbf{Rep}_{E}^{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))

(
resp.,
V^{\prime},W^{\prime}\in\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z_{n}=\eta^{\infty}}^{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
)
. We assume that
Z_{n}
acts on representation
U^{\prime}
via a smooth character
\omega_{U^{\prime}}^{\infty}
. There are natural morphisms

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(V\otimes_{E}U,W\otimes_{E}U),

(5.3)

\displaystyle\mathrm{resp.,}{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta^{\infty}}(V^{\prime},W^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta^{\infty}{\otimes}_{E}\omega_{U^{\prime}}^{\infty}}(V^{\prime}{\otimes}_{E}U^{\prime},W^{\prime}{\otimes}_{E}U^{\prime}),

induced by tensoring the Yoneda
i
-extensions in
{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(V,W)

(
resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),\eta}(V^{\prime},W^{\prime})
)
with
U
(resp.,
U^{\prime}
).

Let
\eta^{\infty}
be a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic character of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
over
E
. Unlike the smooth case, it is not clear that the categories
\mathcal{M}(H)
and
\mathcal{M}_{Z^{\prime},\eta}(H)
have enough injectives. Let
V,W\in\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z_{n}=\eta}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
be locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations, we still have a natural morphism

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta}(V^{\prime},W^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(V^{\prime},W^{\prime}),\forall V^{\prime},W^{\prime}\in\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z_{n}=\eta}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))

by choosing a projective resolution of
V^{\prime}
in the category
\mathcal{M}_{Z_{n},\eta}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
(it remains a projective resolution of
V^{\prime}
in the category
\mathcal{M}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
). In general, it is not clear whether these homomorphisms are injective or surjective. If
i=1
and
V,W
are admissible, then by the argument after
[
15
, Lemma 2.2]
(or see the argument before Remark
2.1
), this morphism is injective since a short exact sequence splits in
\mathcal{M}_{Z_{n},\eta}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
if and only if it splits in
\mathcal{M}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
.

Suppose that the
V,W,U,U^{\prime}\in\textbf{Rep}_{E}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
(resp.,
V^{\prime},W^{\prime}\in\textbf{Rep}_{E,Z_{n}=\eta}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))
) are admissible locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations over locally convex Hausdorff
E
-vector spaces of compact type. Assume that
Z_{n}
acts on representation
U^{\prime}
via character
\omega_{U^{\prime}}
. We have morphisms

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(V\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U,W\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U),

(5.4)

\displaystyle resp.,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta}(V^{\prime},W^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta\widehat{\otimes}_{E}\omega_{U^{\prime}}}(V^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U^{\prime},W^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U^{\prime})

by sending
f\mapsto f\otimes_{E}\mathrm{id}_{U}
(resp.,
f\mapsto f\otimes_{E}\mathrm{id}_{U^{\prime}}
). If
U
(resp.,
U^{\prime}
) is a finite-dimensional locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
, then by
[
19
, Proposition 6.1.5]
, we get that
V\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U,W\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U
(resp.,
V^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U^{\prime},W^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U^{\prime}
) are again admissible locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
(with the diagonal action). Therefore, we have the following morphisms:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(V,W)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(V\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U,W\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U),

(5.5)

\displaystyle\text{resp.},{\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta}(V^{\prime},W^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta\widehat{\otimes}_{E}\omega_{U^{\prime}}}(V^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U^{\prime},W^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}U^{\prime}).\;

by tensoring
U
(resp.,
U^{\prime}
) with the
1
-th extensions.

In (
3.8
), we define the irreducible cuspidal smooth representation
{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
over
E
. Let
\omega_{\pi}
be the central character of
\pi
. Then the central character of
{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
is
\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}:=\omega_{\pi}v_{r}^{k-1}\otimes_{E}\cdots\otimes_{E}\omega_{\pi}
.
In particular,
Z_{n}
acts on
{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
via the character
\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}|_{Z_{n}}
. For any subset
I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, the center
Z_{n}
also acts on representations
\pi_{I}\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{{I}}
,
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
via
\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}
. In the sequel, we put

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(-,-):={\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(-,-),\hskip 120.0pt

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),\eta}(-,-):={\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=\eta}(-,-),\text{for }\eta\in\{\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}\},

for all
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.

5.3
Bruhat filtrations

In this section, we talk about the Bruhat filtrations of parabolically induced representations and describe the associated graded representations.

Let
I,J
be two subsets of
\Delta_{n}(k)
. Induced by the relative Bruhat decomposition

G=\coprod_{w\in[\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}]}{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{I}}(L)w{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{J}}(L),

(5.6)

we define an increasing filtration
\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}_{B}
on
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)
by
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)
invariant subspaces:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{h}_{B}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)=\bigg\{f\in i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi),\mathrm{supp}(f)\in\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}]\\
\mathrm{lg}(w)\leq h\end{subarray}}{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\backslash{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)w{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\bigg\},

(5.7)

for all
h\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
. By
[
12
, Proposition 6.3.1]
(or a corollary of it), there are canonical isomorphisms

{\rm gr}^{h}_{\mathcal{F}_{B}^{\bullet}}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi))\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}]\\
\mathrm{lg}(w)=h\end{subarray}}c\text{-}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}\pi_{I}^{w}

of smooth
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)
-representations for all
h\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, where
c\text{-}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}\pi_{I}^{w}
is the smooth induction with compact support. By
[
12
, Proposition 6.3.3]
, we have isomorphism

\displaystyle r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}({\rm gr}^{h}_{\mathcal{F}_{B}^{\bullet}}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi)))\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}]\\
\mathrm{lg}(w)=h\end{subarray}}i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\big(\gamma_{I,J}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\big),

(5.8)

of smooth
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)
-representations for all
h\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

For
w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}]
, the representation
r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}
is non-zero unless
w\in\mathscr{W}_{I,J}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
.

Proof.

For each
w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}]\subset[\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}]
, we have injections

\displaystyle r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\hookrightarrow r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}i^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)^{w}

(5.9)

of smooth representations of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)
. By
[
12
, Corollary 6.3.4 (b), Theorem 6.3.5]
, we see that

\displaystyle\Big(r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}i^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\Big)^{\mathrm{ss}}

\displaystyle\cong

(5.10)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{u\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cap wJ}\backslash\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle i}_{({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(L)^{w})\cap({\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L))^{wu}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}

\displaystyle\gamma_{I,J}^{w,u}\otimes_{E}r_{({\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w})\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{wu}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{wu}}\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)^{wu}

By
[
11
, Corollary 2.8.8]
(or see
[
12
, Corollary 6.3.4]
and its proof), we see that the smooth representation
r_{({\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w})\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{wu}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{wu}}\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)^{wu}
is non-zero unless
u^{-1}w^{-1}(\Delta_{n}(k))\subset(\Delta_{n}(k)\cup J)\cap w^{-1}(\Delta_{n}(k)\cup I)\subset\Delta_{n}(k)\cup J
. We deduce from Lemma
3.1
that
wu\in\mathscr{W}_{\emptyset,J}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
. Then we get that
u^{-1}w^{-1}(\Delta_{n}(k))=\Delta_{n}(k)
and
\Delta_{n}(k)\subset w^{-1}(\Delta_{n}(k)\cup I)
, we see that
w\in\mathscr{W}_{I,\emptyset}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
by using Lemma
3.1
again. We thus have
w\in\mathscr{W}_{\emptyset,J}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cap\mathscr{W}_{I,\emptyset}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})=\mathscr{W}_{I,J}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
.
This completes the proof.
∎

5.4
Extensions between locally analytic representations-I

We study the extension groups of certain locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representations. This section follows a technical modification of the route of
[
15
, Section 2.2]
and Section
6.4
.

For any
J\subset{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, and
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, we put

\mathcal{E}_{J}^{j}:={\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}(\pi_{J},\pi_{J}),\text{and }{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{j}:={\rm Ext}^{i}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi_{J},\pi_{J}).

By Section
5.2
, there are natural morphisms for
i\in\{0,1\}

\iota_{J}^{i}:\mathrm{H}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{J}^{i},(\text{resp.\;}\overline{\iota}_{J}^{i}:\mathrm{H}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n},E)\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{i}).\;

(5.11)

More precisely, the map
\iota_{J}^{0}
(resp.,
\overline{\iota}_{J}^{0}
) is induced by tensoring

\Psi_{0}\in{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}(1,1)(\text{resp., }\Psi_{0}\in{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(1,1))

with the identity homomorphism
\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{J}}:\pi_{J}\rightarrow\pi_{J}
. On the other hand, the map
\iota_{J}^{1}
(resp.,
\overline{\iota}_{J}^{1}
) is induced by mapping

\Psi\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)

(resp.,
\Psi\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n},E)\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n},E)
) to an
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
-valued representation

[\pi_{J}(1+\Psi\epsilon)]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}(\pi_{J},\pi_{J})(\text{resp., }[\pi_{J}(1+\Psi\epsilon)]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi_{J},\pi_{J})).\;

Lemma 5.6
.

For any
J\subset{\Delta_{n}(k)}
and
i\in\{0,1\}
, the maps
\iota_{J}^{i}
and
\overline{\iota}_{J}^{i}
are injective.

Proof.

The assertion for
i=0
is clear. The natural injection
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)
induces a restriction map

\displaystyle c_{J}:\mathcal{E}_{J}^{i}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}(\omega_{\pi_{J}},\omega_{\pi_{J}})\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}(1,1),

\displaystyle\text{resp., }

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{J}:{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{i}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\omega_{\pi_{J}},\omega_{\pi_{J}})\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}}(1,1).

It is clear that the map
c_{J}
(resp.,
\overline{c}_{J}
) gives a section of
\iota_{J}^{1}
(resp.,
\overline{\iota}_{J}^{1}
). The results follow.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

For
J\in\{\Delta_{n}(k),{\Delta}_{k,j}\}
and
i\in\{0,1\}
, we have the following isomorphisms of
E
-vector spaces:

\displaystyle\iota_{\Delta_{n}(k)}^{i}:\mathrm{H}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}^{i},\hskip 220.0pt

(5.12)

\displaystyle\overline{\iota}_{J}^{i}:\mathrm{H}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{i}.\;

Proof.

By Lemma
2.3
, we have spectral sequences

\displaystyle E^{r,s}_{2,I}

\displaystyle={\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L))}(\pi_{J}^{\vee},{\mathrm{H}}^{s}(\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}}_{{J},\Sigma_{L}},E)\otimes_{E}\pi_{J}^{\vee})\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}(\pi_{J},\pi_{J}),

(5.13)

\displaystyle E^{r,s}_{2,II}

\displaystyle={\rm Ext}^{r}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}((\pi_{J}\otimes\omega_{\pi}^{-1})^{\vee},{\mathrm{H}}^{s}(\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}}_{{J},\Sigma_{L}},E)\otimes_{E}(\pi_{J}\otimes\omega_{\pi}^{-1})^{\vee})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi_{J},\pi_{J}).

For any
J\in\{\Delta_{n}(k),{\Delta}_{k,j}\}
, it follows from Lemma
6.6
that the term
E^{r,s}_{2,II}=0
is zero unless
r\in\{0,1\}
. Therefore, Lemma
5.3
(b) implies the following exact sequence

0\rightarrow X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})\otimes_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{i-1}(\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}}_{{J},\Sigma_{L}},E)\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{i}\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{i}(\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}}_{{J},\Sigma_{L}},E)\rightarrow 0.

Then for each
i\in\{0,1\}
, we deduce the second isomorphism
\overline{\iota}_{J}^{i}
in (
5.12
) from Lemma
5.1
and Lemma
5.6
.

For
J=\Delta_{n}(k)
, the term
E^{r,s}_{2,I}=0
is zero unless
r=0,1
by Lemma
6.6
. Therefore, the Lemma
5.3
(b) implies the following exact sequence

0\rightarrow X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)})\otimes_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{i-1}({{\mathfrak{l}}}_{{\Delta_{n}(k)},\Sigma_{L}},E)\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}^{i}\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{i}({{\mathfrak{l}}}_{{\Delta_{n}(k)},\Sigma_{L}},E)\rightarrow 0.

Then Lemma
5.1
and Lemma
5.6
show that the first map
{\iota}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}^{i}
in (
5.12
) also induces an isomorphism for each
i\in\{0,1\}
.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

In general, the higher extension groups
\mathcal{E}_{J}^{j}
and
{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{j}
are much harder to calculate than
{\mathcal{S}}_{I}^{i}
and
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{I}^{i}
. The Lemma
2.3
and the spectral sequence argument in Lemma
5.1
are of not help here.

Lemma 5.9
.

We have

{\rm Ext}^{i}_{G}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}\big(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{E}_{J}^{i},&\hbox{$J\subseteq I$;}\\
0,&\hbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(5.14)

and

{\rm Ext}^{i}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{i},&\hbox{$J\subseteq I$;}\\
0,&\hbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(5.15)

Proof.

We modify the proof of
[
15
, Proposition 2.11]
to our case. We prove (
5.15
) only and (
5.14
) follows by the same strategy. By
[
40
, Corollary 4.9]
(note that we need to check that
\mathrm{H}_{s}({{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))
satisfies the separated assumption of
[
40
, Corollary 4.9]
. This is true since
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic), we have a spectral sequence

\displaystyle E_{2}^{r,s}={\rm Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\Big(\mathrm{H}_{s}\big({{\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big),

\displaystyle\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\Big)\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s}_{G,\chi(\underline{\lambda})}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}\Big(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big).

(5.16)

One has isomorphisms of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representations of
{\rm Res}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{L}{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}_{s}\big({\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J},i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

\displaystyle=r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi))\otimes_{E}\mathrm{H}_{s}(\overline{\mathfrak{n}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J,\Sigma_{L}},L(\underline{\lambda}))

(5.17)

\displaystyle=r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi))\otimes_{E}\Big(\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathscr{W}_{n},{\rm lg}(w)=s\\
w\cdot\underline{\lambda}\in X^{+}_{{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup J}}\end{subarray}}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(w\cdot\underline{\lambda})_{{J}}\Big),

by
[
40
, Theorem 4.10]
. Therefore, by
[
40
, Proposition 4.7(1)]
, only the terms
E_{2}^{r,s}
with
s=0
can be nonzero. Consequently, for all
r\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{r}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\Big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\Big(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi))\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{J},\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda}_{J})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)),\pi_{J}\otimes_{E}\mathrm{End}_{E}(L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{{J}})\Big),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)),\pi_{J}\Big),

where the last isomorphism follows from the fact that the algebraic representation
\mathrm{End}_{E}(L(\underline{\lambda})_{J})
is semisimple, in which the trivial representation has multiplicity
1
. By Lemma
2.3
, we have a spectral sequence

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{r}_{{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{Z,\chi}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L))}}\Big(\pi_{J}^{\vee},\mathrm{H}^{s}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J},\Sigma_{L}},E)\otimes_{E}\big(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi))\big)^{\vee}\Big)\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi),\pi_{J}).

We recall the Bruhat filtration
\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}_{B}
(
5.7
) on
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)
. It satisfies

r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}\Big({\rm gr}^{k}_{\mathcal{F}_{B}^{\bullet}}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi))\Big)\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}]\\
\mathrm{lg}(w)=k\end{subarray}}i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\big(\gamma_{I,J}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\big)

for each
k\geq 0
. We are going to show that

{\rm Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\Big(\gamma_{I,J}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\Big),\pi_{J}\Big)=0

for any element
1\neq w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}]
or
J\nsubseteq I
. This is a direct consequence of the following spectral sequence (see Lemma
2.3
)

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{r}_{{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{Z,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L))}}

\displaystyle\Big(\pi_{J}^{\vee,\infty},\mathrm{H}^{s}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J},\Sigma_{L}},E)\otimes_{E}\Big(i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\big(\gamma_{I,J}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\big)\Big)^{\vee}\Big)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{r+s}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\gamma_{I,J}^{w}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w},\pi_{J}\Big),

and (
5.20
). It suffices to show that

{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\big(\gamma_{I,J}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\big),\pi_{J}\Big)=0

(5.18)

for
J\nsubseteq I
or
w\neq 1
. By Lemma
5.5
, we can assume that

w\in\mathscr{W}_{I,J}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
. In this case, we see that

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\big(\gamma_{I,J}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\big),\pi_{J}\Big)

(5.19)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(\pi_{J}^{\sim},i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\big(\gamma_{I,J}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\big)^{\sim}\delta_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}\Big)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J\cap w^{-1}I}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(r_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\pi_{J}^{\sim},\big(\gamma_{I,J}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\big)^{\sim}\delta_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J\cap w^{-1}I}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(A,B),

where the first isomorphism follows from
[
28
, Lemma 14]
(note that
W^{\sim}
denotes the smooth dual of
W
, if
W
is a smooth representation), and the second isomorphism follows from Lemma
5.2
. If
J\nsubseteq I
or
w\neq 1
, by the Proof of
[
28
, Proposition 15, especially (2) and (3), & Lemma 16]
, there exists an element
z\in{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J\cap w^{-1}I}(L)
such that
(\gamma_{I,J}^{w})^{\vee}(z)\delta_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}(z)\neq 1.\;
Therefore, we deduce from (
3.7
) that
z
acts on
B
(resp,
A
) by multiplication by scalar

z_{B}:=(\pi_{J\cap w^{-1}I}\otimes_{E}\gamma_{I,J}^{w})^{\sim}(z)\delta_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}(z),(\mathrm{resp.}z_{A}:=(\pi_{J\cap w^{-1}I})^{\sim}(z)).\;

Since
z_{A}\neq z_{B}
, we deduce from Lemma
2.2
that

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\big(\gamma_{I,J}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\big),\pi_{J}\Big)=0

(5.20)

for
J\nsubseteq I
or
w\neq 1
. If
J\nsubseteq I
, we deduce by dévissage that
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{G}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))=0
. If
J\subseteq I
, we have

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi)),\pi_{J}\Big)={\rm Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}\big(\mathcal{F}^{0}_{B}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi)),\pi_{J}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big({r}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\pi_{J},\pi_{J}\Big)={\rm Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big(\pi_{J},\pi_{J}\big).\;

This completes the proof of (
5.15
).
∎

As a corollary of Lemma
5.9
, we have

Corollary 5.10
.

We have

{\rm Ext}^{i}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{E}_{J}^{i-|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|},&\hbox{$I\cup J={\Delta_{n}(k)}$;}\\
0,&\hbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(5.21)

{\rm Ext}^{i}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{i-|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|},&\hbox{$I\cup J={\Delta_{n}(k)}$;}\\
0,&\hbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(5.22)

Proof.

We prove (
5.22
) only, and (
5.21
) follows by the same arguments. We apply the acyclic complex in Proposition
4.3
to the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, i.e.,

\Big[C^{\infty}_{I,k-1-|I|}{\longrightarrow}C^{\infty}_{I,k-2-|I|}{\longrightarrow}\cdots{\longrightarrow}C^{\infty}_{I,0}\Big],\text{with }C^{\infty}_{I,l}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|K\backslash I|=l\end{subarray}}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).

We recall the discussion after
[
40
, Proposition 5.4]
briefly. By
[
25
, (25)]
, we have a resolution
(C^{{\rm an}}(G^{q+1},C^{\infty}_{I,l}))_{q\geq 0}
for each term
C^{\infty}_{I,l}
. Then we form a double complex
(C^{q,l})_{q,l}
with
C^{q,l}:=C^{{\rm an}}(G^{q+1},C^{\infty}_{I,-l})
. Then the extension group in this corollary is equal to the cohomology of the total complex associated to the
({\rm Hom}_{G}(C^{q,l},{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})))_{q,l}
, which leads to a spectral sequence

E_{1}^{s,r}={\rm Ext}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}^{r}\Big(\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|K\backslash I|=s\end{subarray}}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{{\lambda}})\Big)\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{{\lambda}}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{{\lambda}})\big).

By (
5.15
), for any
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, the
r
-th row of the
E_{1}
-page of the spectral sequence is given by

\begin{matrix}&E_{1}^{|I\cup J|-|I|,r}&{\longrightarrow}&E_{1}^{|I\cup J|-|I|+1,r}&{\longrightarrow}&\cdots&{\longrightarrow}&E_{1}^{n-1-|I|,r}\\
&\parallel&&\parallel&&&&\parallel\\
&\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{J}^{i}&{\longrightarrow}&\bigoplus\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\cup J\subseteq K,\\
|K\backslash(I\cup J)|=1\end{subarray}}\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{J}^{i}&{\longrightarrow}&\cdots&{\longrightarrow}&\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{J}^{i}.\end{matrix}

If
I\cup J\neq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, the above sequence is a constant coefficient system on the standard simplex associated to
I\cup J
, which is exact. This implies that
{\rm Ext}^{r}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=0
for all
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. On the other hand, if
I\cup J={\Delta_{n}(k)}
, all the elements of the
E_{1}
-page vanish except the elements on the
|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|
-th column, and hence

\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{J}^{i}\cong{\rm Ext}^{r}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\cong{\rm Ext}^{r+|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

This completes the proof.
∎

We now give an explicit description for the
{\rm Ext}^{1}
-groups in the Lemma
5.9
. Let
J\subseteq I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
be subsets of
\Delta_{n}(k)
. Let
\widetilde{\pi}_{J}
be a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)
over
E
, which is a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic extension of
\pi_{J}
by
\pi_{J}
. It gives a cohomology class
[\widetilde{\pi}_{J}]\in\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}
. Thus, the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction
\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\widetilde{\pi}_{J}\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{J}\big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}
lies in an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\longrightarrow\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\widetilde{\pi}_{J}\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda})_{{J}}\big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\longrightarrow 0.

(5.23)

Pullback (
5.23
) via a natural injection

i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

gives a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\widetilde{\pi}_{J})^{+}
, which is an extension of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. It gives a cohomology class
[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\widetilde{\pi}_{J})^{+}]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)
. By the argument in Section
5.1
, we have
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E)\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E)
(and the same for
J
). Then the natural injection
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
induces a natural morphism

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E)\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L),E)\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)

In particular, we will view
{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L),E)
as a subspace of
{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)
. Let

\Psi\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\rm an}}\big({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E\big)\cong{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E\big),

which induces an extension
\iota_{J}(\Psi)
of
\pi_{J}
of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)
:

\iota_{J}(\Psi)(a)=\pi_{J}(a)\otimes_{E}\begin{pmatrix}1&\Psi(a)\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\\
\forall~a\in{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L).

We may identify
\iota_{J}(\Psi)
with the
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
-valued representation
\pi_{J}(1+\Psi\epsilon)
. We put

\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\Psi)^{+}:=\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\iota_{J}(\Psi))^{+}.\;

Applying and modifying the proof of
[
15
, Lemma 2.13, Remark 2.18]
to our case, we can show that

Lemma 5.11
.

The representation
[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\widetilde{\pi}_{J})^{+}]
is mapped (up to nonzero scalars) to
\widetilde{\pi}_{J}\in\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}
via the isomorphism in (
5.14
).

Proof.

By the discussion in
[
40
, Section 4.4,(4.32)]
, we see that the isomorphism (
5.14
) factors though the following composition,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

(5.24)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\Big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))|_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)},\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\Big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\Big(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})),\pi_{J}\Big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\left(\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda}),\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\right),

where the first map sends
V
to the push-forward of
V
to the push-forward of
V|_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}
via the natural evaluation map
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})
,
f\mapsto f(1)
, the second map is inverse of the map induced by the natural projection
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))|_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\twoheadrightarrow r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))
, and the third map is induced by the injection
\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))
. Note that composition
5.24
is also the edge morphism given by the spectral sequence (
5.16
).

We next show that
[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\widetilde{\pi}_{J})^{+}]
is mapped to
\widetilde{\pi}_{J}
via (
5.24
). Similar to the discussion in
[
15
, Lemma 2.13]
, we can show that the following composition

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\left(\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda}),\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\right)

(5.25)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\Big({\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))|_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)},\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\Big)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\Big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))|_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)},\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\Big)

is the inverse of the composition of the last two maps in (

). By the construction of
[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\widetilde{\pi}_{J})^{+}]
, we see that the representation

is just the image of
\widetilde{\pi}_{J}
via the above composition and the inverse of the first isomorphism in (
5.24
). The lemma follows.
∎

Furthermore, the injection
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
induces a map

By the isomorphism (
5.14
), we get a map
\mathcal{E}_{I}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{J}
. We now describe this map explicitly. By
[
40
, Corollary 4.9]
, we have a spectral sequence

\displaystyle E_{2}^{r,s}={\rm Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}

\displaystyle\Big(\mathrm{H}_{s}\big({\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda})\big),\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\Big)\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\Big(\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big).

(5.26)

One has isomorphisms of locally algebraic representations of
{\rm Res}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{L}{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}_{s}\big({\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda})\big)=

\displaystyle r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}\pi_{I}\otimes_{E}\mathrm{H}_{s}(\overline{\mathfrak{n}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}},L(\underline{\lambda}))

(5.27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{J}\otimes_{E}\Big(\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathscr{W}_{n},{\rm lg}(w)=s\\
w\cdot\underline{\lambda}\in X^{+}_{{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup J}}\end{subarray}}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(w\cdot\underline{\lambda})_{{J}}\Big).

by
[
40
, Theorem 4.10]
. This implies

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}(\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda}),

\displaystyle(\underline{\lambda}))\cong{\rm Ext}^{r+s}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\Big(\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big).

(5.28)

Therefore, we have the following compositions of morphisms:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\Big(\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda}),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)

(5.29)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\Big(\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\Big((\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda}))\big|_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)},\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\Big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\Big(r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi_{I})\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{J},\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\Big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1},

where the first map is induced by the injection

, the second map is induced by the injection

, and the third map sends
V
to the push-forward of

via the natural evaluation map

,
f\mapsto f(1)
. The fourth map is the inverse of the map induced by the natural projection
(\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda}))\big|_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\twoheadrightarrow r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{I}\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{J}
. The last map is induced by the isomorphism (see Proposition
3.6
, (
3.12
))
\pi_{J}\xrightarrow{\sim}r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi_{I})\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{J}
. Using a parallel discussion in (
5.24
), we see that the last three maps in (
5.29
) are isomorphisms (where we use the isomorphism (
5.28
)). We denote the map (
5.29
) by
\varrho_{I,J}
. In a similar way, we can construct an explicit map
\overline{\varrho}_{I,J}:{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{1}\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{1}
which is induced by

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

The natural injection
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
induces a natural morphism
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E)\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)
, which induces a morphism
{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L),E)\rightarrow{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)
. This morphism is also induced by the morphism composition

{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\xrightarrow{\det}{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L).\;

By the construction of
\varrho_{I,J}
in (
5.29
), we deduce:

Lemma 5.12
.

The following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{I}^{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{J}^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varrho_{I,J}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1},}

where the top morphism is induced by the natural injection
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
.

Lemma 5.13
.

Suppose that
J\subseteq I
, the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{analyticExt1})}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{analyticExt1})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varrho_{I,J}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}.}

A similar result holds for
\overline{\varrho}_{I,J}
.

Proof.

Firstly, we write

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{r}_{I}:={\rm Ext}^{r}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\big(\text{resp., }{\rm Ext}^{r}_{J}:={\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\big),

(5.30)

\displaystyle i_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}}:=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big(\text{resp., }i_{J}\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}:=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big),

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{I}}_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}}:={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big(\text{resp., }{\mathbb{I}}_{J}\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}:={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big),

\displaystyle\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}}:=\pi_{I}(\underline{\lambda})\big(\text{resp., }\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}:=\pi_{J}(\underline{\lambda})\big),

\displaystyle\text{and }{\mathbb{I}}^{I}_{J}(\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}):={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

for simplicity.

Considering the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}},{\mathbb{I}}_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(b),\simeq}
\scriptstyle{(a),\simeq}
\scriptstyle{(e)}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}},{\mathbb{I}}_{J}\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(a^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{(b^{\prime\prime}),\simeq}
\scriptstyle{(a^{\prime\prime}),\simeq}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{I}(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}(i_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}}),\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(e^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{(c),\simeq}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{I}(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}(i_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}}),{\mathbb{I}}^{I}_{J}(\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(c^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{(d^{\prime}),\simeq}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{J}(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}(i_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}},\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(c^{\prime\prime})}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{I}(\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}},\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(e^{\prime\prime})}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{I}(\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}},{\mathbb{I}}^{I}_{J}(\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(d^{\prime\prime}),\simeq}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{J}(\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}},\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}),}

The maps
(a),(a^{\prime\prime}),(d^{\prime})
are edge morphisms of spectral sequence (and an easy variation of it) in (
5.16
), and the map
(d^{\prime\prime})
is edge morphism of spectral sequence (
5.26
). The map
(e)
is induced by the injection
{\mathbb{I}}_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{J}\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}
. The maps
(e^{\prime})
and
(e^{\prime\prime})
are induced by the
injection
\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}^{I}_{J}(\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}})
. The maps
(c)
and
(c^{\prime})
are induced by the injection
\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}}\hookrightarrow r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}(i_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}})
, and the map
(c^{\prime\prime})
is
induced by the injection
\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}\hookrightarrow r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}(i_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}})
. The maps
(b),(b^{\prime\prime})
are given by the composition of first and second isomorphisms in (
5.24
). The map
(a^{\prime})
is the edge morphism of the spectral sequence
E_{2}^{r,s}={\rm Ext}^{r}_{I}(\mathrm{H}_{s}({\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),i_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}}),{\mathbb{I}}^{I}_{J}(\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}))\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s}_{G}\big(i_{I}\pi_{I}^{\underline{\lambda}},{\mathbb{I}}_{J}\pi_{J}^{\underline{\lambda}}\big)
. It is clear that all the four blocks of the above diagram are commutative. In this case, we see that
(a^{\prime})
is also an isomorphism. Note that
\varrho_{I,J}
is the composition of
(e^{\prime\prime})
and the isomorphism
(d^{\prime\prime})
. The result follows by an easy diagram chasing.
∎

Remark 5.14
.

Note that all the above description also hold for smooth case, i.e., we are working with the smooth extension group
{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{S}_{J}^{1}
instead of the

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}
. Moreover, the injection
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
induces an injection

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

by an easy dévissage (using
{\rm Hom}_{G}\Big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big/i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)=0
). Using Lemma
2.3
(
2.11
), Lemma
6.7
and the explicit description for
{\rm Ext}^{1}
, we get the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I})\cong{\rm Hom}_{\infty}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E),}

where the top injection is induced by

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(5.31)

In particular, we see that
\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\underline{\lambda},\Psi)^{+}
comes from a locally algebraic extension of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
i^{\infty}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
if and only if
\Psi\in X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I})
.

We push forward (
5.23
) along surjection
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\twoheadrightarrow v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
and then pullback via natural injections

i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

we get a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\widetilde{\pi}_{J})^{0}
, which is an extension of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. It gives a cohomology class
[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\widetilde{\pi}_{J})^{0}]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)
.

Let
\widetilde{\pi}_{I}
be an locally analytic representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
over
E
, which is a locally analytic extension of
\pi_{I}
by
\pi_{I}
. It gives a cohomology class
[\widetilde{\pi}_{I}]\in\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1}
. Then the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction
\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\widetilde{\pi}_{I}\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{I}\big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}
lies in the following exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\longrightarrow\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\widetilde{\pi}_{I}\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda})_{I}\big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\longrightarrow 0.

(5.32)

We push forward (
5.32
) along natural injections
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
and then pullback via natural injections

i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\underline{\lambda})
, we reconstruct
\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\varrho_{I,J}([\widetilde{\pi}_{I}]))^{+}
by Lemma
5.11
and Lemma
5.13
. Furthermore, we pushforward (
5.32
) along the natural morphism

{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\twoheadrightarrow v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

and then pullback via natural injections

i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\underline{\lambda})
, we may reconstruct the representation
\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\varrho_{I,J}([\widetilde{\pi}_{I}]))^{0}
by Lemma
5.11
and Lemma
5.13
. But by definition it is zero. Therefore, we get a homomorphism

\varrho_{I,J}^{0}:\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}/\varrho_{I,J}(\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1})\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).\;

(5.33)

The remainder of this section is devoted to showing

{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

This is the main result of this paper.

We begin with a more general situation. Let
J\subseteq I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
be subsets of
\Delta_{n}(k)
such that
|I|=|J|+1
. Suppose that
I=J\cup\{\upsilon r\}
for some
1\leq\upsilon\leq k-1
. We are going to construct an injection (see (
5.39
))

\varrho_{I,J}^{\sharp}:{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)/{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E\big)\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})).

We see that the first term is isomorphic to
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
. We will show that this injection is an isomorphism when
J=\emptyset
and
I=\{ir\}
for some
1\leq i\leq k-1
by comparing dimensions (see Theorem
5.19
). Then we get the desired result.

We first have the following proposition.

Proposition 5.15
.

\mathbf{(a)}
We have an isomorphism of
E
-vector spaces

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

\mathbf{(b)}
The map
\varrho_{I,J}:\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}
is injective. We further have an exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}/\varrho_{I,J}(\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{G}\big(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\rightarrow\ker(\mathcal{E}_{I}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{J}^{2})\rightarrow 0.

In particular, we may view
\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1}
as a subspace of
\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}
.

\mathbf{(c)}
The map
\overline{\varrho}_{I,J}:{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{1}\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{1}
is injective. We further have an exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{1}/\overline{\varrho}_{I,J}({\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{1})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\rightarrow\ker({\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{2}\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{2})\rightarrow 0.

In particular, we will view
{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{1}
as a subspace of
{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{1}
via the map
\varrho_{I,J}
with no further mention.

Proof.

Since
{\rm Hom}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=0
, we deduce Part
\mathbf{(a)}
from
[
9
, Lemma 3.1]
or
[
32
, Lemma 3.2]
. We apply the acyclic complex in Proposition
4.4
, i.e.,

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{J,k-1}\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{J,k-2}\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{J,k-3}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{J,1}\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{J,0}={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

(5.34)

to representation
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, where
C^{{\rm an}}_{J,j}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|K\backslash J|=j\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. As in the proof of Corollary
5.10
, we get a spectral sequence

E_{1}^{-s,r}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|K\backslash J|=0\end{subarray}}{\rm Ext}^{r,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r-s,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(5.35)

By (
5.14
), only the
0
-th and
(-1)
-th columns of the
E_{1}
-page of (
5.35
) can have non-zero terms. The
1
-th row is

\begin{matrix}&{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{1}&\longrightarrow&{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{1}\\
&\parallel&&\parallel\\
&{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)&\longrightarrow&{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\\
&\parallel&&\parallel\\
&E_{1}^{-1,1}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{1}^{-1,1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&E_{1}^{0,1}\end{matrix}

The
2
-th row is given by

\begin{matrix}&{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{2}&\longrightarrow&{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{2}\\
&\parallel&&\parallel\\
&{\rm Ext}^{2}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)&\longrightarrow&{\rm Ext}^{2}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\\
&\parallel&&\parallel\\
&E_{1}^{-1,2}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{1}^{-1,2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&E_{1}^{0,2}.\end{matrix}

Therefore, by Lemma
5.3
, we obtain the following exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow E_{2}^{0,0}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{0}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\rightarrow\ker({\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{1}\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{1})\rightarrow 0,

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{1}/{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{1}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\rightarrow\ker({\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{2}\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{2})\rightarrow 0.

These prove Part
(c)
. Part
(b)
follows by the same argument.
∎

Remark 5.16
.

It is difficult to describe the morphisms
\mathcal{E}_{I}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{J}^{2}
and
{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{2}\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{2}
explicitly. Therefore, we cannot explain the terms
\ker(\mathcal{E}_{I}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{J}^{2})
and
\ker({\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{2}\rightarrow{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{2})
. Moreover, we will see below that certain subspaces of
\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}/{\varrho}_{I,J}(\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1})
or
{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{J}^{1}/\overline{\varrho}_{I,J}({\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{I}^{1})
are easy to control, which are enough for our computation.

By Lemma
5.12
and Lemma
5.6
, the injections
\iota_{I}^{1}
and
\iota_{J}^{1}
induce a map

\iota_{J,I}^{1}:{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)/{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E\big)\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}/\varrho_{I,J}(\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1}).\;

Consider the composition:

\displaystyle\varrho_{I,J}^{1}:=\varrho_{I,J}^{0}\circ\iota_{J,I}^{1}:{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)/{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E\big)\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(5.36)

More precisely, let
\psi\in{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)/{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E\big)
and let
\Psi
be a lifting of
\psi
to
{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)
. Then
\varrho_{I,J}^{0}(\psi)=[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\iota_{J}(\Psi))^{0}]
(it is clear that
\varrho_{I,J}^{0}(\psi)
is independent of the choice of lifting
\Psi
).

Lemma 5.17
.

The maps
\iota_{J,I}^{1}
and
\varrho_{I,J}^{1}
are injective.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.15
(b), it suffices to show that
\iota_{J,I}^{1}
is injective. By Lemma
5.15
(b), we will view
{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)
,
{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E\big)
and
\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1}
as subspaces of
\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}
. It suffices to prove that
{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E\big)=\varrho_{I,J}(\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1})\cap{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)
. By the definition of
\varrho_{I,J}
in (
5.29
), we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{I}^{1}}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{J}^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{I}}
\scriptstyle{c_{I}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\varrho_{I,J}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{J}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{J}}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}({\pi_{I}}|_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)},{\pi_{I}}|_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{I}^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\varrho_{I,J}|_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}(\omega_{\pi_{J}},\omega_{\pi_{J}})}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\omega_{\pi_{I}},\omega_{\pi_{I}}),}

where the morphism
c_{I}^{\prime}
(resp.,
c_{I}^{\prime\prime}
) is induced by the natural injection
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
(resp.,
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)
) and the morphism
c_{I}
(resp.,
c_{J}
) is induced by the natural injection
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
(resp.,

{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)
). Using the above commutative diagram, we see that
if
