\Psi\in\varrho_{I,J}(\mathcal{E}_{I}^{1})\cap{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)
, then
c_{J}([\pi_{J}(\Psi)])
comes from an element

[\widetilde{\pi}_{I}|_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}({\pi_{I}}|_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)},{\pi_{I}}|_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}).

Let
\Psi^{\prime}:=c_{I}^{\prime\prime}([\widetilde{\pi}_{I}|_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}])\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\omega_{\pi_{I}},\omega_{\pi_{I}})
. By the injectivity of
\varrho_{I,J}
, and the proof of Lemma
5.6
, we deduce that
\varrho_{I,J}
sends
\omega_{\pi_{I}}^{-1}\otimes_{E}\Psi^{\prime}\in{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E\big)
to
\Psi
. This completes the proof.
∎

Consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\longrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\longrightarrow v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\longrightarrow 0,

which induces a long exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{G}(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}\big(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{G}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}\big(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

(5.37)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{G}\big(u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}\big(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\longrightarrow}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}\big(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}_{G}^{1}\big(u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

Since
{\rm Hom}_{G}\big(u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=0
(this is a direct consequence of the calculation of Jordan-Hölder factors of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized parabolic Steinberg representation, see Section
4.4
), we get an injection

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}\big(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}\big(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(5.38)

Proposition 5.18
.

The injection

\varrho_{I,J}^{1}:{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)/{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E\big)\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

factors through the injection (
5.38
). We put

\varrho_{I,J}^{\sharp}:{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)/{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E\big)\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))

(5.39)

the resulting homomorphism.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{w}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
be the kernel of the surjection
\bigoplus_{qr\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup\{qr\}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\twoheadrightarrow u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. We then consider the exact sequence

0\rightarrow\widetilde{w}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{qr\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup\{qr\}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow 0.

Since
{\rm Hom}_{G}(\widetilde{w}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))=0
, we deduce from the long exact sequence of extension groups that the natural map

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{qr\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup\{qr\}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

(5.40)

is injective. For
\Psi\in{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}_{J}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)
, the long exact sequence (
5.37
) implies that
[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\Psi)^{0}]
falls into
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))
if and only if its image in
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))
is trivial. Therefore, by the long exact sequence (
5.37
) and the injection (
5.40
), it suffices to prove that the image of any
[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\Psi)^{0}]
in
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup\{qr\}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))
induced by the inclusion

i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup\{qr\}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is trivial. Note that the natural inclusion induces an isomorphism

{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J\cup\{qr\}}(L),E)/{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup\{qr\}}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)/{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E),

we can choose an element
\Psi_{qr}\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J\cup\{qr\}}}(L),E)
such that its image
\overline{\Psi}_{qr}
in the group

{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),E)/{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),E)

agrees with
\overline{\Psi}
. Then the extension class
[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\Psi_{qr})^{0}]
agrees with
[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\Psi)^{0}]
by (
5.36
). The pullback of
[\mathscr{E}_{I}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\Psi_{qr})^{0}]
along the inclusion
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup\{qr\}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is equal to the pullback of
[\mathscr{E}_{J\cup\{qr\}}^{J}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\Psi_{qr})^{0}]
along the inclusion
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup\{qr\}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J\cup\{qr\}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. But the latter extension is split by the discussion before (
5.33
). This completes the proof.
∎

We are ready to prove the first main theorem.

Theorem 5.19
.

Let
ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, we have isomorphisms of
E
-vector spaces

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n},E\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\omega^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\pi}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}

\displaystyle\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\underline{\lambda})\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

In particular, we have
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=d_{L}+1
. Furthermore, the homomorphism

\varrho_{\{ir\},\emptyset}^{\sharp}:{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E\big)/{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}(L),E\big)\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

(5.41)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Since
{\rm Hom}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=0
, the second isomorphism follows from
[
9
, Lemma 3.1]
or
[
31
, Lemma 3.2]
. We apply the exact sequence in Proposition
4.4
,

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{\emptyset,k-1}\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{\emptyset,k-2}\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{\emptyset,k-3}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{\emptyset,1}\rightarrow C^{{\rm an}}_{\emptyset,0}={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

(5.42)

to the locally analytic representation
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. As in the proof of Corollary
5.10
, we deduce a spectral sequence

E_{1}^{-s,r}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|K|=s\end{subarray}}{\rm Ext}^{r}_{G,\omega^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\pi}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r-s}_{G,\omega^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\pi}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(5.43)

By Proposition
5.10
, Lemma
5.7
, only the objects in the
(1-k)
-th and
(2-k)
-th columns of the
E_{1}
-page can be non-zero, where the
(k-1)
-th row is given by

\begin{matrix}&{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}(L)/Z_{n},E)&\longrightarrow&{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)/Z_{n},E)\\
&\parallel&&\parallel\\
&E_{1}^{1-k,k-1}&\xrightarrow{d_{1}^{1-k,k-1}}&E_{1}^{2-k,k-1}\end{matrix}

and the
k
-th row is given by

\begin{matrix}&{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}(L)/Z_{n},E)&\longrightarrow&{\rm\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{2}\\
&\parallel&&\parallel\\
&E_{1}^{1-k,k}&\xrightarrow{d_{1}^{1-k,k}}&E_{1}^{2-k,k}\end{matrix}.

Since
\mathfrak{sl}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}
is semisimple,
{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{{\rm an}}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}(L)/Z_{n},E)\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{i}(\mathfrak{s}l_{n,\Sigma_{L}},E)=0
for
i=1,2
. By Lemma
5.3
, we deduce

E_{2}^{2-k,k-1}=E_{\infty}^{2-k,k-1}=F^{k-1}H^{1}/F^{k}H^{1}={\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\omega^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\pi}\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

The first isomorphism follows. Note that we also have an isomorphism

{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)/Z_{n},E\big),

which is an
E
-vector space of dimension
d_{L}+1
. At last, by applying Proposition
5.18
to the case
J=\emptyset
and
I=\{ir\}
, we get an injection
\varrho_{\{ir\},\emptyset}^{\sharp}
, which in fact is an isomorphism since both of these two
E
-vector spaces are
d_{L}+1
-dimensional by Proposition
5.19
. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.20
.

when
J=\emptyset
and
I=\{ir\}
, we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle\iota_{ir}:{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E)/{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}(L),E),

(5.44)

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle\mapsto[(a_{1}I_{r},\cdots,a_{k}I_{r})\mapsto\psi(a_{i}/a_{i+1})],

Then the composition
\varrho_{\{ir\},\emptyset}^{\sharp}\circ\iota_{ir}
gives the desired isomorphism

{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(5.45)

Remark 5.21
.

We describe explicitly the isomorphism
\varrho^{1}_{\{ir\},\emptyset}
in Theorem
5.19
. For
\psi\in{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
, let
\mathscr{E}_{\{ir\}}^{\emptyset}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\iota_{i}(\psi))^{0}
be the extension of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, associated with
\psi
as in the (
5.45
). By Theorem
5.19
, we see that the injection (
5.38
)

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}\big(\pi,\underline{\lambda},{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

is actually an isomorphism. Then the pull back of
\mathscr{E}_{\{ir\}}^{\emptyset}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\iota_{\nu}(\psi))^{0}
via the natural injection
u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is split (as an extension of
u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
). Quotient it by
u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, we deduce
\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
, which is an extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. We therefore get that
[\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)]
is the extension class associated with
\psi
via
\varrho_{\{ir\},\emptyset}
. Note that
\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
comes from a locally algebraic extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\underline{\lambda})
if and only if
\psi\in{\rm Hom}_{\infty}(L^{\times},E)
.

Remark 5.22
.

A modification of the recent work of Zicheng Qian
[
32
]
may give a way to compute the higher
{\rm Ext}
-groups
{\rm Ext}^{i}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)
for general
J\subseteq I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
.

5.5
Extensions between locally analytic representations-II

This section follows along the line of
[
15
, Section 2.3]
. Let
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, and let
I\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. Recall the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic (resp., locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic, resp., smooth) generalized Steinberg representations
{\rm St}_{I}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

(resp.,
{\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, resp.,
{\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi)
) of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
defined in Definition
4.1
(resp., Definition
3.7
). In this section, we study certain subrepresentations of
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, and compute the extensions of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by such representations. These subrepresentations have more clear structure than
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

By the transitivity of parabolic inductions, we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi)\Big)^{\infty}\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda})=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big/\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subseteq I}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

\displaystyle\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{I}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}}={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big/\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subseteq I}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).\;

Consider the following composition

\tau_{I}:\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{I}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}}\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).\;

(5.46)

Let
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
be its image. When
I=\Delta_{k,i}
, we denote
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\{ir\}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

Lemma 5.23
.

Let
ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. We have the following commutative diagram with all the rows exact,

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)\Big)^{\infty}\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 40.0pt\tau_{ir}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 40.0pt(\ref{tautauI})}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Proof.

By
[
30
, Theorem]
, the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic subrepresentation of

\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}}\cong\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big({\overline{M}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda}),\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)

is
\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)\Big)^{\infty}\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda})
. By (
3.14
) and (
3.15
), this locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic subrepresentation is an extension of
\mathrm{St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. The first row is exact. In a similar way, we deduce from (
4.9
) the exactness of the third row. It remains to prove that the second row is exact. By the exactness of the first row, we see that the composition
\tau_{ir}
factors through

\tau_{ir}^{\prime}:\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}}\Big/v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\longrightarrow\mathrm{St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).

(5.47)

It suffices to show that
\tau_{ir}^{\prime}
is still injective. The restriction of
\tau_{ir}^{\prime}
on its locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic vectors is injective. Suppose that
\tau_{ir}^{\prime}
is not injective. Let
V
be an irreducible constituent of
\ker(\tau_{ir}^{\prime})
, then
V
has the form (since
V
is not locally algebraic)

\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}\Big(\overline{L}(-s\cdot\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)\Big),

(5.48)

with
s\cdot\underline{\lambda}\in X^{+}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup{\Delta}_{k,i}}
. Note that the kernel of (
5.47
) is also a subquotient of
\sum\limits_{\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, so
V
is an irreducible constituent of

{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\cong\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}\big({\overline{M}}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda}),\pi_{I}\big)

for some
\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)
. If (
5.48
) appears as an irreducible subquotient of
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, then we see that
I
is a subset of
\Delta_{k,i}
and
{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)
is also an irreducible subquotient of
i^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}\pi_{I}
by
[
30
, Theorem]
. This is impossible by the definition of
{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)
. The result follows.
∎

Lemma 5.24
.

The injections
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
for all
ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
induce an injection of
G
-representations:

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\bigoplus_{\mathrm{St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})}^{i\in\Delta_{n}(k)}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).

Proof.

It suffices to prove that any irreducible constituent of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
that is not locally algebraic, i.e., is of the form

\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}}(\overline{L}(-s\cdot\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)),

with
s\cdot\underline{\lambda}\in X^{+}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup{\Delta}_{k,i}}
, cannot appear as an irreducible constituent of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{j}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
for
j\neq i
. This is a direct consequence of
[
8
, Corollary 2.7]
. The lemma follows.
∎

We next compute the socle of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, by modifying the proof of
[
15
, Proposition 2.23]
to our case, but we need some preliminaries.

In our case, the very strongly admissibility of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic parabolic generalized Steinberg representation cannot be deduced directly from the strategy of the proof of
[
15
, Proposition 2.23]
. We prove it by using global methods (more precisely, the Emerton’s completed cohomology).

Lemma 5.25
.

The locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic parabolic generalized Steinberg representations
{\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi)
of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
is very strongly admissible.

Proof.

Step 1.
We write
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)={\rm GL}_{k_{1}r}(L)\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{k_{l}r}(L)
, where
\underline{k}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}:=(k_{1},\cdots,k_{k-|I|})
is the ordered partition of integer
k
associated with the
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
. We thus have an isomorphism
{\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\cong V_{1}\otimes_{E}\cdots\otimes_{E}V_{l}

of smooth representations of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
, where
V_{i}
is also a locally algebraic parabolic Steinberg representation of
{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)
for each
1\leq i\leq l
. By definition, it suffices to show that there is a continuous
G
-equivariant
E
-linear injection
{\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi)\rightarrow W
, where
W
is an admissible continuous representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
on an
E
-Banach space (see
[
19
, Proposition-Definition 6.2.3]
for admissible continuous representation). By
[
10
, Lemma A.3]
, it suffices to prove that each
V_{i}
(as a smooth representation of
{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)
over
E
) is very strongly admissible. Therefore, we can assume that
I=\Delta_{n}(k)
. Fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbb{C}}
. In this case, by the discussion after
[
41
, Definition 3]
,
{\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi)
is an irreducible essentially discrete series representation of
G
over
E
, in the terminology of
[
41
, Definition 3]
, i.e.,
\iota({\rm St}_{I}^{\infty}(\pi))
is an irreducible essentially discrete series representation of
G
over
\iota(E)\subset{\mathbb{C}}
. By definition, there exists a smooth character
\eta:G\rightarrow E^{\times}
(by enlarging
E
if necessary), such that
\iota({\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi)\otimes_{E}\eta)
is (unitary) discrete series representation of
G
over
\iota(E)\subset{\mathbb{C}}
. Put
\mathcal{W}:={\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi)\otimes_{E}\eta
. Let
\omega_{\mathcal{W}}
be the central character of
\mathcal{W}
.

Step 2.
We remark first that there exists a number field
F
, a finite place
w
of
F
satisfying the completion
F_{w}
of
F
at
w
is isomorphic to our local field
L
. Let
D
be a division algebra of dimension
n^{2}
and center over
F
. Suppose that
D
is ramified at a set
S_{D}
of places disjoint from
w
. Let
S_{\infty}
be the set of infinite places of
F
, we further suppose that
D
is ramified at the set
S_{\infty}
. Let
{\mathbb{G}}_{D,{\mathbb{Q}}}={\rm Res}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}D^{\times}
be the scalar restriction of the algebraic group associated to the division algebra
D
. Then
{\mathbb{G}}_{D,{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
is a semisimple anisotropic
{\mathbb{Q}}
-group. By
[
41
, Corollary 3, Lemma 4]
, we see that
there is a classical algebraic automorphic representation
\Pi
of
{\mathbb{G}}_{D,{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
such that the local factor
\Pi_{w}\cong\iota(\mathcal{W}\otimes_{E}\omega_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1})
and
\Pi_{\infty}=1
(trivial algebraic representation). In
[
41
]
, this result is proved by a simplified form of the Selberg trace formula. By enlarging
E
if necessary, we may realize
\iota^{-1}(\Pi)
over
E
.

Step 3.
Let
W
be a finite-dimensional algebraic representation of
{\mathbb{G}}_{D,{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
over
E
, and
K^{w}\subset{\mathbb{G}}_{D,{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{A}}_{f}^{w})
be a tame level such that
(\Pi^{w})^{K^{w}}\neq 0
. Let
W_{0}
be an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice of
W
, and denote by
\mathcal{S}_{W_{0}}
the set (ordered by inclusions) of open compact subgroups of
{\mathbb{G}}_{D,{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{A}}_{f}^{w})({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cong\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(F_{w})
which stabilize
W_{0}
. Then we can associate to
W_{0}
(resp.,
{W_{0}/\varpi_{E}^{s}}
) a local system
\mathcal{V}_{W_{0}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{V}_{W_{0}/\varpi_{E}^{s}}
) of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-modules over
Y_{K_{w}K^{w}}:={\mathbb{G}}_{D,{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash{\mathbb{G}}_{D,{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{A}})/A_{\infty}^{\circ}K_{\infty}^{\circ}K_{w}K^{w}
. We recall that the Emerton’s completed cohomology (see
[
16
]
)

\widetilde{H}^{i}(K^{w},W_{0}):=\varprojlim_{s}\varinjlim_{K_{w}\in\mathcal{S}_{W_{0}}}H^{i}(Y_{K_{w}K^{w}},\mathcal{V}_{W_{0}/\varpi_{E}^{s}}).

Then
\widetilde{H}^{i}(K^{\mathfrak{p}},W_{0})_{E}:=\widetilde{H}^{i}(K^{\mathfrak{p}},W_{0})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}E
gives an admissible Banach representation of
{\mathbb{G}}_{D}^{\mathrm{ad}}(F_{w})=\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(F_{w})
over
E
. Let
\widehat{\Pi}
be the classical
p
-adic automorphic representation of
{\mathbb{G}}_{D,{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbf{A}}_{f})
over
E
attached to the automorphic form
\iota^{-1}(\Pi)
constructed in Step 2 (see
[
16
, Definition 3.1.5]
), then we have an injection

(W^{\vee}\otimes\Pi_{w})\otimes(\Pi^{w})^{K^{w}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{0}(K^{w},W),

by
[
16
, Corollary 2.2.25, Proposition 3.2.2]
. A choice of non-zero vector
v\in(\Pi^{w})^{K^{w}}
then gives rise to an injection
\mathcal{W}\otimes_{E}\omega_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}=W^{\vee}\otimes\Pi_{w}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{0}(K^{w},W)
of representation of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(F_{w})=\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(L)
over
E
. We finish the proof by twisting the character
\eta^{-1}\omega_{\mathcal{W}}
.
∎

The socle of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is given by the following proposition.

Proposition 5.26
.

We have

{\rm soc}_{G}(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))\cong\mathrm{St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle soc}_{G}\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}^{\infty}_{\Delta_{k,i}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}

(5.49)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle soc}_{G}\mathcal{F}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}}^{G}\big(\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{\Delta_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)\big)\cong\mathcal{F}_{G}^{G}\big(\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda}),{\rm soc}_{G}\big(i^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}{\rm St}_{\Delta_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)\big)\big)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle soc}_{G}\Big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big/\sum_{\emptyset\neq J\subseteq\Delta_{k,i}}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

where the first and second isomorphisms follow from
[
7
, Corollary 2.5]
, and the last isomorphism follows from Lemma
6.8
. Suppose that there exists an irreducible constituent
W
of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})/{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
such that
W\hookrightarrow{\rm soc}_{G}(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))
. We see that the pull-back
V
of
({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{\Delta_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}
via this injection gives an extension of
W
by
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, i.e., such
V
lies in the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\hskip 20.0ptV_{\hskip 0.2pt}\hskip 20.0pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\hskip 16.0ptW_{\hskip 0.2pt}\hskip 16.0pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

By (
5.49
), the first row of this diagram is non-split. Similar to the proof of Lemma
5.23
,
W
has the form
\mathcal{F}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}}^{G}(\overline{L}(-s\cdot\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{\Delta_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi))

with
s\in S_{n}^{|\Sigma_{L}|}
satisfying
s\cdot\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta^{k}_{n}\cup{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{+}
.

Claim.

V
is very strongly admissible, i.e., there is a continuous
G
-equivariant
E
-linear injection
V\rightarrow W
, where
W
is an admissible continuous representation of
G
on an
E
-Banach space (see
[
19
, Proposition-Definition 6.2.3]
for admissible continuous representation). Indeed, by
[
17
, Proposition 2.1.2]
and
V\hookrightarrow({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{\Delta_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}
, it suffices to show that
{\rm St}_{\Delta_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is very strongly admissible. Since an admissible Banach representation tensoring with a finite-dimensional
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)
is still an admissible Banach representation, it remains to show that
{\rm St}_{\Delta_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)
is very strongly admissible. This fact is proved in Lemma
5.25
.
We are going to prove that
V
is split by using the same strategy of
[
15
, Lemma 2.4]
. Indeed, this result follows by an easy variation of the proof of
[
15
, Lemma 2.24]
. We briefly indicate below the changes. Only Part
(e)
of the proof of
[
15
, Lemma 2.4]
need some more modification. In our case, we need to show that

{\rm Hom}_{{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle},+}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}}}\Big({\rm St}_{\Delta_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)\otimes_{E}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{k,i}(L)}}\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mu)_{\Delta_{k,i}},{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\rm an}}\big({{\mathbf{N}}}_{0}(L),v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\Big)=0,

(5.50)

where
{{\mathbf{N}}}_{0}(L)
is a compact open subgroup of
{\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{k,i}}
, and
{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle},+}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}}:=\{z\in{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}:z{{\mathbf{N}}}_{0}(L)z^{-1}\subset{{\mathbf{N}}}_{0}(L)\}
. If this vector space is non-zero, then the same argument after
[
15
, (55)]
and the adjunction property (
2.6
)) implies
{\rm Hom}_{G}\Big(({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty})^{\infty},v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\Big)\neq 0.\;
Then we deduce
{\rm Hom}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi),v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\big)\neq 0
. This is impossible by Lemma
6.7
. The proposition follows.
∎

Recall that
\underline{\lambda}:=(\lambda_{1,\sigma},\cdots,\lambda_{n,\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
. Let
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, we put

\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma}:=(\lambda_{1,\sigma},\cdots,\lambda_{n,\sigma}),\underline{\lambda}^{\sigma}:=(\lambda_{1,\sigma^{\prime}},\cdots,\lambda_{n,\sigma^{\prime}})_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}}.

Let
ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. We put
{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma})\cong{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma})_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}
, which is a locally
\sigma
-analytic representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)
. Consider the locally
\sigma
-analytic parabolic induction

\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma})\big)^{\sigma-{\rm an}}.

By
[
30
, the main theorem]
and
[
15
, Lemma 2.7, Lemma 2.10]
, we have

\displaystyle\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)\big)^{\infty}\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma})\big)^{\sigma-{\rm an}}\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda}^{\sigma})

(5.51)

\displaystyle\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}\longrightarrow{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).

Denote by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\Big(\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma})\Big)^{\sigma-{\rm an}}\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda}^{\sigma})\Big/v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

(5.52)

We put

C_{i,\sigma}:=\mathcal{F}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}}^{G}\Big(\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda}^{\sigma})\otimes_{E}\overline{L}(-s_{ir,\sigma}\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma}),{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi)\Big),

which is an irreducible subrepresentation of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})/{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. By Proposition
4.6
(applied to
J=S=\emptyset
),
C_{i,\sigma}
appears as an irreducible constituent in
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

with nonzero multiplicity

[{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):C_{i,\sigma}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n}\\
\mathrm{supp}(w^{\prime})=\emptyset\end{subarray}}(-1)^{l(w^{\prime})}[{\overline{M}}(-w^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\lambda}):\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda}^{\sigma})\otimes_{E}\overline{L}(-s_{ir,\sigma}\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma})]=1.

(5.53)

We are going to define certain subrepresentations of
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

Definition 5.27
.

(1)

Denote by
\Sigma_{i,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
the extension of
C_{i,\sigma}
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
appearing as a subrepresentation of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

(2)

Put

\displaystyle\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\bigoplus^{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})}\Sigma_{i,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

(5.54)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\bigoplus^{ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}}_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\subseteq{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).

By definition,
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is an extension of
\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}C_{i,\sigma}
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

Proposition 5.28
.

For any
V\in\{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\}
, we have
{\rm soc}_{G}V={\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).

Proof.

For each
V\in\{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\}
, the result follows from Proposition
5.26
. By
[
8
, Corollary 2.7]
, the irreducible constituents
\{C_{i,\sigma}\}_{ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)},\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
are all distinct, hence we have
{\rm soc}_{G}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\cong{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.
∎

Proposition 5.29
.

Let
ir,jr\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
and let
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, the following natural morphisms are isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big),

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma_{j}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{j}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big),

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma_{j,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{j,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).\;

Proof.

By Proposition
4.6
, the irreducible constituent of
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})/\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
(resp.,
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{j}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})/\Sigma_{j}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, resp.,
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{j,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})/\Sigma_{j,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
) has the form

\mathcal{F}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\overline{L}(-s\cdot\underline{\lambda}),W)
with
\lg(s)>1
. Therefore, all the isomorphisms follow from
[
15
, Lemma 2.26]
and an easy dévissage argument.
∎

Remark 5.30
.

Note that
\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(-\underline{{\lambda}})
admits a natural increasing filtration
{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(-\underline{{\lambda}})
in BGG category
\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}}
such that

{\rm Fil}^{s}\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(-\underline{{\lambda}})/{\rm Fil}^{s-1}\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(-\underline{{\lambda}})\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}},{\rm lg}(w)=s\\
m_{{\Delta}_{k,i},w}(\underline{{\lambda}})>0,w\cdot\underline{\lambda}\in X^{+}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup{\Delta}_{k,i}}\end{subarray}}\overline{L}(-w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}}),

where
m_{{\Delta}_{k,i},w}(\underline{{\lambda}}):=[\overline{M}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(-\underline{{\lambda}}):\overline{L}(-w\cdot\underline{{\lambda}})]
(note that this multiplicity may be greater than one). By
[
30
, Theorem]
, it induces
an increasing filtration
{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
on
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. Then
\Sigma_{j}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
lies in
{\rm Fil}^{1}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

We will see below that only the part
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
of
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
contributes non-split extensions in the
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)
. Similar to the proof of
[
15
, Lemma 2.28]
, we have

Lemma 5.31
.

Let
ir,jr\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, then for any
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, we have

(1)
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,j}}}^{G}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(s_{j,\sigma}\cdot\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta_{k,j}}\otimes_{E}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,j}}^{\infty}(\pi)\Big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}\Big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\hbox{$i=j$;}\\
0,&\hbox{$i\neq j$}.\end{array}\right.

(2)
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),C_{j,\sigma}\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\hbox{$i=j$;}\\
0,&\hbox{$i\neq j$}.\end{array}\right.

Denote by
\Pi^{i,\sigma}_{1}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),C_{i,\sigma}\big)
the unique nonsplit element.

Remark 5.32
.

For each
1\leq i\leq k-1
, we expect that the unique non-split element
\Pi^{i,\sigma}_{1}
is a subrepresentation of the conjectural representations in
[
8
,
(EXT)
]
.

Lemma 5.33
.

Let
ir,jr\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
.

(1) If
i\neq j
, then the following natural morphism is an isomorphism:

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{j}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(2) The following natural morphism is an isomorphism:

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(3) For
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
,
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma_{i,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=2
.

Proof.

The lemma follows by an easy variation of the proof of
[
15
, Lemma 2.29]
. The “[31,Theorem 1]” (resp., “[Lemma 2.28(2)]”) in the proof of
[
15
, Lemma 2.29]
has to be replaced by Proposition
6.9
(resp., Lemma
5.31
(2)).
∎

Finally, we have:

Proposition 5.34
.

Let
ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E)/{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big),

(5.55)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)/{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma_{i,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

Proof.

The first assertion follows from Theorem (
5.19
), Proposition
5.29
, Lemma
5.33
(2). The second follows from an easy variation of the proof of
[
15
, Proposition 2.30]
.
∎

5.6
Collection of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations

In Remark
5.21
, we obtain a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
, which is an extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. Let
ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, and
\psi\in{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E)/{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}(L),E)
. We denote by
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
the extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
attached to
\psi
via (
5.55
), which is thus a subrepresentation of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
. Let
[\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\underline{\lambda})\big)
be the extension class associated with
\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
. By (
5.19
), Proposition
5.29
and Lemma
5.33
(2), we see that
[\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)]
actually comes by pushing-forward from
[\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)]
via an injection
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. If
\psi\in{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)
for some
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, then
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
is isomorphic to the push-forward of an extension, denoted by
\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
, of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\underline{\lambda})
by
\Sigma_{i,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. It is clear that
[\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)]
is isomorphic to the extension attached to
\psi
via (
5.55
).

6
Appendix: smooth extensions of generalized parabolic Steinberg representations

6.1
Bernstein-Zelevinsky theory

We now recall some details of the Bernstein-Zelevinsky theory, following
[
4
]
and
[
1
]
. Although several of our references work over
{\mathbb{C}}
, the various results over
{\mathbb{C}}
are transferred to our content over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
if we choose (and fix) an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathbb{C}}
. Bernstein-Zelevinsky classifies the irreducible smooth representations of
G={\rm GL}_{n}(L)
on
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
-vector spaces in terms of
Zelevinsky-segments
.

Let
\underline{\alpha}=(n_{1},n_{2},\cdots,n_{s})
be an ordered partition of
n
. We put

{\rm GL}_{\underline{\alpha}}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\rm GL}_{n_{1}}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&{\rm GL}_{n_{2}}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&0\\
0&0&0&{\rm GL}_{n_{s}}\\
\end{array}\right)\subseteq{\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{\alpha}}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\rm GL}_{n_{1}}&\ast&\cdots&\ast\\
0&{\rm GL}_{n_{r}}&\cdots&\ast\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\ast\\
0&0&\cdots&{\rm GL}_{n_{s}}\\
\end{array}\right).\;

For each
1\leq i\leq s
, let
\pi_{i}
be a smooth representation of
{\rm GL}_{n_{i}}(L)
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
. Then
\pi_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\pi_{s}
is naturally a smooth representation of
{\rm GL}_{\underline{\alpha}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
and thus of
{\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{\alpha}}
(where the tensor products are taken over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
). Put

\pi_{1}\times\cdots\times\pi_{s}:={i}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{\alpha}}(L)}^{G}(\delta_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{\alpha}}(L)}^{1/2}\pi_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\pi_{s}),

(6.1)

where
\delta_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{\alpha}}(L)}
is the modulus character of
{\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{\alpha}}(L)
. If all the representations
\pi_{1},\cdots,\pi_{s}
are irreducible cuspidal, then
[
1
, 2.2, 2.3. Corollary]
showed that the Jordan Hölder factors
\mathbf{JH}(\pi_{1}\times\cdots\times\pi_{s})
of
\pi_{1}\times\cdots\times\pi_{s}
are classified by the orientations of some graph. The length of the Jordan Hölder series of
\pi_{1}\times\cdots\times\pi_{s}
is
2^{l}
, where
l
is the number of the pairs of the form
\{\pi,\pi\otimes|\det|_{L}\}\subseteq\{\pi_{1},\cdots,\pi_{s}\}
. Recall that any smooth irreducible representation of
G
is necessarily a sub-representation of some
\pi_{1}\times\cdots\times\pi_{s}
, for some choices of an ordered partition
\underline{\alpha}
and some multiset of irreducible cuspidal representations
\{\pi_{1},\cdots,\pi_{s}\}
. This multiset is called the cuspidal support of
\pi
.

Let
\pi
be a smooth representation of
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
. For
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we put
\pi(l):=\pi\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}}v_{m}^{l}
, where
v_{m}=|\det|_{L}:{\rm GL}_{m}(L)\rightarrow E^{\times}\subset\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
.

Definition 6.1
.

A Zelevinsky-segment is a non-empty set of irreducible cuspidal representations of
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
of the form
\Delta=[\pi,\pi(1),\cdots,\pi(s-1)]
for some integers
m
and
s
. We put
l(\Delta)=ms
.

By
[
1
, 2.10. Proposition]
,
\pi\times\cdots\times\pi(s-1)

admits a unique irreducible subrepresentation
\langle\Delta\rangle
and a unique irreducible quotient
\langle\Delta\rangle^{t}
(in the terminology of
[
1
, 3.1, 9.1]
). By
[
1
]
, every irreducible smooth representation of
G
can be constructed by a sequence of Zelevinsky-segments.

Definition 6.2
.

(Smooth parabolic Steinberg representation)

Since the parabolic subgroups that we use are the opposite of those that appeared in
[
1
]
, the Zelevinsky-segment is arranged in descending order in our content. More precisely, let
s\geq s^{\prime}
be two integers, and let
\pi
be an irreducible cuspidal representation of
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
over
E
. We put the Zelevinsky-segment

\Delta_{[s,s^{\prime}]}(\pi):=[\pi(s),\cdots,\pi(s^{\prime})]=\Delta_{[s-s^{\prime},0]}(\pi(s^{\prime})).

In this case,
\pi(s-1)\times\cdots\times\pi:={i}_{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}_{(m,m,\cdots,m)}(L)}^{G}(\delta_{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}_{(m,m,\cdots,m)}(L)}^{1/2}\pi(s-1)\otimes_{E}\cdots\otimes_{E}\pi)
admits a unique irreducible generic quotient
\langle\Delta_{[s-1,0]}(\pi)\rangle^{t}
. In this paper, we call it the (smooth) parabolic Steinberg representation of
{\rm GL}_{ms}(L)
over
E
(with respect to the Zelevinsky-segment
\Delta_{[s-1,0]}(\pi)
). In the sequel, we may rewrite it as
{\rm St}_{(m,s)}^{\infty}(\pi)
.

6.2
Proof of Proposition
3.6

Proof.

By the transitivity of parabolic induction, we see that
\pi_{I}
is a subrepresentation of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{J}
since
\pi_{I}
is the unique irreducible subrepresentation of
{i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
. This proves (
3.13
).

Proof of (
3.12
).
By definition and
[
4
, 1.5. Proposition (b)]
,
\pi_{I}
is the unique irreducible subrepresentation of

\displaystyle{\displaystyle i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\otimes_{i=1}^{l}{i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)}^{{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)}^{1/2}\Delta_{[k_{i}-1,0]}(\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k-2s_{i-1}-k_{i})+k-s_{i}}),

where we identify
{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)
(resp.,
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)
) with the subgroup
1\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)\times\cdots\times 1
of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\subset G
(resp.,
(1\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)\times\cdots\times 1)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
). This implies that

\displaystyle\pi_{I}=\otimes_{i=1}^{l}\langle\Delta_{[k_{i}-1,0]}(\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k-2s_{i-1}-k_{i})+k-s_{i}})\rangle.

(6.2)

Therefore, by
[
4
, Proposition 1.5 (a)]
, we deduce

\displaystyle{r}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{I}=\otimes_{i=1}^{l}r^{{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)}_{{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}\langle\Delta_{[k_{i}-1,0]}(\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k-2s_{i-1}-k_{i})+k-s_{i}})\rangle,

(6.3)

where we identify
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{J}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)
) with the subgroup
(1\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)\times\cdots\times 1)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{J}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
). Let
\underline{k}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}
be the ordered partition of integer
k
associated with the
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)
. Since
J\subseteq I
, we can write
\underline{k}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}
as
\underline{k}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}=(\underline{k_{1}},\cdots,\underline{k_{l}})
, where
\underline{k_{i}}=(k_{i,1},\cdots,k_{i,l_{i}})
is an ordered partition of integer
k_{i}
for each
1\leq i\leq l
. For
0\leq t\leq l_{i}
, we put
s_{i,0}^{\prime}=0
and
s_{i,t}^{\prime}=\sum_{j=1}^{t}k_{i,j}r
. Similar to (
6.2
), we have the following

\displaystyle\pi_{J}=\otimes_{i=1}^{l}\otimes_{j=1}^{l_{i}}\langle\Delta_{[k_{i,j}-1,0]}(\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k-2(s_{i-1}+s_{i,j-1}^{\prime})-k_{i,j})+k-s_{i-1}-s_{i,j}^{\prime}})\rangle.

(6.4)

By applying
[
1
, 3.4. Proposition]
step by step, we have

\displaystyle r^{{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)}_{{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}\langle\Delta_{[k_{i}-1,0]}(\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k-2s_{i-1}-k_{i})+k-s_{i}})\rangle

(6.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\otimes_{j=1}^{l_{i}}\langle\Delta_{[k_{i}-1-s_{i,j-1}^{\prime},k_{i}-1-s_{i,j}^{\prime}]}(\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k-2s_{i-1}-k_{i})+k-s_{i}})\rangle\otimes_{E}v_{k_{i,j}r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k_{i}-2s_{i,j-1}^{\prime}-k_{i,j})},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\otimes_{j=1}^{l_{i}}\langle\Delta_{[k_{i,j}-1,0]}(\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k-2(s_{i-1}+s_{i,j-1}^{\prime})-k_{i,j})+k-s_{i-1}-s_{i,j}^{\prime}})\rangle.\;

Now (
3.12
) follows by comparing
(
6.3
), (
6.4
) and (
6.5
).

Proof of (
3.14
).
It is clear that
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\cap i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{J}\supseteq i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J}(L)}^{G}\pi_{I\cup J}
. Let
\mathcal{V}
be its cokernel. It suffices to prove that
\mathcal{V}=0
.

Claim.
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\mathcal{V}=0
. Admitting this claim, if
\mathcal{V}
admits at least one nonzero irreducible subquotient
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
, then
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}=0
by the exactness of
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}
. But this would lead to a contradiction to Lemma
6.3
below.

Proof of claim.
Note that

i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\backslash I\cap J}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{I\backslash I\cap J}\cap i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{J}\supseteq i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\cap i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{J}\supseteq i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J}(L)}^{G}\pi_{I\cup J},

by (
3.13
). After replacing
I,J
by
I\backslash I\cap J,J
, we can assume that
I\cap J=\emptyset
. By the transitivity of parabolic inductions, it suffices to prove that

\Big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup J}}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup J}}(L)}\pi_{I}\Big)\cap\left(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup J}}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I\cup J}}(L)}\pi_{J}\right)=\pi_{I\cup J}.

(6.6)

We can suppose further that
I\cup J={\Delta_{n}(k)}
by
[
4
, 1.5. Proposition (b)]
. Since
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}
,
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{J}
and
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I\cup J}(L)}^{G}\pi_{I\cup J}
are subrepresentations of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
, we have

\displaystyle r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}

\displaystyle=\Big(r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\Big)^{\mathrm{ss}}

(6.7)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]}i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\Big(\gamma_{I,\emptyset}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\Big),

\displaystyle r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{J}}

\displaystyle=\Big(r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{J}\Big)^{\mathrm{ss}}

(6.8)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{u\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]}i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}}_{J}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\Big(\gamma_{I,\emptyset}^{u}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)^{u}}\pi_{J}^{u}\Big),

where the first equality in (
6.7
) (resp., (
6.8
)) follows from the semi-simplicity of the smooth representation
{r}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
. By (
3.11
), we see that an irreducible constitute
w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}})
(for some
w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
) appears as an irreducible constitute in
i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}}_{J}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\Big(\gamma_{I,\emptyset}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{u}}\pi_{I}^{w}\Big)
for some
w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}]
if and only if

\displaystyle 0\neq

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\Big(w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}),i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}}_{J}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big(\gamma_{I,\emptyset}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\cong{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\Big(r_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}),\gamma_{I,\emptyset}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}\pi_{I}^{w}\Big).

where the second isomorphism follows from the adjunction formula (
2.5
). Therefore, by the supercuspidality of
w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}})
and Lemma
3.1
, we see that
w\in\mathscr{W}_{I,\emptyset}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
. Then, we deduce from (
3.12
) and the proof of
[
12
, Corollary 6.3.4 (b), i.e., the computation of
\delta
-factors]
that

\displaystyle 0\neq{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\Big(w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}),\gamma_{I,\emptyset}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)^{u}}\pi_{I}^{w}\Big)\cong{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\Big(w^{-1}w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}),r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{I}\Big).

This implies that
w^{\prime}=w
. The same argument holds for
J
. Thus, if
w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}})
appears as an irreducible constitute of
\Big(r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\Big)\cap\Big(r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{J}}\Big)
, then
w^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{I,\emptyset}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cap\mathscr{W}_{J,\emptyset}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})=1
(under the assumption
I\cup J={\Delta_{n}(k)}
). We then conclude that

\Big(r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\Big)\cap\Big(r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{J}}\Big)=\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}=r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{\Delta_{n}(k)}.

The claim follows.

Proof of (
3.15
).
It is clear that
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\cap\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{m}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}_{j}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}_{j}}\Big)\supseteq\sum_{j=1}^{m}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\cap i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}_{j}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}_{j}}
. Let
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
be its cokernel.
Since
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\Big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\cap\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{m}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}_{j}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}_{j}}\Big)\Big)
and
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{m}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}\cap i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}_{j}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}_{j}}\Big)
are subrepresentations of the semi-simple representation
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
,
which shows that all the sums are direct. Therefore, we get that
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}=0
. By Lemma
6.3
, we deduce
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}=0
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be a non-zero irreducible subquotient of
{i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
, then
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\mathcal{V}\neq 0
.

Proof.

If
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\mathcal{V}=0
, we can choose
\emptyset\neq I\subset{\Delta_{n}(k)}
such that
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\mathcal{V}
is non-zero and quasi-cuspidal (i.e., for any
J\subsetneq I
, we have
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}^{G}\mathcal{V}=0
, see also
[
1
, 1.10]
). Let
\mathcal{W}
be an irreducible subquotient of
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\mathcal{V}
. Then
\mathcal{W}
is cuspidal. Note that

(r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}})^{\mathrm{ss}}=\bigoplus_{w\in[\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}]}i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\cap{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\Big(\gamma_{\emptyset,I}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}}\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)^{w}\Big).\;

It follows from the cuspidality of
\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)^{w}
that
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}={\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}
. By Lemma
3.1
, we deduce
w\in\mathscr{W}_{\emptyset,I}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
. Therefore, there exists an element
w\in\mathscr{W}_{\emptyset,I}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
such that
\mathcal{W}
is an irreducible subquotient of
i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\cap{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\Big(\gamma_{\emptyset,I}^{w}\otimes_{E}r^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}}\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)^{w}\Big)
. Therefore, for such
w
we have

\displaystyle 0\neq{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\Big(

\displaystyle\mathcal{W},i_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\cap{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\big(\gamma_{\emptyset,I}^{w}\otimes_{E}\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)^{w}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle={\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}}\Big(r_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)^{w}}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\mathcal{W},\gamma_{\emptyset,I}^{w}\otimes_{E}\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)^{w}\Big).

This leads to a contradiction to the cuspidality of
\mathcal{W}
.
∎

6.3
Jordan-Hölder factors of parabolically induced representations

The main goal of this section is to determine the Jordan-Hölder factors
\mathbf{JH}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}(\pi))
of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}(\pi)
. Recall that the parabolic Steinberg representation
\mathrm{St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi)
is the unique irreducible generic quotient of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}(\pi)
.

Let
\Sigma
be the root system of
{\rm GL}_{n}
, then
\Sigma
is the hyperplane
\{x=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}:x_{1}+\cdots+x_{n}=0\}
. Let us identify the root
\epsilon_{i}:(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in{\mathfrak{t}}\mapsto x_{i}
to the coordinate
x\mapsto x_{i}
for
i=1,\cdots,n
. Then each root
\alpha\in\Phi
induces an
{\mathbb{R}}
-linear form on
\Sigma
. The Weyl group
\mathscr{W}_{n}
act on
\Sigma
by
w\cdot x\mapsto(x_{w^{-1}(1)},\cdots,x_{w^{-1}(n)})
. Let
C=\{x\in\Sigma:\alpha_{i}(x)>0,i=1,\cdots,n-1\}
be the standard Weyl chamber. Let
I^{\prime}
be a subset of
\Delta_{n}
, then for any
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, we have
w(\Phi^{+})\cap\Delta_{n}=I^{\prime}
if and only if
w(C)
is contained in the set

C_{I}=\{x\in V:\alpha_{i}(x)>0,\forall i\in I^{\prime},\alpha_{i}(x)<0,\forall i\in\Delta_{n}\backslash I^{\prime}\}
. Let
w_{\Delta_{n}\backslash I^{\prime}}
be the longest element of
\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{n}\backslash I^{\prime}}
. Then we have
w_{\Delta_{n}\backslash I^{\prime}}^{2}=1
,
w_{\Delta_{n}\backslash I^{\prime}}(\Delta_{n}\backslash I^{\prime})\subset-(\Delta_{n}\backslash I^{\prime})
,
w_{\Delta_{n}\backslash I^{\prime}}(I^{\prime})\subset\Phi^{+}
, and
w_{\Delta_{n}\backslash I^{\prime}}(\Phi^{+})\cap\Delta_{n}=I^{\prime}
.

Recall that
[
1
, Section 2]
gives a bijection between

\mathbf{JH}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi))
and orientations of some graph
\Gamma_{\pi}
. The graph
\Gamma_{\pi}
consisting of the vertices
\{\pi(k-i)\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
, and the edges
\Big\{\overset{\pi(k-i)}{\bullet}-\overset{\pi(k-i-1)}{\bullet}\Big\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
, i.e.,

\textstyle{\overset{\pi(k)}{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overset{\pi(k-1)}{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overset{\pi(0)}{\bullet}}

(6.9)

An orientation of
\Gamma_{\pi}
is given by choosing a direction on each edge. Let
\mathcal{O}(\Gamma_{\pi})
be the set of all orientations on
\Gamma_{\pi}
. We can construct a map
\Theta:\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(\Gamma_{\pi})
as follows. The edge
\overset{\pi(k-i)}{\bullet}-\overset{\pi(k-i-1)}{\bullet}
is oriented by the form
\overset{\pi(k-i)}{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overset{\pi(k-i-1)}{\bullet}
if and only if
w(ir)<w((i+1)r)
, where
ir,(i+1)r\in I
are viewed as simple roots. For a subset
I\subset\Delta_{n}(k)
and each
w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
, we see that
w(\Phi^{+})\cap\Delta_{n}=I\cup\Delta_{n}^{k}
if and only if
w(\Phi^{+})\cap\Delta_{n}(k)=I
, since
w
fixes simple roots in
\Delta_{n}^{k}
. Let
\overrightarrow{\Gamma_{\pi}}(I)
be the orientation of
\Gamma_{\pi}
defined by
\pi(k-i)\rightarrow\pi(k-i-1)
if and only if
ir\in I
. By definition, we see that
\Theta^{-1}(\overrightarrow{\Gamma_{\pi}}(I))=\{w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}):w^{-1}(\Phi^{+})\cap\Delta_{n}(k)=I\}
. By identifying the set
\Delta_{n}(k)
with the simple roots of
\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cong\mathscr{W}_{k}
, we let

w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}
be the longest element of
\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}
(i.e., the subgroup of
\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
generated by simple reflections which belong to
\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I
). By definition, we see that
(w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I})^{2}=1
,
w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}(I)\in\mathscr{W}_{\emptyset,I}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
and
w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}\in\Theta^{-1}(\overrightarrow{\Gamma_{\pi}}(I))
.

For each
w\in\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cong S_{k}
, we let
y_{w}^{\infty}(\pi)
(resp.,
z_{w}^{\infty}(\pi)
) be the unique irreducible quotient (resp., subrepresentation) of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}w(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}})
(see
[
1
, 2.10. Proposition]
), where
w(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}})=(\otimes_{i=1}^{k}\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{w(k-i)})\otimes_{E}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{1/2}
. Let
\Sigma_{0}
be a system of representatives of the classes in
\mathscr{W}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
for the equivalence relation

w\sim w^{\prime}\text{ if and only if }i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}w(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}})\cong i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}).

Then
[
1
, 2.11. Corollary]
show that the following conditions are equivalent:

•

w\sim w^{\prime}
,

•

w^{-1}(\Phi^{+})\cap\Delta_{n}(k)=w^{\prime-1}(\Phi^{+})\cap\Delta_{n}(k)
,

•

z_{w}^{\infty}(\pi)\cong z_{w^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\pi)
,

•

y_{w}^{\infty}(\pi)\cong y_{w^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\pi)
.

Together with the Frobenius reciprocity and the semi-simplicity of
{r}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{i}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}{\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
, the above equivalent conditions imply that

r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}z_{w}^{\infty}(\pi)=\bigoplus_{w\sim w^{\prime}}w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}).

(6.10)

These also show that the system
\Sigma_{0}
of representatives can be chosen by
\{w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}\}_{I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)}
. We put
z_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi):=z_{w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}}^{\infty}(\pi)
. By
[
1
, Section 2]
or
[
6
, Page 628-629]
, we see that
