\mathbf{JH}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi))=\{z_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\}_{I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)}.

The following lemma compares our construction in Definition
3.7
with the Zelevinsky classification (i.e.,
[
1
, Section 2]
) of
\mathbf{JH}(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi))
. The proof follows along the route of
[
26
, Theorem (8.1.2), Lemma (8.1.3) and Lemma (8.1.4)]
.

Lemma 6.4
.

We have

(1)

i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)
has a composition series whose successive quotients are the
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
with
J\supseteq I
, each occurring with multiplicity one.

(2)

u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)
has a composition series whose successive quotients are the
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
with
J\varsupsetneq I
, each occurring with multiplicity one.

(3)

The composition series constructed in parts (1) and (2) are indeed Jordon-Hölder series. In particular, for each
I\subset\Delta_{n}(k)
,
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
is irreducible, and
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{\infty}(\pi)=\mathrm{St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi)
.

(4)

For any
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
,
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
is isomorphic to
z_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
. In particular, for any
I^{\prime},I^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)
, the representations
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I^{\prime}}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
and
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I^{\prime\prime}}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
are isomorphic if and only if
I^{\prime}=I^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

Proof of
(1)
. An easy induction shows that there exists a numbering

I=J_{0},J_{1},\cdots,J_{N}={\Delta_{n}(k)}
of the set
\{J|J\subseteq I\}
such that
J_{n}\subseteq J_{m}
then
n\leq m
. Now define

F^{l}=\sum_{j=l}^{N}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J_{j}}}^{G}(\pi).

Then we have a filtration
0=F^{N+1}\subset F^{N}\subset\cdots\subset F^{0}=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)

such that, for each
l=0,\cdots,N
, we have

\displaystyle F^{l}/F^{l+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=l}^{N}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J_{j}}}^{G}(\pi)\bigg/\sum_{j=l+1}^{N}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J_{j}}}^{G}(\pi)=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J_{l}}}^{G}(\pi)\Big/\sum_{j=l+1}^{N}\bigg(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J_{j}}}^{G}(\pi)\cap i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J_{l}}}^{G}(\pi)\Big)\cong v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J_{l}}}^{\infty}(\pi).

The second equality and the third isomorphism follow from
[A2]
in Proposition
3.6
.

Proof of
(2)
. This is a direct consequence of
(1)
and the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)\longrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)\longrightarrow v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\longrightarrow 0.

Proof of
(3)
. We claim that
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi)
has a Jordon-Hölder series whose successive quotients are
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
with
J\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)
, each occurring multiplicity one. By (
[
1
, 2.3. Corollary]
), a Jordon-Hölder series for
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi)
has length
2^{k-1}
. This shows that the composition series constructed in part (1) is indeed a Jordon-Hölder series. By the uniquely of the irreducible quotient of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi)
, we get
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{\infty}(\pi)=\mathrm{St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi)
.

Proof of
(4)
. The above discussion implies that there exists a bijection
\beta
from
\{I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)\}
onto itself such that
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\cong z_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\beta(I)}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
for any
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)
. In fact, we will prove that
\beta(I)=I
by induction on
|\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I|
. It is clear that
\beta(\Delta_{n}(k))=\Delta_{n}(k)
. Let
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)
with
|\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I|>0
and let us assume that
\beta(J)=J
for any
|\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash J|<|\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I|
. For these
J
, we thus have

r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)=\bigoplus_{w\sim w^{\prime}}w^{\prime}(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}).

(6.11)

by (
6.10
). Note that
w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}\in\mathscr{W}_{\emptyset,I}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
. By the same arguments as in the discussion after (
6.7
) and (
6.10
), we deduce that
w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)
appears in the semi-simplification of
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{{I}}
(up to isomorphism). But by the induction hypothesis, Part (1) and (
6.11
), we see that
w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)
cannot be isomorphic to a subquotient of
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
for
J\varsupsetneq I
. Hence
w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}\big(\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}\big)
is isomorphic to a subquotient of
r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)=r_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}z_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\beta(I)}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
. We thus have
w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash I}\sim w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash\beta(I)}
, i.e.,
\beta(I)=w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash\beta(I)}(\Phi^{+})\cap\Delta_{n}(k)=w^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash(I)}(\Phi^{+})\cap\Delta_{n}(k)=I
. We complete the proof of part
(4)
. By definition of equivalence relation, we see that
z_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I^{\prime}}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
and
z_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I^{\prime\prime}}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
are isomorphic if and only if
I^{\prime}=I^{\prime\prime}
.
Therefore, we get that
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I^{\prime}}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
and
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I^{\prime\prime}}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
are isomorphic if and only if
I^{\prime}=I^{\prime\prime}
. This completes the proof.
∎

6.4
Smooth extensions of generalized parabolic Steinberg representations

This section follows a technical modification of the route of
[
28
]
. In this paper, the author compute the higher
{\rm Ext}
-groups between smooth generalized (Borel) Steinberg representations via some spectral sequences arising from the so-called smooth Tits complex. This method can be adapted to our parabolic case, where the smooth Tits complexes in
[
28
]
are replaced by the results in Section
4.3
. In parabolic case, we meet some cuspidal objects, these cause difficulty in the computations. Therefore, the theory of semi-simple types by Bushnell and Kutzko
[
3
]
is the cornerstone of the computation.

Many results in this section are not new. Indeed, in
[
28
, Corollary 18]
, Sascha Orlik computed the smooth extension groups
{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi),v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\big)
for general
I,J
. The proof uses the Zelevinski classification and the theory of semi-simple types by Bushnell and Kutzko
[
3
]
. Indeed, the semisimple Bushnell-Kutzko types for
{\rm GL}_{n}
, which classify the cuspidal smooth representations of
{\rm GL}_{n}
over
\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
(or complex field
{\mathbb{C}}
), play a central role in its proof. But in Borel case, all these become trivial. In this section, we avoid using this theory for several reasons.

•

We only need
{\rm Ext}^{1}
-groups for some special case (see Proposition
6.9
). We can achieve this by a easy argument of spectral sequences.

•

The semisimple Bushnell-Kutzko types requires the introduction of many concepts, discussions and statements, which are deviated from our central goals and themes.

Therefore, we use instead the modified treatment of Orlik’s approach on Borel case
[
28
]
. The computations (Section
5.4
) on extension groups of locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic generalized (parabolic) Steinberg representations also follows this route, but in the setting of locally analytic representation.

Let
I\subset{\Delta_{n}(k)}
and let
\underline{k}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=(k_{1},\cdots,k_{l_{I}})
be the ordered partition of integer
k
associated with the
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
. Then
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)={\rm GL}_{k_{1}r}(L)\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{k_{l_{I}}r}(L)
. For
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, we put

{\mathcal{S}}_{I}^{i}:={\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I}),\text{and
}{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{I}^{i}:={\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I}).

The cuspidal representation
\pi
cause difficulty in the computation of
{\mathcal{S}}_{I}^{i}
and
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{I}^{i}
. But there are natural morphisms

\displaystyle\kappa_{I}^{i}:\bigwedge^{i}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I})\cong{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(1,1)\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}_{I}^{i},

(6.12)

\displaystyle\text{resp.\;}

\displaystyle\overline{\kappa}_{I}^{i}:\bigwedge^{i}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})\cong{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=1}(1,1)\rightarrow{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{I}^{i}

induced by the Yoneda
i
-extensions (see (
5.3
)).

Lemma 6.5
.

For any
I\subset{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, the maps
\kappa_{I}^{1}
and
\overline{\kappa}_{I}^{1}
are injective. In particular, we have
\dim_{E}{\mathcal{S}}_{I}^{1}\geq\dim_{E}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I})
and
\dim_{E}{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{I}^{1}\geq\dim_{E}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})
.

Proof.

The injection
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)
induces a map
{\mathcal{S}}_{I}^{i}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\omega_{\pi_{I}},\omega_{\pi_{I}})\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(1,1)
(resp.,
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{I}^{i}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\omega_{\pi_{I}},\omega_{\pi_{J}})\cong{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L),Z_{n}=1}(1,1)
), where
\omega_{\pi_{I}}
is the central character of
\pi_{I}
. It is clear that this map gives a section of
\kappa_{I}^{1}
(resp.,
\overline{\kappa}_{I}^{1}
). The results follow.
∎

For any
1\leq j\leq k-1
, denote by
\Delta_{k,j}
the set
{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash\{jr\}
. We establish the following lemma (crucially using the theory of types of Bushnell and Kutzko
[
3
]
).

Lemma 6.6
.

We have

(a)

{\mathcal{S}}_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}}^{i}={\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}},\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}})=\bigwedge^{i}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{G}}_{n}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})
.

(b)

{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}}^{i}={\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G}(\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}},\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}})=\bigwedge^{i}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{G}}_{n})
.

(c)

{\mathcal{S}}_{I}^{i}={\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}(\pi_{I},\pi_{I})=\bigwedge^{i}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I})
.

(d)

{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{i}:={\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}},\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}})=\bigwedge^{i}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})
.

These isomorphisms are induced by the morphisms in (
6.12
) respectively.

Proof.

Part
(b)
is a direct consequence of
[
28
, Corollary 18]
with
I=J={\Delta_{n}(k)}
(by enlarging
E
if necessary). The proof of
[
28
, Corollary 18]
makes use of the theory of types of Bushnell and Kutzko
[
3
]
. By applying
[
28
, Lemma 5]
to our situation, we get a spectral sequence (see
[
28
, Proof of Corollary 2]
for a similar spectral sequence),

\displaystyle E_{2}^{r,s}={\rm Ext}^{r,\infty}_{G/Z_{n}}(

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}_{s}(Z_{n},\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}}\otimes_{E}(\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}})^{-1}),\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}}\otimes_{E}(\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}})^{-1})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s,\infty}_{G}(\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}},\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}}).

Note that
{\mathrm{H}}^{\ast}_{\infty}(Z_{n},E)=\Lambda^{\ast}{\rm Hom}(Z_{n}/^{0}Z_{n},E)\cong\Lambda^{\ast}E
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}_{s}(Z_{n},\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}}\otimes_{E}(\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}})^{-1})

\displaystyle={\mathrm{H}}^{s}_{\infty}(Z_{n},E)^{\vee}\otimes_{E}\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}}\otimes_{E}(\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}})^{-1}

\displaystyle\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}}\otimes_{E}(\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}})^{-1},&\hbox{$s=0,1$;}\\
0,&\hbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Therefore, Lemma
5.3
(c) gives a long exact sequence

\cdots\rightarrow E_{2}^{i,0}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G}(\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}},\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}})\rightarrow E_{2}^{i-1,1}\rightarrow E_{2}^{i+1,0}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{i+1,\infty}_{G}(\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}},\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}})\rightarrow\cdots.

At first, we have
{\rm Ext}^{0,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}},\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}})\cong E_{2}^{0,0}\cong E
. Since
{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G}(\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}},\pi_{{\Delta_{n}(k)}})=0
for all
i\geq 2
, we see that
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}^{2}={\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}^{4}=\cdots
and
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}^{1}={\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}^{3}=\cdots
. By
[
5
, Chapter X, Theorem 2.4]
, we see that
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}^{l}
is zero for all
l
large enough. We deduce that
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{l}=0
for all
l\geq 1
. We prove Part
(a)
. To prove Part (c), we write
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)={\rm GL}_{k_{1}r}(L)\times{\rm GL}_{k_{2}r}(L)\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{k_{l-1}r}(L)\times{\rm GL}_{k_{l}r}(L)
for some ordered partition

\underline{k}_{I}=(k_{1},\cdots,k_{t},\cdots,k_{l})
of
k
. Then by (
6.2
), we have

\displaystyle\pi_{I}=\otimes_{i=1}^{l}\langle\Delta_{i}\rangle,\Delta_{i}:=\Delta_{[k_{i}-1,0]}(\pi\cdot v_{r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k-2s_{i-1}-k_{i})+k-s_{i}})

(6.13)

Then by Part
(b)
and Proposition
2.4
(Künneth formula for smooth extension groups), we have

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{\ast,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\left(\pi_{I},\pi_{I}\right)=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{l}{\rm Ext}^{\ast,\infty}_{{\rm GL}_{k_{i}r}(L)}(\langle\Delta_{i}\rangle,\langle\Delta_{i}\rangle)=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{l}\big(\bigwedge^{\ast}X_{E}^{*}({\rm GL}_{k_{i}r})\big)=\bigwedge^{\ast}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}).

It remains to prove Part
(d)
. Similarly, we have the following spectral sequence,

\displaystyle E_{2}^{r,s}={\rm Ext}^{r,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}(L)/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}}({\mathrm{H}}_{s}(Z_{n},\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}}\otimes_{E}(\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}})^{-1}),

\displaystyle\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}}\otimes_{E}(\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}})^{-1})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r+s,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}(L)}(\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}},\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}}).

Note that
E_{2}^{r,s}
is zero unless
s\in\{0,1\}
and
E_{2}^{r,0}\cong E_{2}^{r,1}\cong{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{r}
for all
r\geq 0
. Therefore, Lemma
5.3
(c) gives a long exact sequence

\cdots\rightarrow E_{2}^{i,0}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}(L)}(\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}},\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}})\rightarrow E_{2}^{i-1,1}\rightarrow E_{2}^{i+1,0}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{i+1,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}(L)}(\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}},\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}})\cdots.

By the vanishing of
{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}(L)}(\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}},\pi_{\Delta_{k,j}})
for all
i>2
, we see that
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{2}={\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{4}=\cdots
and
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{3}={\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{5}=\cdots
. Since
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{l}
is zero for all
l
large enough, we deduce that
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{l}=0
for all
l\geq 2
. Then we get a long exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{0}\rightarrow E\rightarrow E_{2}^{-1,1}\rightarrow{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{1}\rightarrow E\oplus E\rightarrow{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{0}\rightarrow{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{2}\rightarrow E\rightarrow{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{1}\rightarrow{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{3}=0.

This implies
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{0}\cong E
, and
{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}_{\Delta_{k,j}}^{1}\cong E
. By Lemma
6.5
, we get
(d)
.
∎

We are ready to compute the smooth Extensions between smooth generalized parabolic Steinberg representations. The following contents follow along the lines of
[
28
]
. If
W
is a smooth representation, then we let
W^{\sim}
be its smooth dual.

Lemma 6.7
.

We have

{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi)\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{S}}^{i}_{J},&\hbox{$J\subseteq I$;}\\
0,&\hbox{otherwise,}\end{array}\right.

(6.14)

and

{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi)\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}^{i}_{J},&\hbox{$J\subseteq I$;}\\
0,&\hbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(6.15)

Proof.

We prove (
6.15
) at first, and (
6.14
) follows by the same strategy. By Lemma
5.2
, we have for all
r\geq 0
the isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi)\big)={\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi),\pi_{J}\big).

Restrict the proof of Lemma
5.9
to smooth case. Recall the Bruhat filtration
\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}_{B}
on
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi)
(see (
5.7
)) and the vanishing result of (
5.18
). If
J\subsetneq I
, we deduce by dévissage
(see the graded pieces (
5.8
) for all
h\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
) that
{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi)\Big)=0
for all
i\geq 0
. If
J\subseteq I
, we have for all
i\geq 0
the isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi),\pi_{J}\Big)\cong

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(r^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}(L)}(\mathcal{F}_{B}^{0}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}(\pi)),\pi_{J}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big({r}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L)}\pi_{J},\pi_{J}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{i,\infty}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L),\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\Big(\pi_{J},\pi_{J}\Big).

This completes the proof.
∎

The following proposition follows from the same arguments as in the proof of
[
28
, Proposition 17]
, where the acyclic complex in the proof of
[
28
, Proposition 11]
is replaced by our exact sequence (
4.7
) in Proposition
4.3
.

Proposition 6.8
.

We have

{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi)\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{S}}^{i-|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|}_{J},&\hbox{$I\cup J={\Delta_{n}(k)}$;}\\
0,&\hbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(6.16)

and

{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi)\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}^{i-|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|}_{J},&\hbox{$I\cup J={\Delta_{n}(k)}$;}\\
0,&\hbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(6.17)

We now compute the smooth
{\rm Ext}^{i}
-groups of smooth generalized parabolic Steinberg representations.

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
J\subseteq I
be subsets of
\Delta_{n}(k)
such that
|I|=|J|+1
. Assume that
I=J\cup\{\upsilon r\}
for some
1\leq\upsilon\leq k-1
. We have

\displaystyle X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,\upsilon}}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi),v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1,\infty}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi),v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\big).

(6.18)

and
{\rm Ext}^{i,\infty}_{G}(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi),v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\big)=0
for all
i\neq 1
.

Proof.

Using the fact
{\rm Hom}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi),v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\big)=0
(see Lemma
6.4
(4)), we deduce
the second isomorphism of (
6.18
) from
[
9
, Lemma 3.1]
or
[
31
, Lemma 3.2]
. We apply the acyclic complex of Proposition
4.3
, i.e.,

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{J,k-1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{J,k-2}\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{J,k-3}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{J,1}\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{J,0}=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\rightarrow v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\rightarrow 0,

(6.19)

to the representation
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
. By the same arguments as
[
28
, Page 626, proof of Theorem 1]
, we first choose a projective resolution of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi)
(recall that
\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}_{E}(G)
has enough projectives, see the beginning of
[
28
, Section 3]
or Section
2.3
). Then we get a double complex such that its associated spectral sequence converges:

E_{1}^{-s,r}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subseteq K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|K\backslash J|=s\end{subarray}}{\rm Ext}^{r,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi)\big)\Rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{r-s,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi),v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{\infty}(\pi)\big).

(6.20)

By Proposition
6.8
, we see that
S:=J\cup({\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I)=\Delta_{k,\upsilon}
is the minimal subset
S
of
{\Delta_{n}(k)}
containing
J
with
{\rm Ext}^{r,\infty}_{G,\omega_{\pi}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi),i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{K}}}^{G}(\pi)\big)\neq 0
. Hence, the
h
-th row of the
E_{1}
-page of the above spectral sequence is given by

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}^{h-|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|\Delta_{n}(k)\backslash K|=1\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}^{h-|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|}_{K}\longrightarrow\cdots

(6.21)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq K\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}\\
|K\backslash S|=1\end{subarray}}{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}^{h-|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|}_{K}\longrightarrow{\rm\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}^{h-|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|}_{S}\longrightarrow 0.\;

Only the objects in the
-(k-1-|J|)
-th and
-(k-2-|J|)
-th columns of the
E_{1}
-page can be non-zero. By Lemma
6.6
, the
|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+1
-th row is given by

\begin{matrix}&X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})&\longrightarrow&X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,\upsilon}}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})\\
&\parallel&&\parallel\\
&E_{1}^{-(k-1-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+1}&\xrightarrow{d_{1}^{-(k-1-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+1}}&E_{1}^{-(k-2-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+1}\end{matrix}.

For
d\geq 2
, the
|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+d
-th row is given by

\begin{matrix}&\bigwedge^{d}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})&\longrightarrow&\bigwedge^{d}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,\upsilon}}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})\\
&\parallel&&\parallel\\
&E_{1}^{-(k-1-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+d}&\xrightarrow{d_{1}^{-(k-1-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+2}}&E_{1}^{-(k-2-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+d}\end{matrix}.

Note that
X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Delta_{n}(k)}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})=0
and
\bigwedge^{d}X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})
for all
d\geq 2
. We thus have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{-(k-2-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+1}=E_{1}^{-(k-2-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+1}/\mathrm{Im}(d_{1}^{-(k-1-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+1})=X_{E}^{*}({\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,\upsilon}}/{\mathbf{Z}}_{n}),

\displaystyle E_{2}^{-(k-2-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+d}=E_{2}^{-(k-1-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+1}=E_{2}^{-(k-1-|J|),|{\Delta_{n}(k)}\backslash I|+d}=0,

for all
d\geq 2
. The results follow from Lemma
5.3
(b).
∎
